The assumptions ledger¶

Every assumption of the pipeline is an entry of docs/assumptions/assumptions.yml, traced to a published number or method (literature: citation key, page, the quoted text), a decision or measurement of this project (validation), an instrument document (instrument), or a convention with no physical effect. An entry that has neither a citation nor a validation is marked open and listed in the open items; nothing is justified that is not.

tngxmm ledger check verifies that every hashed spec field of the production presets is named in the used_by of at least one entry and that every reference resolves. The proofs (each quoted text boxed on its page of the cited paper) are made by a tool in the private references area, which is not in the repository; tngxmm ledger html builds the review page from the ledger and those proofs, one self-contained HTML file organised by pipeline and node, with links to Kedro-Viz.

Beside the generated list below: Open items of the assumption ledger (what is unjustified or only partly justified) and Sensitivity and convergence tests of the assumption ledger (the measured effect of the choices); the decisions behind the validations are in Decision record.

Entries¶

run.random_streams¶

Kind: convention

The sha256 random streams of every view (core.rng) are keyed by the campaign key sim.rng_campaign (“v4” for the v1 presets and “v4_tng300”, “v4_tng100”, “v4_tng50” for the box presets), the simulation, snapshot, halo, projection, replica, purpose and camera, so that the V4 draws (roll angle, donor, z_obs, photon and instrument noise) are independent of the V3.2 draws. The preset name name is not the key, and seed_offset enters no seed: it is a label recorded with every view (obs_meta, results rows, check C3) and part of the spec and generation hashes.

Value: streams keyed by sim.rng_campaign; name = the preset name (not a seed input); seed_offset = 1_000_000_000 (recorded, not a seed input)

Used by: name, seed_offset

cosmology.planck15¶

Kind: literature

Flat LCDM with the Planck 2015 parameters that IllustrisTNG adopts (H0 = 67.74 km/s/Mpc, Omega_m = 0.3089), for every distance, critical density and R500c(z) of the mocks, the photometry and the fits, so that the simulation and the analysis share one cosmology.

Value: H0 = 67.74 km/s/Mpc, Om0 = 0.3089 (Ode0 = 1 - Om0)

Used by: cosmology.h0_km_s_mpc, cosmology.om0

Evidence: cosmology_planck2015 p. 32 (Table 4, column TT,TE,EE+lowP+lensing+ext); illustristng p. 8 (Sect. 2 (cosmological parameters adopted by IllustrisTNG))

sim.simulation¶

Kind: literature

The mocks are drawn from the 352 primary zoom targets of TNG-Cluster (the production simulation) and, for the maximal campaign and the resolution tests, from the periodic boxes TNG300-1, TNG100-1 and TNG50-1 (every FoF central above the mass cut, no zoom targets).

Value: v1: tng-cluster, primary only; v1_tng300 / v1_tng100 / v1_tng50: box runs, all centrals

Used by: sim.sim_name, sim.primary_only

Evidence: tngcluster p. 3 (Sect. 1 (we simulate 352 high-mass halos)); tngcluster p. 8 (Sect. 3 (the 352 systems that are the primary zoom targets))

sim.paths¶

Kind: convention

Location of the simulation output on the Vera file system.

Value: /virgotng/... (per preset)

Used by: sim.base_path

sim.snapshots¶

Kind: validation

Only TNG full snapshots are used (99, 91, 84, 78, 72, 67, 59, 50, 40; snapshot 59, z = 0.70, bridges the 0.5-1.0 gap). The mini snapshots carry no per-cell GFM_Metals and GFM_CoolingRate, so the gas selection and the metal content would be approximated.

Value: 9 full snapshots, 0.0 < z < 1.5

Used by: sim.snaps

Evidence: nelson2019_tngdr p. 7 (Sect. 3.1 (full and mini snapshots)); nelson2019_tngdr p. 9 (Table 3 (the full snapshots: 99, 91, 84, 78, 72, 67, 59, 50, 40 and others, with a and z))

Validation: Full snapshots only; mini-snapshot fallback; composition-aware truth – the mini-snapshot fallbacks raise lx_intr_r500 by +2.9 +- 0.4 % against the full snapshots of the same halos (node-pilot review)

sim.mass_floor¶

Kind: literature

Halo sample: every cluster with M500c >= 1e14 Msun (physical), the mass range of the reference observations and of the published simulation studies of the hydrostatic bias.

Value: 1e14 Msun

Used by: sim.mass_cut_m500c_msun

Evidence: barnes2021 p. 4 (Sect. 2.1.4 (clusters selected with the mass cut M500 >= 1e14 Msun))

sim.projection_axes¶

Kind: literature

Each halo-snapshot is viewed along the three coordinate axes (z, y, x) of the box.

Value: (0, 1, 2)

Used by: sim.views

Evidence: barnes2021 p. 5 (Sect. 2.2 (synthetic images: projections along the x, y and z axes)); rasia2012 p. 4 (Sect. 3 (simulated clusters, three orthogonal projections))

sim.gas_selection¶

Kind: validation

The emitting gas of the production mocks is NOT the FoF group: the four v1 presets use all the gas of the 4 R500c cube about GroupPos (entry sim.gas_cutout, sim.loader = ‘cutout’). The FoF group of the halo (loadHalo), clipped at sim.sphere_r200c_factor x R200c (2 R200c, about 3 R500c) about GroupPos, is the alternative loader ‘fof’, and the FoF members remain the gas of the temet-exact reference luminosities and maps (sim.lx_reference_gas = ‘fof’). The audit number below is why the production gas is the whole cube: FoF-only gas drops the cool infalling gas outside the group. The precedents below are for the FoF and clipped selections and are kept as the literature of the alternative.

Value: production: loader 'cutout' (the 4 R500c cube, sim.gas_cutout); alternative: loader 'fof', sphere_r200c_factor = 2.0 (FoF members within 2 R200c); gas_radius_r500 and los_depth_r500 unset

Used by: sim.loader, sim.gas_radius_r500, sim.los_depth_r500, sim.sphere_r200c_factor

Evidence: barnes2021 p. 7 (Sect. 2.2.3 (the X-ray spectrum is generated for every gas particle/cell within the FoF group)); barnes2021 p. 7 (Sect. 2.2.3 (the spectra are projected onto a square grid of physical side length 3 r500)); tngcluster p. 6 (Caption of the TNG-Cluster volume visualisation, top right panel (FoF-scope gas of all halos)); rasia2012 p. 6 (Sect. 5.1 (X-MAS Chandra mocks: the emission of the particles within a finite depth along the line of sight, centred on the cluster)); lebrun2014 p. 5 (Sect. 2.2.2 (X-ray observables and analysis: synthetic X-ray observations of simulated groups and clusters))

Validation: Science choices that fix the V4 campaign – FoF-only gas omits cool infall gas: projected T 1-11 % hotter at R500, mock masses higher (audit); the production gas is therefore the whole cube (DECISIONS, ‘Realism, release 1.1’, geometry of the mock gas)

sim.gas_cutout¶

Kind: validation

Production configuration: sim.loader = ‘cutout’ with sim.mock_cutout_shape = ‘box’, sim.mock_box_r500 = 5.66 and sim.mock_cylinder_r500 = 4 (the box paragraph below); sim.gas_radius_r500 and sim.los_depth_r500 unset. The spherical selection that comes first is the alternative mode (mock_cutout_shape unset). With loader ‘cutout’ the mock emits from exactly the gas cells (PartType0) whose snapshot indices the spherical truth stage stored for the halo (truth_cutouts/<sim>/snap_<snap>.h5, every particle within r_cut = max(3 R200c, 1.02 max(3.15 R500c, 2 R200c)) of GroupPos, defined in sim/truth_sphere.py), so the photons come from the same particle set as the hydrostatic truth (the spherical truth, truth.sphere) and carry the projected outskirts that the FoF group (which fills 0.63-0.82 of the R500c shell at z > 0.7 and stops at the group boundary) and the 2 R200c clip drop. The emitting cylinder is bounded by that sphere (radius and half-depth r_cut, or a shallower sim.los_depth_r500). With sim.gas_radius_r500 the gas is clipped to the uniform sphere of that many R500c about GroupPos for every halo of every simulation (a sphere of 4 R500c is the intended production setting), the emitting cylinder has that radius and the half-depth sim.los_depth_r500 (default the radius), and a radius beyond r_cut of a halo (4.2-5.7 R500c) is refused for that halo. 4 R500c is the smallest round multiple of R500c whose cylinder contains the projected 1.5 Mpc aperture (lx_1p5mpc, lx_1mpc) of every halo (smallest R500c 0.41 Mpc, TNG-Cluster, so 1.5/0.41 = 3.66 R500c; cylinder radius 1.64 Mpc for that halo) and lies inside every stored sphere (smallest r_cut 4.24 R500c; 3258 spheres). This is coverage, not convergence: no published value fixes the radius, and the projected-emission convergence test (DECISIONS, ‘Realism, release 1.1’, geometry of the mock gas) is its validation: at a half-side of 4 R500c the emission from beyond the cube is below 0.12 per cent of the 0-1 R500c emission and below 0.62 per cent of the median XMM background in every annulus inside 4 R500c (90th percentile, 20 halos x 3 views). A missing, incomplete or truncated cutout is refused, not replaced by another selection. The gas cells of a TNG-Cluster zoom that are coarse (heavier than 8 x the median gas cell mass of the cutout, found only in cutouts that reach low-resolution dark matter) are KEPT, counted and reported with their mass share and their share of the 0.5-2 keV emission (they emit but are unresolved; author’s decision). This low-resolution gas criterion is inferred from the snapshot data, not taken from a reference. With sim.mock_cutout_shape = ‘box’ the mock gas is instead ALL the gas of the axis-aligned cube of half-side sim.mock_box_r500 (5.66 = 4 sqrt(2), the cube that holds the 4 R500c cylinder along any axis, the size of the canonical cubes) R500c about GroupPos, from the separate files mock_cutouts/<sim>/ (campaign mock-cutout; gas only): the views project along the simulation axes, so the projection is a square of side 8 R500c with a uniform line-of-sight depth of +-4 R500c; no spherical clip and no shorter cylinder (gas_radius_r500 and los_depth_r500 must be unset); half-side 4 R500c >= 1.64 Mpc for the smallest halo keeps the 1.5 Mpc coverage. The hydrostatic truth stays spherical. Per halo the file records the nearest low-resolution dark matter (median 6.1 R500c over the 2463 TNG-Cluster targets; the cube corners reach 6.93 R500c), the coarse-gas census, and the high-resolution coverage of the cube and of each projected square. The emitting volume of the production mock is a cylinder in the cube (sim.mock_gas_region = ‘cylinder’ with sim.mock_cylinder_r500 = 4: radius and line-of-sight half-depth 4 R500c along each view’s axis; without mock_cylinder_r500 the cylinder inscribed in the cube; ‘cube’ = the enclosing cylinder): a circular edge, the same +-4 R500c depth at every projected radius below 4 R500c. The square-and-depth sentences above describe the 4 R500c cube of releases 1.1.x; the emitting gas of the 5.66 cube with the 4 R500c cylinder is the same.

Value: production: loader = 'cutout', mock_cutout_shape = 'box', mock_box_r500 = 5.66, mock_cylinder_r500 = 4.0, mock_gas_region = 'cylinder'; cutout_root, mock_cutout_root, gas_radius_r500 and los_depth_r500 unset (the output tree; no sphere clip); coarse cells kept

Used by: sim.loader, sim.cutout_root, sim.gas_radius_r500, sim.los_depth_r500, sim.mock_cutout_shape, sim.mock_box_r500, sim.mock_gas_region, sim.mock_cutout_root, sim.mock_cylinder_r500

Validation: Realism, release 1.1 – login test, 3 halos (M500c 1.0-1.4e14 Msun): cutout against FoF gas, cells within R500c +0.07 to +0.97 %, photons within R500c +0.8 to +1.3 % (+0.3 to +1.0 % with the FoF cylinder depth); 593 of 2463 target spheres hold low-resolution dark matter inside r_cut (nearest 0.3-5.3 R500c); coarse gas cells (> 8 x the median mass) are in 108 of them, at most 0.3 % of the gas mass, none in 30 control spheres; box cutout, 3 small halos: 1.08-1.17 x the cells and 1.005-1.041 x the luminosity of the 4 R500c sphere, projected luminosity within 2 R500c equal to < 1.3 %; nearest low-resolution dark matter of the 2463 TNG-Cluster targets: median 6.1 R500c, inside the cube corner distance (6.93) for 64 %

Code: src/tngxmm/sim/cutout_gas.py, src/tngxmm/sim/truth_sphere.py, src/tngxmm/sim/mock_cutout.py

sim.gas_periodic_wrap¶

Kind: validation

Every particle read for the mock, the truth and the tngref columns is moved to its minimum image about the halo centre, so a halo near a box face keeps the gas across the face.

Value: periodic_wrap = true

Used by: sim.periodic_wrap

Evidence: barnes2021 p. 3 (Sect. 2.1.1, IllustrisTNG (TNG300 is a periodic cubic volume)); nelson2019_tngdr p. 10 (Sect. 3.1.4, Subboxes (unlike the full boxes the subboxes are not periodic)); pillepich2018 p. 3 (Sect. 2.1.1, Gravity and Hydrodynamics (gravity in the cosmological simulations is solved with periodic boundary conditions))

Validation: Generation decisions after the node pilot – without the wrap the per-pixel closure against the official TNG-Cluster map has a 99th percentile |dlog| of 3.16 and 1.95 dex on two edge halos; with it p99 < 1e-5 dex

sim.gas_cut¶

Kind: literature

Gas cells counted as X-ray emitting: T > 3e5 K, net cooling (GFM_CoolingRate < 0, so cells with net heating do not emit), not star forming (the selection TNG-Cluster uses for its X-ray mock observables). The 0.5-2 keV emission of the excluded hot, net-heating cells is not measured. Mini snapshots have no GFM_CoolingRate and keep every hot non-star-forming cell; production uses full snapshots only.

Value: t_min_k = 3e5 K

Used by: sim.t_min_k

Evidence: chatzigiannakis2025 p. 3 (Sect. 2 (gas selection of the X-ray mocks))

sim.table_metal_unit¶

Kind: validation

The photon normalisation (lum_erg_s, the expected counts per cell) is read from the reference table at log10(Z / u). The table adds the APEC metals at 10^m times their AG89 abundances, so its own unit u is the AG89 metal mass fraction over H..Zn, 0.019366, which the v1 presets use. temet’s unit u = 0.023 (the value before release V4.1) weights the metals x0.84: 1 per cent fewer photons at kT >= 3 keV, 2 per cent at 2 keV and 9 to 13 per cent at 0.5-1 keV for Z = 0.3 Z_sun (0.5-2 keV). The temet-exact reference luminosities keep 0.023, as the official products do. This table sets only the band luminosity of a cell (its photon normalisation); the photon energies and the photon-to-energy ratio come from the per-element spectra in the Lodders (2003) table (sim.plasma_model), so the AG89 pattern enters only where the element ratios of a TNG cell differ from AG89.

Value: table_metal_unit = 0.019366 (v1); None would be the legacy 0.023

Used by: sim.table_metal_unit

Validation: Reference-table metal axis – AG89 metal mass fraction over H..Zn = 0.019366 (soxs angr table); 0.019366 / 0.023 = 0.84; photon-normalisation change +1 % (kT >= 3 keV) to +13 % (0.5 keV) at Z = 0.3 Z_sun

Code: src/tngxmm/sim/load_halo_gas.py, src/tngxmm/sim/compute_apec_spectra.py

sim.tvir_floor_off¶

Kind: validation

The optional core-excised variant (temperature floor tvir_frac x T_vir) is switched off in every V4 preset. The V2 floor max(0.5 T_vir, 5.5 MK) was used while matching the intrinsic luminosities of the TNG-Cluster reference; with temet’s emissivity reproduced exactly it is not needed, and it biases the luminosity low.

Value: tvir_frac = 0 (standard criterion)

Used by: sim.tvir_frac

Validation: – section 6: against temet (sphere r < R500c, 0.5-5 keV) the 0.5 T_vir floor gives 0.958 / 0.904 / 0.933 for three halos (5-12 % too faint); it brought the fourth (17186013) to 1.004 only by deleting the gas temet under-counts

sim.tvir_mu¶

Kind: validation

Mean molecular weight of the virial-temperature estimate T_vir(M500c, R500c). The estimate is read only as the temperature of the count-rate to flux factor (ECF) of the campaign Lx photometry (measure_lx, seed_lx); the optional tvir_frac gas cut that would also read it is off in every V4 preset. 0.588 is the value for a fully ionised gas of hydrogen mass fraction 0.76 (1 / (2 X + 3 Y / 4)); Barnes et al. (2021) adopt 0.59.

Value: 0.588

Used by: constants.mu_tvir

Evidence: barnes2021 p. 9 (Eq. 12 (mu = 0.59, the mean molecular weight Barnes et al. adopt))

Validation: – R2: a 2 % change of mu changes T_vir by 2 % and the 0.5-2 keV ECF by at most 0.6 % (z = 0.9), below 0.1 % at z = 0.05

Code: src/tngxmm/photometry/measure_lx.py

sim.photon_floor¶

Kind: validation

Photons are generated down to 0.2 keV in the rest frame, so that every photon with an observed energy of at least 0.2 keV exists and the PPS band 1 (0.2-0.5 keV) is complete at every redshift. The old 0.3 keV left observed 0.2-0.29 keV empty below z = 0.5.

Value: emin_phot_kev = 0.2 keV

Used by: sim.emin_phot_kev

Evidence: epic_mos p. 1 (Abstract (the current EPIC calibration is better than 10 per cent over 0.2 to 10 keV)); rosen2016 p. 7 (Sect. 3.5, Energy Conversion Factors (energy bands 1 to 5 of the 3XMM pipeline; band 1 is 0.2-0.5 keV)); pyxsim p. 3 (p. 3 text on the Doppler shift and cosmological redshift of the photons (each photon is redshifted by 1/(1+z) before it is received)); barnes2021 p. 7 (Sect. 2.2.3 (the energy range and resolution of the spectral table depend on the desired instrument)); xmm_uhb (Sect. 3.3 (EPIC), https://xmm-tools.cosmos.esa.int/external/xmm_user_support/documentation/uhb/epic.html, first paragraph); pyxsim_docs (source_models/thermal_sources.html (CIESourceModel arguments), https://hea-www.cfa.harvard.edu/~jzuhone/pyxsim/source_models/thermal_sources.html)

Validation: SAS-ready products: photons from 0.2 keV, PPS band 1, SAS headers, SAS detection – at z = 0.05 a third of band 1 was empty with the 0.3 keV floor

sim.photon_ceiling¶

Kind: validation

Photons are generated up to 20 keV in the rest frame (and the ARF and sky-background grids extend to 15 keV observed), so the 4.5-12 keV band is not truncated at high redshift.

Value: emax_phot_kev = 20 keV; arf_energy_max_kev = 15 keV

Used by: sim.emax_phot_kev, instrument.arf_energy_max_kev

Evidence: rosen2016 p. 7 (Sect. 3.5, Energy Conversion Factors (energy bands 1 to 5 of the 3XMM pipeline; band 5 is 4.5-12.0 keV)); pyxsim p. 3 (p. 3 text on the Doppler shift and cosmological redshift of the photons (each photon is redshifted by 1/(1+z) before it is received)); barnes2021 p. 7 (Sect. 2.2.3 (the energy range and resolution of the spectral table depend on the desired instrument)); xmm_uhb (Sect. 3.3 (EPIC), https://xmm-tools.cosmos.esa.int/external/xmm_user_support/documentation/uhb/epic.html, first paragraph); pyxsim_docs (source_models/thermal_sources.html (CIESourceModel arguments), https://hea-www.cfa.harvard.edu/~jzuhone/pyxsim/source_models/thermal_sources.html)

Validation: – bias audit section 5, item 1: EMAX_PHOT_KEV >= 20 keV rest and the instrument ARF box >= 15 keV (the legacy 10 keV truncated the hard band)

sim.photon_sampling_numerics¶

Kind: validation

Numerical settings of the photon generator: 5000 bins of the APEC energy grid and an effective area of 4000 cm^2 for the photon draw.

Value: phot_nbins = 5000; area_cm2 = 4000 cm^2

Used by: sim.phot_nbins, sim.area_cm2

Validation: SENS job results – spectral grid: worst 0.5-2 keV error 0.23% at 5000 bins against 100000 (S1b); event band counts of 10000 and 20000 bins within 0.2% of production (12 views); area 4000 cm^2 is 1.58 x the largest summed ARF peak (2525 cm^2), no photon lost

sim.redshift_clamp¶

Kind: validation

No clamp of the photon-generation redshift: the mock is observed at the redshift of its replica target (the real observation’s redshift with a 3 % jitter), not at max(z, 0.05).

Value: z_eff_min = None; z_obs_source = replica

Used by: sim.z_eff_min, sim.z_obs_source

Evidence: pyxsim p. 3 (p. 3 text before Eq. 5 (the observed redshift and distance may differ from those of the original photon sample)); pyxsim_docs (api/photon_list.html (make_photons arguments redshift and dist), https://hea-www.cfa.harvard.edu/~jzuhone/pyxsim/api/photon_list.html)

Validation: Calibration subset and matched draw on the literature – z_obs comes from the target table (replica / matched draw); gate P compares its distribution with the observed sample

sim.plasma_model¶

Kind: validation

One plasma model on both sides of the fit: the generator uses APEC 3.1.3 spectra, the same solar abundance table (lodd, Lodders 2003) for the emission, the per-element solar fractions and the TBabs absorption as the fitter, with the temperature of a cell interpolated between APEC grid nodes instead of rounded up. The emission is collisional ionisation equilibrium (APEC; no non-equilibrium ionisation), with no thermal or turbulent line broadening, which is below the EPIC resolution. Each cell’s spectrum is a per-element sum (VAPEC-like): H and He, the seven metals TNG tracks (C, N, O, Ne, Mg, Si, Fe, from GFM_Metals in Lodders solar units) each with its own abundance, and the untracked metals scaled with the cell’s Fe. The band luminosity that sets the number of photons of a cell comes from the reference table at the cell’s total metallicity (sim.table_metal_unit).

Value: apec_version 3.1.3; apec_abund_table lodd; t_sampling interpolate; fitter abund_table lodd

Used by: sim.apec_version, sim.apec_abund_table, sim.t_sampling, massfit.abund_table

Evidence: apec p. 1 (Smith et al. 2001, the APEC plasma model); wilms2000_tbabs p. 12 (Appendix (the tbabs model and its interfaces)); lodders2003 p. 10 (Lodders 2003, Table 4: the adopted present-day solar helium, A(He) = 10.899 (the table of the journal PDF, IOP)); heasoft_xspec p. 210 (XSPEC 12.15.0 manual, Sect. 5.8.1 abund: abund lodd sets the Lodders (2003) table, the fitter’s massfit.abund_table); sanders2018 p. 21 (Sanders et al. 2018, Appendix (MBProj2 computes its count-rate conversions with XSPEC and APEC))

Validation: – bias audit section 5 items 2 and 4: different APEC versions / abundance tables on the two sides, and round-up temperature sampling, were sources of a mock-vs-fitter bias

truth.hse_barnes¶

Kind: literature

The intrinsic hydrostatic mass of each simulated cluster follows Barnes et al. (2021): 3-D gas density and mass-weighted temperature in 50 linearly spaced radial shells over 0-1.5 R500, fitted with the Vikhlinin et al. (2006) forms, differentiated analytically, inserted into the hydrostatic equation with the thermal pressure; M500 is where the interpolated profile crosses 500 rho_c. Deviations (own mean molecular weight per cell, no star-forming gas, filling-factor density, spherical selection, innermost crossing beyond 0.4 R500c) are listed as separate entries.

Value: hse_pressure thermal-fit-mu; hse_shell_binning linear:50:0:1.5; t_weighting mass

Used by: sim.hse_pressure, sim.hse_shell_binning, sim.t_weighting, sim.truth_columns, sim.truth_profiles

Evidence: barnes2021 p. 4 (Sect. 2.1.5, Eq. 1); barnes2021 p. 9 (Sect. 2.4, Eq. 12 (and the interpolated r500 crossing)); vikhlinin2006 p. 3 (Eq. 3 (modified beta model with gamma = 3))

truth.own_mu¶

Kind: validation

The truth uses the thermal pressure each gas cell carries (its own mean molecular weight, from its hydrogen and electron fractions) instead of an assumed mu = 0.59. This is a deviation from Barnes et al. (2021), who adopt mu = 0.59; it is recorded in the paper. Its size (SENSITIVITY_TESTS.md, S8; 24 TNG-Cluster halo-snapshots): mu = 0.59 instead changes ln M_HSE(R500c) by -0.0041 in the median (16-84 per cent -0.0044 / -0.0036).

Value: mu per cell = 4 / (1 + 3 X_H + 4 X_H x_e)

Used by: sim.hse_pressure

Evidence: barnes2021 p. 9 (Eq. 12 (mu = 0.59, the value we depart from)); ettori2019 p. 3 (Sect. 2, Eq. 1 (mu from the mass fractions X, Y, Z of the ionised plasma)); ansarifard2020 p. 10 (Sect. 4.3.5 (mu = 0.59 as in Barnes et al.))

Validation: Headline intrinsic hydrostatic mass: Barnes et al. 2021 with fitted slopes – composition-aware column barnes21_nosf_fit_mu equals the pressure-based thermal HSE mass with fitted slopes; TNG gas mu = 0.592

truth.star_forming_gas¶

Kind: literature

Star-forming cells are excluded from the truth profiles: their temperature is the effective equation-of-state value and is assigned 1000 K for observables in TNG-Cluster. This is our deviation from Barnes et al. (2021), who cut star-forming gas only in the X-ray images. Its size on its own (SENSITIVITY_TESTS.md, S8; 24 TNG-Cluster halo-snapshots): keeping the star-forming cells changes ln M_HSE(R500c) by -0.0003 in the median (16-84 per cent -0.0024 / -0.0001, at most -0.045 for one halo).

Value: hse_gas_phase_cut = nonsf

Used by: sim.hse_gas_phase_cut

Evidence: tngcluster p. 7 (Sect. 2.4 (star-forming gas is assigned its cold phase temperature of 1000 K)); barnes2021 p. 7 (Sect. 2.2.3 (cuts applied to the X-ray images, not to the 3-D profiles)); biffi2016 p. 3 (Sect. 3.1 (cold and multiphase gas particles removed from the hydrostatic computation))

truth.sphere¶

Kind: validation

The hydrostatic truth is computed from every particle in a sphere about the halo centre (the region that defines the catalogue M500c), not from the FoF members, which fill only part of the R500c shell at high redshift.

Value: truth_selection_hse sphere; truth_sphere_r200c = 3.0 R200c; truth_selection fof (profiles, M500 at z_obs)

Used by: sim.truth_selection_hse, sim.truth_sphere_r200c, sim.truth_selection

Evidence: barnes2021 p. 4 (Sect. 2.1.5 (3-D profiles from radial bins about the cluster centre)); biffi2016 p. 3 (Sect. 3 and 3.1 (spherical shells, no substructure removal))

Validation: Spherical hydrostatic truth – z > 0.7: M_HSE(<R500c)/M500c 0.807 [0.74, 1.03] (sphere) against 0.731 [0.50, 0.88] (FoF); FoF fills 0.63-0.82 of the R500c shell

truth.shell_filling¶

Kind: validation

The shell gas density of the truth is the gas mass over the summed cell volume (the density of the gas present), and shells whose filling factor is below 0.1 are omitted from the HSE profiles.

Value: hse_shell_density filling; hse_min_filling 0.1

Used by: sim.hse_shell_density, sim.hse_min_filling

Evidence: gouin2023 p. 3 (Sect. 2.3, Eq. 1 (radial profiles of IllustrisTNG cluster gas averaged over the cells of a shell, weights = cell volumes)); gouin2023 p. 3 (Sect. 2.3, Eq. 2 (the volume covering fraction f_k = summed cell volume / volume of the spherical shell)); galarraga2021 p. 7 (Sect. 4.1, Eqs. 6-7 (profiles of TNG300 gas weighted by cell volume, sum of the cell volumes of the shell in the denominator))

Validation: Node-pilot-2b fixes: filling-consistent HSE density – the non-SF zoom gas fills ~1 everywhere; FoF non-SF gas fills >= 0.63 at R500c in all 22 validation halos; 0.1 only removes filament-dominated shells far outside R500c

truth.fill_check¶

Kind: literature

Campaign check C17 judges the non-SF filling factor of the spherical truth at R500c (summed cell volume m / rho over the shell volume) against 0.9-1.1 only for simulations whose cells tile space: the Voronoi cells of the moving-mesh runs (TNG). In the SPH and meshless runs (FLAMINGO and EAGLE: SPH; SIMBA: GIZMO meshless finite mass) m / rho is a kernel volume, rho being a kernel estimate at the particle that includes the particle’s own contribution. That estimate is biased whatever the kernel (Dehnen & Aly 2012: the Wendland kernels of SWIFT over-estimate the density, the cubic spline under-estimates it), so the volumes of a filled shell need not add up to its volume, in either direction. There the fill is reported, not judged (FLAMINGO L1_m10: median 0.95, 0.84-1.05 over 100 clusters); the completeness of a sphere is checked by the chunk scan. No window is set for SPH: one fitted to the measured spread would be a tuned number.

Value: fill window 0.9-1.1 for moving-mesh runs; reported only for SPH and meshless runs

Used by: sim.truth_selection_hse

Evidence: pillepich2018 p. 3 (Sect. 2.1 (AREPO: a finite volume method on an unstructured, moving Voronoi tessellation of the domain)); dehnen2012 p. 5 (Sect. 2.5, Fig. 3 (the cubic spline under-estimates and the Wendland kernels over-estimate the SPH density; at a particle position the self contribution gives an over-estimate))

Code: src/tngxmm/core/campaign_checks.py, src/tngxmm/ingest/descriptor.py

truth.tngref¶

Kind: validation

The intrinsic X-ray luminosity and surface-brightness maps in the TNG official convention are computed with temet’s own table and per-cell treatment on the halo’s FoF gas, in the bands 0.5-5.0 and 0.5-2.0 keV on the 2000-pixel official grid, so that our intrinsic Lx is like for like with the public TNG-Cluster maps.

Value: lx_reference temet-exact; lx_reference_gas fof; tngref_map on; bands 0.5-5.0, 0.5-2.0; 2000 px

Used by: sim.lx_reference, sim.lx_reference_gas, sim.tngref_map, sim.tngref_map_bands, sim.tngref_map_npix

Evidence: tngcluster p. 5 (Sect. 2.4 (X-ray emission from the APEC/AtomDB tables, emissivity of each gas cell from its density, temperature and metallicity)); tngcluster p. 14 (Text on Fig. 10, bottom panel (the soft-band luminosity is measured within R500c in 3D))

Validation: Science choices that fix the V4 campaign – temet-exact equals the official TNG-Cluster maps to 1e-5 in L(<R500c) and 4e-6 dex (pixel p99) on 6130348 @ 99 and 2194356 @ 50; FoF maps reach 0.986-0.9996 of the official L(<R500c)

donors.replica_design¶

Kind: validation

Each reference observation (638 in the bank) is replicated by K = 5 simulated halos, each halo used in at most 3 views per halo-snapshot (one per axis), with the donor observation fixing the instrument conditions. The mock is observed at the redshift of its reference observation.

Value: reference_sample bank638; halos_per_observation 5; halo_reuse_cap 3

Used by: donors.reference_sample, donors.halos_per_observation, donors.halo_reuse_cap

Evidence: gianfagna2021 p. 5 (Sect. 4.2 (a twin sample selected from the simulation to reproduce the clusters of a real observing program)); seppi2022 p. 5 (p. 5 (the simulation uses the real attitude file and good time intervals of the survey, a digital twin of the real observation))

Validation: Science choices that fix the V4 campaign – K = 5 gives 3,040 views (~16 h); 24 observations stay unmatched by mass (mostly ACT at z > 1.15); on the full-snapshot lists gate P of the K = 5 replica FAILS (mass D 0.241) and the K’ = 5 matched draws pass (section Full snapshots only)

Code: src/tngxmm/donors/build_replica_targets.py

samples.archive_selection¶

Kind: literature

The real pool is selected twice: by the SZ or lensing catalogue, and by the existence of a usable XMM-Newton archive observation. The second step is not modelled: proposals are not random, and an archive of pointed observations can carry part of an X-ray selection, which favours cool cores at fixed mass. The mocks are paired with the real clusters on fit mass and redshift, so the mass and redshift part of the selection is matched by construction; a selection at fixed mass on core state or X-ray brightness is not. Stated in the paper as a limitation, with a bound written before the comparison (decision of 2026-11-04): for clusters at z < 0.35 the mock-against-real comparison is also reported separately for cool-core and non-cool-core clusters, classified on both sides by the same photometry with the concentration c = F(< 40 kpc) / F(< 400 kpc) and the threshold 0.075 of Santos et al. (2008) as used by Rossetti et al. (2017), with the cool-core fractions of the real pool and of the paired mocks. If the two classes give the same offset, the archive selection on core state does not drive it. At z >= 0.35 the 40 kpc core is not resolved by XMM-Newton (Rossetti et al. exclude those clusters), so c is reported there as a same-photometry label only, not as a cool-core class.

Value: not modelled; reported split at c(40 / 400 kpc) = 0.075 on both sides for z < 0.35; a label only above

Used by: donors.reference_sample

Evidence: rossetti2017 p. 1 (Abstract (X-ray selected samples are biased towards cool cores; cool-core fraction 29 per cent in the Planck sample against 59 per cent in MACS)); rossetti2017 p. 5 (Sect. 4.1, Fig. 2 (the threshold c = 0.075 separates cool cores from non-cool cores)); rossetti2017 p. 3 (Sect. 2.1 (four clusters at z > 0.35 are left out because the core region of the indicator is not resolved with XMM-Newton))

donors.matching_mass¶

Kind: literature

A simulated halo is matched to a real cluster on the TRUE M500c expected for it: the catalogue mass divided by the (1-b) of the survey that produced it. Planck PSZ2 MSZ is a hydrostatic- calibrated mass, corrected with the Planck 2015 XXIV baseline (CCCP) 1-b = 0.78; ACT DR6 M500c is already calibrated to weak lensing (A10/WL = 0.65 is built in), and the lensing masses (CCCP, WtG) are not corrected. This is our own per-cluster use of a sample-level prior (the largest referee risk of the matching, OPEN_ITEMS.md section B). The author reversed this on 2026-10-27: mocks are to be matched to real clusters on the hydrostatic fit mass estimated the same way on both sides (C1, not yet implemented). Until then every v1 realism verdict is conditional on (1-b) = 0.78 for PSZ2: the frozen realism evaluation is not count-thinned, so the luminosity of the matched mocks, and the C2ST, follow the matching mass directly.

Value: matching_mass literature_one_minus_b; (1-b): PSZ2 0.78, ACT DR6 1, lensing 1

Used by: donors.matching_mass

Evidence: planck2015_xxiv p. 7 (Table 2 (mass-bias priors) and Sect. 4.3 (CCCP adopted as baseline)); psz2 p. 13 (Sect. 5.3 (MSZ as the hydrostatic mass expected for the assumed scaling relation)); act_dr6 p. 7 (Sect. 2.5 (A10/WL mass ratio 1-b = 0.65 +- 0.05)); hoekstra2015 p. 23 (Sect. 5 (1-b against the CCCP aperture masses)); hoekstra2015 p. 23 (Sect. 5, Fig. 21 (1-b = 0.69 for the Planck clusters below SNR 7; the bias depends on mass)); act_dr6 p. 19 (App. A, Table A3 (A10_M500cUncorr is the PSZ2-comparable column; M500c is WL-normalised and Eddington-corrected, p. 7)); act_dr6 p. 7 (Sect. 2.5, eq. 5 (Tinker HMF prior, sigma_int = 0.2))

Validation: Calibration subset and matched draw on the literature – with 0.78 gate P fails for the K = 5 replica and passes for the K’ draws; alternatives WtG 0.688, CMB lensing 0.99, 2013 baseline 0.8, primary CMB 0.58 are in PSZ2_ALTERNATIVES

donors.replica_lx_at_fixed_mass¶

Kind: literature

A replica halo matched to a real cluster on mass is NOT expected to match its soft-band luminosity: TNG-Cluster is brighter in L_X(0.5-2 keV) at fixed true M500c than observed SZ-selected and lensing-calibrated clusters. At M500c = 5e14 Msun, z = 0.2 the mocks’ measured L(<R500c) is 10^44.63 erg/s against 10^44.31 (Bulbul+19, SPT), 10^44.26-44.44 (Lovisari+20, hydrostatic masses with b = 0.2 or 0) and 10^44.30-44.46 (REXCESS, Malmquist-corrected, b = 0.2 or 0): +0.2 to +0.4 dex. Placed at the lensing masses of the same clusters (63 bank clusters), the observed lx_1mpc lies 0.26 +- 0.05 dex below the TNG-Cluster relation of the mocks. This is a property of the simulation, not of the photometry: the mocks’ lx_1mpc agrees with the TNG official luminosities (+0.008 dex) and the observed lx_1mpc with MCXC (-0.04 dex after the APEC band ratio). The lensing-mass reference clusters (CCCP, WtG) are X-ray-selected cool-core-rich systems and sit ~0.2 dex brighter at fixed mass, which is why the weak-lensing class shows no offset.

Value: expected mock - real log L E(z)^-2 at matched true mass: +0.2 to +0.4 dex (SZ-selected); ~0 for the X-ray-selected WL class

Used by: donors.matching_mass, donors.halo_reuse_cap

Evidence: pop2022 p. 11 (Sect. 4.2 (TNG300 L_X-M; hydrostatic-mass bias of the observations raises the offset)); pop2022 p. 10 (Sect. 4.2 (L_X 0.5-2 keV within R500c against observed samples)); tngcluster p. 14 (Sect. 4.2.3, Fig. 10 (face-value comparison, no hydrostatic correction of the observed masses)); barnes2018 p. 8 (Sect. 4.2, Fig. 2 (TNG300 gas fraction within r500 ~10 per cent above observed)); bulbul2019 p. 22 (Eq. 26 (core-included 0.5-2 keV L_X-M500-z of 59 SPT clusters, Mpiv 6.35e14, zpiv 0.45, h = 0.6774)); lovisari2020 p. 8 (Table 2, L_X-M_tot (0.1-2.4 keV, < R500, hydrostatic masses; form in Table 1 and eq. 2, p. 4)); lovisari2020 p. 1 (Abstract (relaxed clusters brighter at fixed mass; the selection sets the normalisation)); pratt2009 p. 18 (Table B.2, L[0.5-2] (< R500) - M_Y, Malmquist-bias corrected); chiu2022 p. 22 (Eq. 67 (eFEDS 0.5-2 keV L_X-M500 with HSC weak-lensing masses)); chiu2022 p. 24 (Sect. 6.2 (no difference between eROSITA- and SZ-selected L_X-M-z)); andradesantos2017 p. 1 (Abstract (cool-core fraction, X-ray versus SZ selected))

Code: src/tngxmm/donors/build_replica_targets.py

donors.donor_jitter¶

Kind: validation

The donor’s exposure times (0.15 log-normal common to the cameras, 0.05 per camera) and N_H (0.10) are jittered, and z_obs by 3 % relative, with the unjittered donor values stored, so that an ML model cannot learn the exposure-to-donor-to-mass fingerprint.

Value: jitter_scale_sigma 0.15; jitter_cam_sigma 0.05; jitter_nh_sigma 0.10 (ln); z 3 % relative

Used by: donors.jitter_scale_sigma, donors.jitter_cam_sigma, donors.jitter_nh_sigma

Validation: – design decisions D10 / D11: the KS cost of the jitter is small (D 0.013 -> 0.023)

donors.nh_fallback¶

Kind: convention

Column density used when a donor has no N_H value.

Value: 0.03e22 cm^-2 (donor-table median)

Used by: donors.nh_fallback_1e22cm2

Evidence: hi4pi p. 5 (Sect. 3.1 (the HI4PI N_HI map covers the full sky))

donors.aperture_and_fov_legacy¶

Kind: convention

Legacy V3 constants of the redshift prior: the usable fit field of view (13.5’) and the largest Lx aperture (1500 kpc).

Value: fov_fit_arcmin 13.5; aperture_max_kpc 1500

Used by: donors.fov_fit_arcmin, donors.aperture_max_kpc

donors.excluded_observations¶

Kind: validation

Three reference observations are left out of the replica because their donor core offset in the mask frame is implausible (more than 6 arcmin): 0747420101 (57’), 0161560101 (17.8’), 0030140101 (7.1’), measured at the guarded centres of the real fits (the plain peaks of the table before 2026-11-03 gave 57’, 17’ and 6.9’).

Value: exclude_obsids = (0747420101, 0161560101, 0030140101)

Used by: donors.exclude_obsids, donors.build_core_offsets, params:donors.core_offsets.max_offset_arcmin

Validation: Node-pilot-2b fixes – 0747420101 is an excluded donor with a 57’ core offset (usability limit max_offset_arcmin = 6 in params:donors.core_offsets)

Code: src/tngxmm/donors/build_core_offsets.py

instrument.donor_conditions¶

Kind: validation

Each mock inherits from its real donor observation the filter of every camera, the pn submode (hence the out-of-time fraction), the readout window and the active CCDs of every camera. No published mock-observation study does this (they use a fixed exposure and a nominal background); the justification is that the instrumental conditions of the mock sample then follow those of the real sample.

Value: filter per-donor; pn_submode_header per-donor; readout_windows donor; active_ccds donor

Used by: instrument.filter, instrument.pn_submode_header, instrument.readout_windows, instrument.active_ccds

Evidence: webb2020 p. 3 (Table 1 (the 11204 XMM-Newton observations of 4XMM-DR9 by camera mode and filter)); epic_mos p. 4 (Sect. 5 (window mode applies to the central CCD, so the active area depends on the mode of the observation))

Validation: Readout windows – events and exposure maps share one footprint; donor modes in the v4 targets: PN LW 5, SW 15; MOS1 W2 10, W3 5; MOS2 W2 5, W3 5, FastUncompressed 5

instrument.readout_window_geometry¶

Kind: instrument

Window geometry per readout mode from the XMM-Newton Users Handbook (pn Large Window 198 x 384 px, Small Window 63 x 64 px; MOS central CCD 300 x 300 and 100 x 100 px) and the CCF mode parameter tables. The pn Small Window boundary runs through the centres of the dead gap lanes either side of CCD 4 (65.5 columns); the detector mask removes the lanes, so the active window measures 63 columns x 64 rows, the Handbook’s 63 x 64 (tests/unit/test_instrument_windows.py::test_pn_small_window_on_ccd4).

Value: PN LW 99 rows x 12 CCDs; PN SW 64 rows x CCD 4 (active 63 x 64 px); MOS W2 100 x 100 px; W3 300 x 300 px

Used by: instrument.readout_windows

Evidence: epic_mos p. 4 (Sect. 5 (MOS readout modes: large window 300 x 300 pixels, small window 100 x 100, applied to the central CCD only)); webb2020 p. 3 (Table 2 (pn data modes: the Prime Large Window is half the height of the full window)); webb2020 p. 3 (Table 2 (MOS data modes: PPW2 small central window, PPW3 large central window)); xmm_uhb (Sect. 3.3.2, Table 3 (basic numbers for the science modes of EPIC), https://xmm-tools.cosmos.esa.int/external/xmm_user_support/documentation/uhb/epicmode.html)

Instrument document: XMM-Newton Users Handbook section 3.3.2, Table 3 (EPIC modes); CCF EPN_MODEPARAM_0003, EMOS1_MODEPARAM_0006

instrument.mos_ccd_loss¶

Kind: instrument

The active CCDs of a donor follow its own PPS exposure map; the epoch rule if it has none uses the MOS1 CCD6 loss (9 March 2005, revolution 961) and CCD3 loss (11 December 2012, revolution 2382).

Value: active_ccds donor

Used by: instrument.active_ccds

Evidence: snowden2008 p. 3 (Sect. 2 (13 of the 14 MOS CCDs work; CCD #6 of MOS1 was lost to a micrometeorite hit on 2005 March 9)); snowden2008 p. 4 (Sect. 3.1 (the outer-CCD background history shows the loss of MOS1 CCD #6)); xmm_uhb (Sect. 3.3 (EPIC), third and fourth bullets on the micro-meteoroid events, https://xmm-tools.cosmos.esa.int/external/xmm_user_support/documentation/uhb/epic.html); xmm_mos1_ccd3 (ESA news page ‘EPIC MOS1 Event of Rev. 2382’ (last updated 30-Apr-2013), https://www.cosmos.esa.int/web/xmm-newton/mos1-ccd3); xmm_cal_tn0018 (Sect. 10.1 (Micrometeoroid Impacts), p. 25 of Issue 3.14 (18 February 2025)); pierre2016 p. 7 (Sect. 3.2 (X-ray processing, ‘Specific detector problems encountered’)); tholken2018 p. 2 (Sect. 2.1 (XMM-Newton data reduction of a cluster analysis))

Instrument document: XMM-Newton Users Handbook, EPIC section (MOS1 CCD6 micrometeoroid event, CCD3 declared not usable)

instrument.detector_footprint¶

Kind: instrument

Under instrument.detector_footprint = ‘donor-pps’ (the InstrumentSpec default, the production value; the stage’s own InstrumentOptions default is ‘mask’, the packaged 0/1 detector mask) each camera images the good-silicon fraction of its donor: the donor’s PPS exposure map (band 0.5-1 keV, all exposures of the camera) moved into the detector frame through the donor pointing and roll, divided by the stage’s vignetting at the band centre and by the clean on-chip level, clipped to [0, 1]. It multiplies the exposure maps and thins every event at its detector pixel. The PPS map is the output of SAS eexpmap, which excludes the observation’s own bad pixels and accumulates the instrument map over the attitude histogram, so the donor’s bad columns, CCDs, windows and chip gaps, smeared by its attitude track, enter the mock. The packaged mask instead is one 2020 observation’s map thresholded on 2.5 arcsec pixels: that observation’s bad columns at full depth in every view, MOS central-CCD gaps fully dead and wider than the donors’ (stacked half-depth widths 15-20 arcsec against 6-14, minimum 0.00-0.02 against 0.05-0.08 of the clean level; the physical gap is 300 micron, about 8 arcsec), 5-9 % less exposed silicon per camera than the donor for every one of 195 pairs. Regime: per camera of a donor that has a PPS exposure map of that camera in the 0.5-1 keV band; the division by the stage’s vignetting at the band centre is exact only where the stage’s vignetting equals the one SAS used (it does not at large off-axis angles for MOS, see instrument.vignetting_axis_rga); the pn beyond the 15 arcmin vignetting grid is handled by instrument.vignetting_beyond.

Value: detector_footprint donor-pps (production: the donor's PPS EXPMAP2000 as the efficiency map) | mask (the unconditioned stage: the packaged 0/1 mask)

Used by: instrument.detector_footprint, instrument.replay_views, sim.generate_views

Evidence: xmm_sas_eexpmap (eexpmap Description page (node3), https://xmm-tools.cosmos.esa.int/external/sas/current/doc/eexpmap/node3.html); xmm_sas_eexpmap (eexpmap Parameters page (node5), parameter attrebin, https://xmm-tools.cosmos.esa.int/external/sas/current/doc/eexpmap/node5.html); epic_mos p. 4 (Sect. 4 (the 300 micron gaps between the MOS CCDs; the gaps around the central CCD of MOS1 and MOS2 coincide)); rosen2016 p. 6 (Sect. 3 (astrometric correction: the attitude solution leaves frame shifts of about 1 arcsec, up to 10 arcsec)); rosen2016 p. 9 (Sect. 4.2 (attitude drift at the start or end of an exposure; images from the attitude GTI within 3 arcmin of the nominal pointing))

Validation: – 195 donors, one count-matched replica each, same thinned photons: before, the replica’s exposed silicon was 0.948 / 0.953 / 0.915 of the donor’s (MOS1 / MOS2 / pn, median); after, the per-camera fov fraction differs from the donor’s by -0.002 / -0.006 / -0.022 (median, 80 pairs; the pn residual lies beyond 15 arcmin); the merged fov_frac paired median goes from -0.0204 to -0.0138, its C2ST AUC (logistic / boosting) from 0.745 / 0.975 to 0.734 / 0.971 and all 17 features from 0.985 / 0.998 to 0.983 / 0.999. The footprint is not what separates the populations: masking the union of real and mock low-exposure regions identically on both leaves 0.981 / 0.997 (fov_frac alone 0.686 / 0.950); the remaining fov_frac deficit is the pn beyond the stage’s 15 arcmin vignetting grid and the off-axis vignetting (MOS2 PPS 10 % higher at 13-14 arcmin).

Instrument document: SAS eexpmap documentation (Description and Parameters pages, eexpmap-4.13): bad pixels of the event file excluded, instrument map accumulated over the rebinned attitude histogram, attrebin 4 arcsec; the donors’ PPS EXPMAP products (eexpmap-4.12.1, SAS 21.0)

Code: src/tngxmm/instrument/donor_footprint.py, src/tngxmm/instrument/simulate_instrument.py, src/tngxmm/instrument/make_exposure_maps.py

instrument.vignetting_beyond¶

Kind: instrument

With instrument.vignetting_beyond = ‘ccf-edge’ (the InstrumentSpec default, the production value; the stage’s own InstrumentOptions default is ‘zero’) the detector beyond 15’ off axis gets the effective area at the last angle of the CCF vignetting table (XRTn_XAREAEF VIGNETTING, 27 angles, D_THETA 0.01 deg, last angle 15.6’), held constant to the end of the grid, instead of none, as the PPS exposure maps of the donors do. Regime: only the detector area beyond 15 arcmin (mainly the pn, whose footprint reaches beyond it); the value there is an extrapolation of the table, not a calibration. The effect of the extent alone was not isolated: with the axis re-centring and without the RGA term (donor-measured values at the time of the measurement) the merged fov_frac paired median moves from -0.0139 to -0.0082 and the all-feature C2ST AUC (logistic / boosting) from 0.820 / 0.971 to 0.789 / 0.954 (190 donors, combined variant of 3024ecf, an older configuration; the final configuration is re-measured by the frozen evaluation).

Value: vignetting_beyond ccf-edge (production: last tabulated angle 15.6 arcmin held constant) | zero (stage default)

Used by: instrument.vignetting_beyond, instrument.replay_views, sim.generate_views

Evidence: xmm_cal_tn0018 (Sect. 4.5 (Vignetting), pp. 13-14 of Issue 3.14 (18 February 2025))

Instrument document: CCF XRTn_XAREAEF VIGNETTING table (27 angles, D_THETA 0.01 deg, to 15.6’), as carried by the donors’ PPS EXPMAPs (SAS 21.0)

Code: src/tngxmm/instrument/load_responses.py

instrument.vignetting_axis_rga¶

Kind: instrument (OPEN: no citation and no validation yet)

The stage’s vignetting centre comes from the XMM calibration files (production value; the stage’s own InstrumentOptions default ‘pointing’ leaves it on the pointing). instrument.vig_axis = ‘ccf’ centres each camera’s vignetting (theta map, camera partition, AGN, EXPMAPs, OPTAXX) on the telescope axis of the calibration file XMM_MISCDATA (module instrument.calibration_axis) instead of on the pointing direction; the detector footprint is not moved. The reflection grating assemblies modulate the MOS vignetting in azimuth. XRTn_XAREAEF holds energy x off-axis angle only, and the Calibration Access and Data Handbook says that this table leaves the RGA out: SAS adds it through CAL_getRGAObscure from the RGA_OBSCURATE table of RGSn_QUANTUMEF. With instrument.mos_rga_vignetting = ‘ccf’ (the production value since 2026-11-03) the stage applies that calculation (module instrument.rga_obscuration): obscured fraction eta’ = eta alpha / alpha0, alpha = alpha0 - (F / L) theta cos(phi), so the MOS throughput relative to on axis is 1 + a(E) theta cos(phi - phi0) with a(E) = eta / (1 - eta) F / (L alpha0); eta(E) from RGA_OBSCURATE, alpha0 = G_ALPHA and L = CC2_TX - G_TX of RGSn_LINCOORD, F = FOCAL_LENGTH of XMM_MISCDATA, giving a = 0.0142 per arcmin from 0.5 to 3 keV (0.0153 at 7.5 keV). phi0 is the sky direction of the RGS dispersion axis (spacecraft -Z) in the mask frame, 90 deg + PA_REF = -155.8 deg. The factor multiplies the cluster partition, the AGN, the sky background and the EXPMAP vignetting (and so the donor footprint, which divides the donor’s map by that vignetting). Until release 1.1.3 the switch value ‘pps-measured’ applied a dipole fitted on the donors’ PPS exposure maps; it was removed at the integration of 2026-10-30 (no measured factors), and its removal is what lowered fov_frac from 0.288 to 0.281 (real 0.289; AUC 0.52 -> 0.69, docs/dev/DECISIONS.md, realism-bkg). Why: the same two effects were first measured on the donors’ own PPS exposure maps (centres (-0.54’, -1.68’) MOS1, (-0.45’, -0.61’) MOS2, (-1.38’, -1.63’) pn from the pointing in the mask frame; MOS dipole 1 + a r cos(phi - phi0), a = 0.0125 and 0.0128 per arcmin, phi0 = -157 deg; pn a < 0.0004), which makes the realism test circular, so the independent audit asked for the calibration-file values; those measured numbers are only the reference the calibration values are compared with. Regime: the vignetting is calibrated well to about 10 arcmin off axis (TN-0018 Sect. 4.5) and the RGA modulation is energy and off-axis dependent (Mateos et al. 2009), so a single dipole is an approximation outside that range.

Value: vig_axis ccf (production: telescope axis of XMM_MISCDATA) | pointing (stage default); mos_rga_vignetting 'ccf' (production: the SAS CAL RGA obscuration, instrument.rga_obscuration) | None (no MOS RGA modulation)

Used by: instrument.vig_axis, instrument.mos_rga_vignetting, instrument.replay_views, sim.generate_views

Evidence: xmm_cal_tn0018 (Sect. 4.5 (Vignetting), pp. 13-14 of Issue 3.14 (18 February 2025)); xmm_cal_tn0018 (Sect. 2.3 (Summary of important ongoing calibration topics), p. 6 of Issue 3.14); xmm_uhb (Sect. 3.3 (EPIC), detector layout, https://xmm-tools.cosmos.esa.int/external/xmm_user_support/documentation/uhb/epic.html); rosen2016 p. 10 (Sect. 6 (different boresight positions give sources different vignetting factors)); xmm_uhb (Sect. 3.2.2.2 (Off-axis effective area) and Fig. 14, https://xmm-tools.cosmos.esa.int/external/xmm_user_support/documentation/uhb/effareaoffaxis.html (read 2026-10-20)); xmm_calhb (CAL_getRGAIntercept, Procedure (Sect. 3.4.29), Eq. 28, node440.html; CAL_getRGAObscure, Procedure, node447.html; XAreaEf, Description, node665.html; TELCOORD, node7.html (read 2026-11-03)); xmm_uhb (Sect. 4.4.2 (Position angle constraints and determination), https://heasarc.gsfc.nasa.gov/docs/xmm/uhb/posdet.html (read 2026-11-03)); mateos2009 p. 10 (Sect. 5 and Fig. 10 (MOS vignetting modulated in azimuth by the RGA obscuration, energy and off-axis dependent; pn has no azimuthal term))

Instrument document: SAS telescope-axis calibration (XMM MISCDATA, XMM-SOC-CAL-TN-0018 Issue 3.14 sect. 4.5); RGA obscuration of the SAS calibration layer (CCF RGS1_QUANTUMEF_0015 / RGS2_QUANTUMEF_0016 RGA_OBSCURATE, RGS1/2_LINCOORD_0008, XMM_MISCDATA_0022; Calibration Access and Data Handbook, CAL_getRGAIntercept Eq. 28 and CAL_getRGAObscure)

Code: src/tngxmm/instrument/simulate_instrument.py, src/tngxmm/instrument/calibration_axis.py, src/tngxmm/instrument/load_responses.py, src/tngxmm/instrument/rga_obscuration.py, src/tngxmm/instrument/make_exposure_maps.py

instrument.sky_background¶

Kind: literature (OPEN: no citation and no validation yet)

The astrophysical background photons of every mock (simulate_instrument.skybkg_spectrum, instrument.sky_model = ‘donor-rass’): the Local Hot Bubble (APEC, kT = 0.111 keV, unabsorbed), the Galactic halo (APEC, kT = 0.191 keV) and the unresolved cosmic X-ray background (power law, photon index 1.41), the last two absorbed by the view’s Galactic column, all at solar abundance of Anders and Grevesse (1989). The temperatures are published medians: the LHB of Yeung et al. (2024, |b| > 30 deg), the halo of Henley and Shelton (2013, 83 sight lines). The LHB and halo normalisations are the conditions donor’s own: each field’s RASS diffuse-background spectrum in the 1-2 deg annulus about the cluster, from the HEASARC X-ray Background Tool, fitted with this model (the temperatures, the index and the CXB fixed; the CXB at the Moretti et al. 2003 0.5-2 keV total, the CXB the mocks carry as drawn AGN plus the unresolved remainder), as Snowden et al. (2008) constrain the foreground of XMM-Newton cluster fields (donor_tables.rass_sky; table donor_sky_rass.csv, sha256 1a42bcf8). Fitted free, the CXB came out at that level (median 9.1e-7 against 9.2e-7 per arcmin^2 over the 638 fields); fixing it moves the halo normalisations by -0.8 per cent at the median (16-84 per cent -11 to +10) and the LHB by < 0.5 per cent. The unresolved CXB is the Moretti et al. (2003) 0.5-2 keV total minus the intensity of the AGN the stage draws (1e-15 to 1e-12 erg/s/cm^2 from the same counts), 23 per cent of the total. The spectrum is folded through the camera’s vignetted effective area and drawn as Poisson events per active pixel. The previous sky (the xmm_simulator defaults, one fit to the MACS J0949 field, LHB norm 2.93e-6 per arcmin^2) stays as sky_model = ‘xmm_simulator’. Not modelled: solar-wind charge exchange, the variation of the foreground inside the 1-2 deg annulus, the temperature of each field. Seven broad RASS bands do not determine the temperatures; with them fixed the fits leave reduced chi^2 of several (RASS systematics and the fixed temperatures), so the normalisations are the field’s level, not a spectral model of it.

Value: sky_model donor-rass: LHB APEC kT 0.111 keV, halo APEC kT 0.191 keV, norms per donor from RASS (1-2 deg annulus); unresolved CXB power law index 1.41, norm from Moretti et al. 2003 minus the drawn AGN; abundances AG89 solar

Used by: instrument.sky_model, sim.generate_views, instrument.replay_views

Evidence: snowden2008 p. 8 (Sect. 3.4.1 (the RASS spectrum of an annulus about the cluster from the HEASARC tool constrains the CXB of the field)); snowden2008 p. 10 (Sect. 4.4 (the 1-2 deg annulus of the Abell 1795 example)); snowden2008 p. 9 (Sect. 3.4.1 (the model: unabsorbed LHB, absorbed halo, power law)); yeung2024 p. 8 (Sect. 4 (median LHB temperature 0.111 keV over |b| > 30 deg)); henley2013 p. 1 (Abstract (median halo temperature 2.22e6 K = 0.191 keV)); henley2013 p. 4 (Sect. 3 (abundance table of the halo fits, the one of the stage’s APEC)); moretti2003 p. 9 (Sect. 5 (total 0.5-2 keV CXB intensity)); moretti2003 p. 11 (Sect. 7.1 (soft-band number counts, the AGN draw and the resolved intensity)); deluca2004 p. 1 (Abstract (CXB photon index)); liu2017 p. 5 (Sect. 4 (sky range of the LHB emission measure))

Validation: No tuned factors: the last replacements (2026-11-03) – 638 donor fields: LHB norm median 6.3e-7 (16-84 per cent 4.7-9.4e-7, 99.5 per cent inside the Liu et al. range), halo median 1.05e-6 (6.7e-7 to 2.0e-6, about 3 times the Henley & Shelton high-latitude median), RASS CXB median 9.1e-7 (Moretti total at index 1.41: 9.2e-7), chi^2 median 40 for 4 dof; the earlier fixed LHB, 2.93e-6, was twice the top of the published range; the soft-band field level of mocks against donors (STATUS A2) is the test

Code: src/tngxmm/instrument/simulate_instrument.py, src/tngxmm/donor_tables/rass_sky.py, src/tngxmm/instrument/point_sources.py

instrument.conditions_donor¶

Kind: instrument

Under the spec field donors.observing_conditions = {‘rule’: ‘nn’, ‘n’: N, …} (instrument.conditions_donor; the production value since release 1.1.1, the same value for the generation, the replay and the frozen evaluation; None is the physics donor’s own conditions) a view keeps its PHYSICS donor (mass, redshift, apertures, the count-matching target) and is observed under a CONDITIONS donor drawn from the N nearest real observations of the donor pool in a condition space: per camera log10 live time (PPS exposure-map level) and log10 QPB scale of the donor background table, log10 N_H, log10 theta500 and the core offset of the donor’s own cluster, each standardised by the pool’s robust spread, plus 9 for a pn or MOS filter mismatch and 4 for a pn submode mismatch (squared standard units); the physics donor itself is the nearest. Replica k takes element (k-1) mod N of a permutation seeded per physics donor (new donor-level stream ‘conditions’), so the K replicas of a donor get different conditions donors; every donor-keyed input (PPS exposure maps, background row, live times, filters, modes, active CCDs, readout windows, AGN normalisation row, N_H, core offset, pointing and roll) is the conditions donor’s. The conditions donor is drawn before the photons, so the production generation absorbs the photon list directly with its N_H; a replay of stored photons moves them to the conditions donor’s N_H by keeping each with probability T(E;N_H,c)/T(E;N_H,view), which cannot add photons where N_H,c is the lower column (the deficit is recorded, nh_deficit). In the PRODUCTION generation (campaign run, sim.generate_views) nothing else is thinned: the mocks keep the luminosity of the TNG gas, and the mocks that are fitted for the mass bias are these. The realism replays are unthinned too (since 2026-11-03; the frozen evaluation refuses a thin table). A diagnostic replay with instrument.replay.thin_table multiplies the keep probability by the count-matching factor f of the physics donor (median 0.54, 16-84 per cent 0.26-0.93, DECISIONS ‘Count matching: the background of the net counts’), a factor measured on the views against the data, so that the replica has the net counts of the real cluster; it is never part of a verdict. Jitter from the view’s new ‘conditions’ stream: (exposure) one common log-normal factor on the live times, sigma = the robust spread of log effective live time among the N candidates, clipped to their range; (roll) the position angle re-drawn as the nominal angle of the conditions donor’s date (the position angle of the Sun seen from the pointing) plus an off-nominal angle drawn from the archive (PA_PNT minus that angle for the 593 bank observations of the target table), POSANGLE follows it and the cluster’s detector-frame offset turns by minus the change (the cluster keeps its sky offset from the pointing; the detector, all cameras together, turns under it); (pointing) an isotropic Gaussian aimpoint offset, sigma 0.735 arcmin per axis, truncated at 2.42 arcmin, from repeated on-axis observations of the same clusters in the bank (22 clusters, 45 observations: pointing minus catalogue position about each cluster’s mean, median radius 0.865 arcmin, 90th percentile 2.42 arcmin). Release 1.2 (key per_projection, set in no preset yet): the views of one halo-snapshot draw in view order, each uniformly from the first integer of its own ‘conditions’ stream, among the N nearest pool observations of its anchor (its physics donor; for a halo-snapshot with no real observation in the pairing window, the nearest observation of the pairing, which lends no physics; such views cannot be generated yet) that no earlier view of the halo-snapshot has taken. The views of a halo-snapshot therefore never share a conditions donor, even where several share a physics donor; the fold restriction and the jitter rule are unchanged. Where views share an anchor a later view draws further from it (production table, six views on one physics donor: median distance 2.20 at view 0 and 2.99 at view 5, median exposure-jitter width 0.28 and 0.33 in ln).

Value: donors.observing_conditions {'rule': 'nn', 'n': 5, 'jitter': ['exposure', 'roll', 'pointing']} (production, release 1.1.1) | None (the physics donor's own conditions) | the same with 'per_projection': True (release 1.2, no preset yet)

Used by: donors.observing_conditions, instrument.replay_views, sim.generate_views

Evidence: xmm_uhb (Sect. 4.2.2 Celestial constraints, https://xmm-tools.cosmos.esa.int/external/xmm_user_support/documentation/uhb/celest.html); xmm_uhb (Sect. 4.4.2 Position angle constraints and determination, https://xmm-tools.cosmos.esa.int/external/xmm_user_support/documentation/uhb/posdet.html); xmm_uhb (Sect. 4.4.1 Instrument alignment, https://xmm-tools.cosmos.esa.int/external/xmm_user_support/documentation/uhb/align.html); rosen2016 p. 6 (Sect. 3 (astrometric correction: frame shifts of about 1 arcsec, up to 10 arcsec))

Validation: Release 1.1.1 – Final confirmation, 96 physics donors x 5 replicas (480 views, 171 distinct conditions donors; INDIST/cond/node/analysis_n5.txt): supervised physics-donor accuracy 0.81 -> 0.09 on the data-quality features (0.73 -> 0.17 on all features; chance 0.01); replica ICC(1) 0.82 -> 0.33 (data-quality features), 0.58 -> 0.32 (all); real-vs-mock C2ST on 194 + 194 views 0.879 -> 0.883 (logistic), 0.961 -> 0.970 (boosting). Earlier round: N = 5, jitter exposure + roll + pointing, the combined round-2 instrument (‘combined_r2’: an older configuration, with the absolute AGN normalisation, not the final one; the final configuration is re-measured by the frozen evaluation), login-node evaluation (INDIST/cond). (a) Real against one replica per donor, 194 donors: feature C2ST AUC (logistic / boosting) 0.879 / 0.961 with the donor’s own conditions, 0.883 / 0.970 under the rule (folds grouped by the physics donor; 0.884 / 0.966 grouped by the conditions donor), unchanged within the noise; 119 distinct conditions donors. (b) 12 physics donors with all 5 replicas (60 views): the nearest real observation among the donor’s 5 condition neighbours in the background, footprint and compact-source features is the physics donor for 0.53 of the replicas with the donor’s conditions and 0.22 under the rule (chance 0.20); a logistic classifier of the physics donor trained on 4 replicas of each donor and tested on the fifth: 0.90 -> 0.33 with all 17 features, 0.92 -> 0.25 with the data-quality features (chance 0.083). (c) Replica-to-replica intra-class correlation over the 12 donors, mean of the 17 features 0.63 -> 0.39, of the data-quality features 0.74 -> 0.30 (log_bkg_rate 0.98 -> 0.42, hr_bkg 0.95 -> 0.09, cam_ratio_bkg 0.99 -> 0.07, fov_frac 1.00 -> 0.51). Counts: the replica’s net 0.15-1 R500 counts move by the ratio of effective live times of the two donors (median residual 0.000 dex, robust scatter 0.042 dex, 57 views); the N_H move leaves a deficit (f T_c/T_p > 1) for 33 per cent of the views, median 0, 90th percentile 9.7 per cent of the photons. Condition space at N = 5: the other candidates differ from the donor by a median 0.12 dex in effective live time (random pairs 0.33), 0.07 dex in pn QPB scale (0.16), 0.18 dex in N_H (0.27), with a filter or submode mismatch for 15 per cent (random 79 per cent).

Instrument document: XMM-Newton Users Handbook, online edition, sections 4.2.2 (celestial constraints), 4.4.1 (instrument alignment) and 4.4.2 (position angle), read 2026-10-20

Code: src/tngxmm/instrument/conditions_donor.py, src/tngxmm/instrument/replay.py, src/tngxmm/core/rng.py, src/tngxmm/donors/pair_projections.py

instrument.camera_layout¶

Kind: validation

The cluster sits at its donor’s measured core offset in the mask frame; the V3 camera offsets are set to zero because the packaged masks already carry the camera layout; the random dither cap is not applied (donor offsets are copied exactly).

Value: placement donor-offset; camera_offset_arcmin all (0, 0); dither_cap false

Used by: instrument.placement, instrument.camera_offset_arcmin, instrument.dither_cap

Evidence: epic_mos p. 4 (Sect. 4 (seven CCDs per MOS camera, the central one on the optical axis; the two MOS cameras are mounted orthogonally and the pn at 45 degrees to them)); snowden2008 p. 3 (Sect. 2 (the MOS detectors have seven CCDs, one near the optical axis and six in a hexagonal pattern around it)); xmm_uhb (Sect. 3.3.1.1 (EPIC MOS chip geometry) and Fig. 20 caption, https://xmm-tools.cosmos.esa.int/external/xmm_user_support/documentation/uhb/moschipgeom.html); xmm_uhb (Sect. 3.3 (EPIC), details of the detector layout and Fig. 19 caption, https://xmm-tools.cosmos.esa.int/external/xmm_user_support/documentation/uhb/epic.html)

Validation: Instrument choices (from the W2 inventory and the audit) – without the V3 camera offsets the mock footprints match real PPS exposure maps to 0.02’ on 24 observations (median IoU PN 0.74 -> 0.94, MOS2 0.81 -> 0.95)

Constants of the instrument stage that fix the frame of the products, not the physics: the nominal sky position of the mock field (ra0, dec0), the pixel scale of the merged products (the PPS image scale), the size of the merged canvas, and the clearance of the legacy random-dither placement.

instrument.pattern_loss¶

Kind: instrument

The PATTERN selection of the EPIC images (pn PATTERN <= 4, MOS <= 12) is applied from the pattern fractions of the calibration files (EPN_QUANTUMEF_0018, FRACTION_ENERGY: FRAC_SD / FRAC_SDTQ, and FRAC_S / FRAC_SD for the single-pixel pn band 1), not from a constant: instrument.pattern_loss = ‘ccf’, the only implemented rule. Releases up to 1.2 carried a field pattern_loss_factor = 0.92 that was read only as a switch (any value but 1 selected the CCF rule) and never multiplied; it is replaced by the named switch. SAS arfgen multiplies QE_TOTAL by the fraction of the selected patterns, FRAC_SD for a PATTERN<=4 pn image: arfgen(PATTERN<=4) = all-pattern ARF x FRAC_SD to 0.2 % at 0.5-8 keV, while the MOS ARF already carries the PATTERN<=12 loss through mos_areacorr (arfgen within 5 %). The legacy ratio and the fit’s all-pattern pn ARF therefore over-predict a real PPS pn image by 0.3 / 1.2 / 2.3 / 4.4 / 5.0 % at 0.5 / 1 / 2 / 5 / 8 keV. instrument.pattern_efficiency ‘sas’ (FRAC_SD in the mocks) and massfit.pn_arf ‘pattern’ (the PATTERN<=4 ARF in every fit) correct mocks and fits through one code path (the v1 presets).

Value: pattern_loss = 'ccf'; pattern_efficiency = 'sas' (FRAC_SD in the mocks); pn_arf = 'pattern' (the PATTERN<=4 pn ARF in every fit)

Used by: instrument.pattern_loss, instrument.pattern_efficiency, massfit.pn_arf

Evidence: epic_mos p. 5 (Sect. 6 (EPIC MOS event patterns: patterns 0 to 12 are X-ray events)); lovisari2020 p. 3 (Data-reduction paragraph (MOS PATTERN<=12, pn single events PATTERN==0)); mernier2017 p. 3 (Data-reduction paragraph (MOS pattern<=12, pn single events only because pn double events may suffer from charge transfer inefficiency)); xmm_uhb (Sect. 3.3.11, Table 7 (List of EPIC event patterns), https://xmm-tools.cosmos.esa.int/external/xmm_user_support/documentation/uhb/epic_evgrades.html); xmm_cal_tn0018 (Sect. 11.2.2 (EPIC-pn, imaging modes), p. 33 of Issue 3.14 (18 February 2025)); baldi2012 p. 2 (Sect. 2 (XMM-Newton data reduction of a cluster sample)); bartalucci2017 p. 11 (Appendix A, Sect. 1.2 (XMM-Newton data reduction)); tozzi2015 p. 3 (Sect. 2.1 (XMM-Newton data reduction of the Phoenix cluster))

Validation: – R6: apply_pattern_loss returns 1.0000 in 0.5-2 keV for the three cameras

Instrument document: XMM CCF EPN_QUANTUMEF_0018 (extension FRACTION_ENERGY; FRAC_T = FRAC_Q = 0, FRAC_SD = FRAC_SDTQ = 0.984 at 1 keV, so the ratio applied is 1)

Code: src/tngxmm/instrument/apply_pattern_loss.py

instrument.response¶

Kind: instrument

The spectral response of the mocks and of the fits. The presets use the per-observation responses: every image takes the RMF and on-axis ARF SAS 22.1 makes for its own exposure, a mock those of its conditions donor, on both sides (instrument.responses ‘donor’, massfit.responses ‘per-observation’), and both sides select the calibration files as cifbuild does (ccf_selection ‘valid-at-date’); the response set (instrument.obs_responses) must be built before a view is generated or fitted. Before this adoption the presets used one response per camera for every image, mock or real (the shared responses described next), and a preset may still do so with all four fields set to None. The shared responses: the instrument stage redistributes the photons with xmm_simulator’s packaged RMF of the camera and the fit reads the same files (MBPROJ/responses_v1, identical sha256), made on 2020-10-14 with rmfgen 2.5.1 of SAS 16.1 for one observation the files do not name; their MOS redistribution is that of the calibration epoch 2011-09-07 to 2013-04-26 (EMOSn_REDIST_0120, the newest issue in 2020). The effective area of the generator and the fit’s on-axis ARFs are built from the same CCF tables (mirror area with the MOS-to-pn CORRAREA, filter, quantum efficiency, the legacy mos_areacorr for MOS), each the highest issue number in the CCF file names: for the MOS quantum efficiency the 2002 files EMOS1_QUANTUMEF_0026 and EMOS2_QUANTUMEF_0024 (header ISSUE 14), which SAS no longer selects; cifbuild takes the constituent valid at the observation date with the highest issue, 0020 before 2002-11-08 and 0021 after (MOS on-axis area 6.4 / 2.9 per cent below the SAS issues at 0.5-1 keV, 2.0 / 1.7 per cent at 1-2 keV). Neither side has the MOS contamination or the EPIC-to-NuSTAR correction that SAS 22 applies by default. A mock is therefore made and fitted with one response and never meets an epoch or calibration difference; a real fit does. Measured with SAS 22.1 on 13 real observations of 2000-2025 (report R5-A4-responses): fitted with the shared responses instead of its own (cluster-region RMF, on-axis ARF with the SAS 22 defaults, ABSCORRAREA included), a real observation reads its three-camera temperature 0.0-1.8 / 0.9-3.3 / 2.3-5.7 per cent high at 3 / 5 / 8 keV (median +1.0 / +2.2 / +4.1), more for later observations. Three terms make it up: the EPIC-to-NuSTAR correction +0.9 / +1.9 / +3.5 per cent; the withdrawn MOS quantum efficiency alone -1.2 / -1.7 / -2.4 for the three cameras (MOS1 -4.9 / -6.5 / -9.1, MOS2 -2.3 / -3.3 / -4.8); the rest (MOS contamination, the MOS hard-band area of the legacy mos_areacorr, redistribution) +0.3 to +2.1 / +0.6 to +3.0 / +1.2 to +4.5, rising with the date. Measured separately, the three terms add to the total of every epoch within 0.06 points (0.3 points at 8 keV in an XSPEC check; at 8 keV the totals run from +2.31 in 2000 to +5.62 in 2025). The redistribution alone moves it by -0.4 to +0.8 per cent; a single MOS2 camera of 2015-2025 reads 12-16 per cent high at 8 keV (its contamination). instrument.ccf_selection and massfit.ccf_selection ‘valid-at-date’ make both sides take the files cifbuild selects (instrument.ccf_select, which reproduces cifbuild’s index files on 436 scope-types of four runs); with the shared responses the real-side error is then +2.2 / +3.9 / +6.7 per cent (median). Before the adoption the presets kept the shared responses with the highest file-name issue as the production choice pending the author, not for the size of their error; the shared set’s error is quoted for both selections, and the closure arm v1_obsresp_shared measures it through the whole fit once the response set exists. instrument.responses ‘donor’ with massfit.responses ‘per-observation’ replace the shared responses by the RMF and on-axis ARF SAS 22.1 makes for each exposure (a mock: its conditions donor’s; instrument.obs_responses, the set built by slurm/obs_responses.sh), which takes all three terms out of the comparison of mocks and real data. A preset must pair the two response switches and the two selections (config.check_response_pairing), and a fit refuses a mock whose RESPSRC or CCFSEL differs from its own responses. The calibration error itself remains. The published cross-calibration has two cases: ACIS correct, the closure arm v1_xcallo (the effective area divided by the ACIS/PN spline of Schellenberger et al. 2015, Table A.3), and pn correct, no change (the nominal fit); the range of the term is [v1_xcallo, nominal], and the pair v1_nustarlo / v1_nustarhi (divided / multiplied by the EPIC-to-NuSTAR correction of the CCF) gives the size of the latest change of the EPIC calibration. v1_xcalhi (multiplied by the spline) has no counterpart in the paper and is kept as a diagnostic. The term is also stated as an external systematic: XMM-Newton hydrostatic masses are 0.859 times the Chandra ones for the HIFLUGCS clusters (Schellenberger et al. 2015, Eq. D.1).

Value: instrument.responses = 'donor'; massfit.responses = 'per-observation'; instrument.ccf_selection = 'valid-at-date'; massfit.ccf_selection = 'valid-at-date'; instrument.area_perturbation = None | shared (the presets before the adoption): the four response fields None; closure arms: area_perturbation 'acis-pn-s15:-1' (range [v1_xcallo, nominal]), 'epic-nustar:-1', 'epic-nustar:+1', diagnostic 'acis-pn-s15:+1'; v1_obsresp_shared (donor responses fitted with the shared set)

Used by: instrument.responses, instrument.area_perturbation, instrument.ccf_selection, massfit.responses, massfit.ccf_selection

Evidence: xmm_cal_tn0018 p. 5 (Sect. 2.2 (changes of the calibration: the epoch-dependent MOS redistribution, the time-dependent pn response, the two empirical effective-area corrections applied by default from SAS 22.0)); xmm_cal_tn0018 p. 9 (Sect. 4 (the empirical effective-area corrections: MOS-to-pn CORRAREA; EPIC-to-NuSTAR, an identical change of the three cameras of 6-8 per cent between 3 and 12 keV)); xmm_cal_tn0018 p. 13 (Sect. 4.4 (EPIC-MOS contamination: its time evolution in the CCF)); schellenberger2015 p. 8 (Sect. 6.2 (the stacked-residuals splines as effective-area corrections between two instruments)); schellenberger2015 p. 8 (Sect. 6.2 (full-band EPIC-pn temperatures against ACIS at 10 keV; Table 2)); schellenberger2015 p. 9 (Table 2 (full band: EPIC temperatures below ACIS at 10 keV by 17 per cent for MOS1, 22 for MOS2, 29 for pn, 23 for the combined XMM)); schellenberger2015 p. 18 (Table A.3 (spline knots 0.54-6.55 keV, value y and second derivative of ACIS/PN, MOS1/PN, MOS2/PN; -14.69 is y’’ of ACIS/PN at the first knot)); schellenberger2015 p. 6 (Sect. 6.1 (stacked residuals: flat above 3 keV, a decrease of about 20 per cent for ACIS/PN from 3 to 1 keV)); schellenberger2015 p. 1 (Abstract (full-band 0.7-7 keV temperatures of EPIC against ACIS at 10 keV)); schellenberger2015 p. 25 (Appendix D, Eq. D.1 (M500 of XMM-Newton = 0.859 M500 of Chandra for the HIFLUGCS clusters)); nevalainen2010 p. 1 (Abstract (IACHEC clusters: hard-band temperatures consistent between instruments, full-band temperatures uncertain by 10-15 per cent)); read2014 p. 1 (Abstract (MOS-to-pn on-axis effective-area differences from 46 point sources)); sas_cifbuild p. 5 (Sect. 3.6 (membership of the current calibration file: validity start VALDATE, end EVALDATE, the applicable issue at a time T)); sas_cifbuild p. 13 (Sect. 8 (algorithm: per scope and type, the constituent with VALDATE <= observation date, SUBDATE <= analysis date, EVALDATE >= observation date and the highest ISSUE))

Validation: – three cameras, shared against own SAS 22.1 responses: T +0.0 to +1.8 / +0.9 to +3.3 / +2.3 to +5.7 per cent at 3 / 5 / 8 keV over 13 epochs 2000-2025 (RMF alone -0.4 to +0.8); MOS quantum-efficiency issue alone -1.2 / -1.7 / -2.4 (MOS1 -4.9 / -6.5 / -9.1); with the shared ARFs of the SAS selection +1.2 to +3.0 / +2.6 to +5.0 / +4.8 to +8.3; arms by the band-ratio fit at 5 keV: v1_xcallo -14.2, v1_nustarlo -1.8, v1_nustarhi +1.9 (diagnostic v1_xcalhi +19.0) per cent; v1_xcallo at 10 keV -21.7 (pn; paper 29), -24.2 / -24.0 (MOS1 / MOS2; paper 17 / 22), -22.6 (three cameras; paper 23, partly coincidental); the three terms measured separately add to the total of each epoch within 0.06 points (0.3 at 8 keV in XSPEC)

Instrument document: shared: xmm_simulator rmfs/<camera>.rmf = MBPROJ/responses_v1/<camera>.rmf (rmfgen 2.5.1, SAS 16.1, 2020-10-14) and CCF XRTn_XAREAEF (ONAXISXAREAEF, CORRAREA), E*_FILTERTRANSX, E*_QUANTUMEF (highest issue); per observation: SAS 22.1.0 rmfgen 2.8.7 and arfgen 1.106.1 with the valid CCF set of 2026-10-09 (EMOSn_REDIST by epoch, EPN_REDIST_0013, EMOSn_CONTAMINATION_0002, XRT3_XAREAEF_0014 ABSCORRAREA); SAS rmfgen documentation: the MOS redistribution has a patch at the boresight with a broadened response below 1 keV that has evolved with time

Code: src/tngxmm/instrument/load_responses.py, src/tngxmm/instrument/ccf_select.py, src/tngxmm/massfit/build_responses.py, src/tngxmm/instrument/obs_responses.py, src/tngxmm/instrument/area_perturbation.py, src/tngxmm/validation/closure_grid.py, src/tngxmm/config.py

instrument.pn_out_of_time¶

Kind: instrument

pn out-of-time events: every pn X-ray event (cluster, sky, AGN) is out-of-time with the probability of its readout mode (full frame 6.3 %, extended full frame 2.3 %, large window 0.16 %, small window 1.1 %) and lands uniformly on its own CCD column, both sides of the source, as epchain simulates it. Their energies carry the CTI correction of the assigned row instead of the true one, as epevents gives them: E x c(A, y_rec) / c(A, y_true), where c is the ratio of corrected to raw amplitude that the SAS calibration routines apply on the EPN_CTI calibration (CAL_pnCtiCorrect and CAL_pnAdditionalGainCtiCorrect with the epevents defaults), evaluated once per pn mode, epoch (yearly, 2000-2027) and CCD by tools/pn_cti with the CAL state at the CCD temperature of the camera, 183.2 K (the focal-plane housekeeping value F1128 of the PPS pn event lists, -89.95 C at every epoch read), and packaged as tngxmm/instrument/calibration/pn_cti_slopes.csv. The CTI model depends on that temperature; a table evaluated without it (before 2026-11-03) had k 0.74-0.88 times as large. Along a column ln c is a straight line in RAWY to 1 per cent at most, so the shift is exp(k(A) (y_rec - y_true)); k is 9.2e-4 per row at 1 keV in Full Frame in 2010 (mean of the twelve CCDs; 1.09e-3 at 0.5 keV, 7.6e-4 at 2 keV), two thirds of the 1.4e-3 that releases 1.1-1.1.3 had measured on real streak hardness and that the integration of 2026-10-30 removed. The RAWY slope of ln(PI / 5 PHA) of single-pixel events in PPS event lists, i.e. the correction the pipeline applied, is 0.99 times this k (tools/pn_cti/pps_cti_slope_check.py). The fit’s out-of-time template moves its counts between the bands with the same k (mean of the twelve CCDs, band centres). The column mean of k is used within a CCD; the precursor dependence of the CTI (below 0.003 in the streak hardness at the column occupancy of real streaks) and the pixel-by-pixel correction of split events are left out.

Value: pn_oot_model full-column; instrument.pn_oot_energy = ccf-cti; massfit.pn_oot_energy = ccf-cti (CTI table of the CAL at the CCD temperature 183.2 K) | none (energies unchanged)

Used by: instrument.pn_oot_model, massfit.pn_oot, instrument.pn_oot_energy, massfit.pn_oot_energy
