Evidence: xmm_sas_epevents (epevents-6.55 documentation, Out-of-time events (node6, node7) and Description (node5), https://xmm-tools.cosmos.esa.int/external/sas/current/doc/epevents/node7.html); xmm_sas_cal (SAS 22.1.0 doc/calsources/cal.f90 (CAL_pnCtiCorrect, CAL_pnAdditionalGainCtiCorrect) and calpnalgo-2.45, CTI correction and Gain correction (node5, node6), https://xmm-tools.cosmos.esa.int/external/sas/current/doc/calpnalgo/node5.html); dennerl2002 p. 3 (p. 3 (CTI in orbit: the pre-launch laboratory measurements were taken at the operating temperature of the camera)); xmm_cal_tn0018 (Issue 3.14, Sect. 6 (EPIC-pn long-term CTI), https://xmmweb.esac.esa.int/docs/documents/CAL-TN-0018.pdf); webb2020 p. 6 (p. 6 (source-specific products: for pn sources the background avoids the RAWY column of the source to exclude out-of-time events)); chexmate_sb2023 p. 3 (XMM data-reduction paragraph (epchain is run to produce the pn out-of-time event lists)); rosen2016 p. 8 (Sect. 3.6 (the emldetect background map includes a component that models the OoT features)); xmm_uhb (Sect. 3.3.10, Table 6 (EPIC Out-of-Time events), pn rows, https://xmm-tools.cosmos.esa.int/external/xmm_user_support/documentation/uhb/epicoot.html); xmm_uhb (Sect. 3.3.10 (EPIC out-of-time events), text and Figs. 41-42, https://xmm-tools.cosmos.esa.int/external/xmm_user_support/documentation/uhb/epicoot.html)

Validation: – PPS check: the RAWY slope of ln(PI / 5 PHA) of single-pixel events, per CCD and raw amplitude (0.45-5.5 keV), is 0.990 times the table’s k (16-84 per cent 0.974-1.008; 711 cells of 21 observations, 2000-2025), against 1.240 for the table evaluated without the CCD temperature; unit test on the stored sums of 0840580401. Streak hardness HR(1-2 / 0.5-1 keV) far side over source against the hardness expected from each streak’s own column (the column events given copies on the far-side rows with the table’s factor): 78 real Full Frame streaks -0.027 +- 0.017 (mean; -0.048 +- 0.017 with the earlier table), 42 realistic TNG pair views with a strong streak -0.026 +- 0.026 (median), so the estimator reads a common 0.03 low; the real streaks 0.833 (median, 16-84 per cent 0.71-0.96), the TNG views 0.86 with this table. The fixture view, whose source is softer (HR 0.76 against 1.07), reads 0.889 +- 0.047 over 12 seeds with this table against 0.899 expected from its own column (0.911 against 0.921 with the earlier table); paired with the same seeds and unchanged energies the shift is -0.122 against -0.129 expected, 0.062 scatter per seed. Width ratio real 1.01 [0.88-1.09] vs V4 0.92 +- 0.09. (Study A5 of 2026-11-03, tngxmm_dev/TODO_RUN/a5-streak-hardness)

Instrument document: XMM-Newton Users Handbook section 3.3.10, Table 6 (out-of-time fractions); SAS epchain / epevents withoutoftime; CCF EPN_CTI_0058 through the SAS 22.1.0 CAL routines at the pn CCD temperature of the housekeeping (F1128, HKAUX headers of the PPS pn event lists); the PPS event lists themselves for the check of the table

Code: src/tngxmm/instrument/pn_oot_energy.py, src/tngxmm/instrument/simulate_instrument.py, src/tngxmm/massfit/pn_oot.py, src/tngxmm/instrument/measure_oot_streak.py, tools/pn_cti/build_pn_cti_probe.sh, tools/pn_cti/pn_cti_probe.f90, tools/pn_cti/pps_cti_slope_check.py

instrument.out_of_fov¶

Kind: validation

Events on detector pixels with zero effective area (outside the 15’ vignetting field of view, including the QPB and out-of-time events in the CCD corners) are dropped, as the OUT_OF_FOV flag selection of the PPS images does; the exposure map is zero there.

Value: out_of_fov_events = drop

Used by: instrument.out_of_fov_events

Evidence: snowden2008 p. 3 (p. 3, instrument description (the filter wheel aperture leaves the outer CCD corners shielded from the sky)); snowden2008 p. 4 (p. 4, Sect. 3.1 (corner regions outside the field of view are read out like the rest and experience about the same quiescent particle background))

Validation: Readout windows – ‘keep’ put 4.4-4.7 % (pn), 0.7-0.9 % (MOS1), 1.6-1.7 % (MOS2) of the counts on EXPMAP == 0 pixels, real PPS products 0.0-0.24 % (campaign check C14)

instrument.donor_background¶

Kind: literature

With instrument.background = ‘donor’ (the InstrumentSpec default, the production value; the stage’s own InstrumentOptions default is ‘nominal’, the FWC level of every camera) the particle background of each camera follows its donor observation. pn: the donor’s PPS images are decomposed, outside 1.5 R500, into a vignetted (photon) and a flat (particle) part per camera and band (instrument/donor_background.py, Poisson fit of counts = a E + b E/V with V the band’s vignetting read off the exposure map); the FWC spectrum of the camera is renormalised to the donor’s flat 4.5-12 keV level and band by band to its flat spectrum (bands with a noisy flat level are shrunk to the 4.5-12 keV level, 0.3 dex prior; the decomposition’s own bias is divided out with its median on nominal mocks). MOS: the QPB level of each PPS band comes from the donor’s out-of-FOV corners (qpb_level ‘corners-band’, entry instrument.donor_corner_qpb) and the residual soft protons from the IN/OUT excess (soft_protons ‘inout’, entry instrument.background_three_components), because the in-FOV decomposition fails for the MOS: its MOS1 / MOS2 flat-level ratio is 1.15-1.39 in every epoch against 1.02 in the corners (the audit expects the particle levels of the two MOS cameras to agree), and the two-component fit returns a vignetted level of zero (degenerate) for about 70 per cent of the MOS1 cameras. Cluster photons, the sky model, the AGN and the hydrostatic estimand are not touched. Limits stated by the audit: the pn factors still rest on the in-FOV decomposition and the renormalisation of De Luca & Molendi (2004) is applied outside its regime (see the notes of the two blocks below), and the pn is not re-measured from out-of-FOV corners (the audit asks for about 20 donors); the effect of the final configuration is re-measured by the frozen evaluation.

Value: background donor (production): pn QPB band factors per donor from tables/donor_background.csv, MOS from the corner table (entries donor_corner_qpb, background_three_components) | nominal (stage default)

Used by: instrument.background, instrument.replay_views

Evidence: deluca2004 p. 17 (App. B.3, conclusions on the residual soft-proton background); deluca2004 p. 17 (App. B.3 (a residual soft-proton component survives the screening; flat power-law spectrum not convolved with the effective areas)); deluca2004 p. 16 (Fig. A.6 caption (the 2-12 keV shape of the quiescent particle spectrum is constant between high- and low-intensity closed observations)); snowden2008 p. 4 (Sect. 3.1 (QPB: spatial and temporal variation in magnitude and hardness)); snowden2008 p. 6 (Sect. 3.1.1 (the FWC spectra are scaled to each observation by the corner spectra)); snowden2008 p. 6 (Sect. 3.2-3.2.1 (most observations keep residual soft protons; their distribution over the FOV differs from photons and QPB)); snowden2008 p. 3 (Sect. 3 (the SP spectrum recorded by EPIC is a power law of variable magnitude and slope)); leccardi2008 p. 17 (App. A-B (to a first approximation the NXB is flat over the detector; only the photon components are multiplied by the effective area)); leccardi2008 p. 18 (App. B, Eq. B.1 (the ratio of the NXB normalisations of two detector regions does not depend on the observation)); leccardi2008 p. 18 (App. B (quiescent soft-proton spectrum)); leccardi2008 p. 4 (Sect. 3.1.2 (IN over OUT 6-12 keV surface-brightness ratio of 48 cluster observations)); katayama2004 p. 7 (Sect. 6.3 (pn: renormalising each background spectrum to the observation’s particle rate cuts the 2-7 keV scatter from 8 to 2 per cent)); katayama2004 p. 1 (Abstract); gastaldello2017 p. 1 (Abstract (cosmic-ray origin of the unfocused background; anti-correlation with solar activity))

Code: src/tngxmm/instrument/donor_background.py, src/tngxmm/instrument/simulate_instrument.py

instrument.donor_live_time¶

Kind: validation

With instrument.live_time = ‘donor-expmap’ (the InstrumentSpec default, the production value) each camera’s live time is the donor’s on-axis PPS exposure-map level (95th percentile within 1.5’ of the camera’s optical axis in the 0.5-1 keV map, all exposures of the camera summed; column live_s of tables/donor_background.csv) instead of the donor package’s cameras_s (the EXPOSURE keyword). The PPS exposure maps integrate the good time intervals of each chip and, for the pn, carry the mode’s frame-time correction, so the pn map lies about 15 per cent below the EXPOSURE keyword while the MOS maps match it. The stage’s own InstrumentOptions default ‘package’ keeps the EXPOSURE keyword. Budget of the pn gap: what the sources document is the out-of-time correction folded into the frame time (OOTFRAC = TIMEDEL / FRAMETIM; Full Frame has more than 6 per cent out-of-time events, Large Window 0.16 per cent) and the live time of the mode (Users Handbook Table 3: Full Frame 99.9, Extended Full Frame 100, Large Window 94.9, Small Window 71.0 per cent); the median gap of 14 per cent (mock / donor 1.165) is larger than the out-of-time share of Full Frame alone, and the split of the remainder into dead time, flare good-time-interval screening and the exposure keyword of the donor package is NOT measured per donor. The switch takes the donor’s own map, so the total is right whatever its composition; the composition is open.

Value: live_time donor-expmap (production) | package (stage default)

Used by: instrument.live_time, instrument.replay_views

Evidence: xmm_ccf_sas (SAS eexpmap description, https://xmm-tools.cosmos.esa.int/external/sas/current/doc/eexpmap/node3.html); xmm_uhb (Sect. 3.3.2 (Science modes of the EPIC cameras), Table 3 and its notes, https://xmm-tools.cosmos.esa.int/external/xmm_user_support/documentation/uhb/epicmode.html (local copy of the page))

Validation: Realism, release 1.1 – on-axis exposure-map level mock / donor, median over 195 count-matched pairs: pn 1.165 (10-90 %: 0.91-1.43), MOS1 0.985, MOS2 0.995

Code: src/tngxmm/instrument/donor_background.py, src/tngxmm/instrument/simulate_instrument.py

instrument.qpb_spatial¶

Kind: instrument

With instrument.qpb_spatial = ‘fwc’ (the InstrumentSpec default, the production value; the stage’s own default is ‘flat’) the quiescent particle background is not flat over the detector: each event lands on a pixel drawn with probability proportional to the filter-wheel-closed event density of its PPS band (the camera’s latest E*_FWC CCF event list, FLAG == 0 and the PPS PATTERN selection, per CCD, divided by each CCD’s FWC exposure, smoothed 1’ within the CCD, placed on the packaged mask by SAS’s own DET -> sky solution of the observation the masks were made from, read from its PPS event lists (donor_background.DET_TO_MASK, tools/det_to_mask.py; until 2026-11-03 a cross-correlated registration, which agreed to 1 mask pixel); donor_background.fwc_qpb_maps). MOS1 CCD3 and CCD6 are masked in the template: they are lost (instrument.mos_ccd_loss) and the FWC density of the closed lists still carries their events. The camera’s QPB total is unchanged. Regime: the template is the filter-wheel-closed distribution; the filter-open distribution may differ in the fluorescence lines (see the Snowden et al. note below). Measured structure (mean 1 over the detector): MOS 0.5-1 keV 0.8 in the centre to 1.1-1.35 at 14’, MOS1 1-2 keV 1.10 centre, pn 0.5-4.5 keV 1.10-1.16 centre, pn 4.5-12 keV 0.81 centre (the Cu-Ni-Zn complex is absent there). On 60 combined-configuration views it changed no background feature beyond its noise (ring ratio +0.022 to +0.017, camera ratio +0.052 to +0.050), so the adoption rests on the physics (the QPB is not flat), not on a measured gain of the realism test.

Value: qpb_spatial fwc (production, MOS1 CCD3 and CCD6 masked) | flat (stage default)

Used by: instrument.qpb_spatial, instrument.replay_views

Evidence: snowden2008 p. 4 (Sect. 3.1 and Fig. 2 (FWC images per band; the QPB and the Al and Si lines are not uniform over the detector)); leccardi2008 p. 17 (App. A (closed observations: Al lines stronger on the outer CCDs, Si lines on the central CCD)); katayama2004 p. 3 (Sect. 4 (pn: the 7.2-9 keV band is dominated by Cu fluorescence from the electronics board except in the central ~6’)); deluca2004 p. 12 (App. A.2 (spatial distribution of the quiescent NXB)); xmm_uhb (Sect. 3.3.7.2 (EPIC internal quiescent background), https://xmm-tools.cosmos.esa.int/external/xmm_user_support/documentation/uhb/epicintbkgd.html)

Instrument document: CCF E*_FWC filter-wheel-closed event lists (EMOS1_FWC_0005, EMOS2_FWC_0005, EPN_FWC_0005)

Code: src/tngxmm/instrument/donor_background.py, src/tngxmm/instrument/simulate_instrument.py

instrument.pn_edge_rows¶

Kind: instrument

The pn rows next to the readout node are left to the detector footprint. The PPS exposure maps are zero at RAWY <= 12 and the calibrated pn event lists hold no event there (and no FLAG == 0 event at RAWY = 13), so the donor’s footprint (instrument.detector_footprint, the donor’s own PPS exposure map) ends at the readout rows for the events and the exposure alike, and no row is cut on top. Releases up to 1.2 removed the events within 12 rows of the footprint’s edge, which are the real rows 14-25, while the exposure maps kept them: 3.6-6.9 per cent of the pn events of bands 2-5.

Value: pn_edge_rows = footprint | drop (releases up to 1.2)

Used by: instrument.pn_edge_rows

Evidence: xmm_uhb (Sect. 3.3.2, Table 3, note 5 (basic numbers for the science modes of EPIC), https://xmm-tools.cosmos.esa.int/external/xmm_user_support/documentation/uhb/epicmode.html); xmm_sas_eexpmap (eexpmap Description page (node3), https://xmm-tools.cosmos.esa.int/external/sas/current/doc/eexpmap/node3.html); katayama2004 p. 3 (p. 3 (radial background profile: the count rate rises beyond about 12 arcmin from a concentration of non-X-ray noise around the readout node)); webb2020 p. 3 (Sect. 3.2 (the event-list filter removes bad events such as bad rows, edge effects and spoiled frames)); xmm_cal_tn0018 (Sect. 11.2.2 (EPIC-pn, imaging modes), p. 32 of Issue 3.14 (18 February 2025))

Instrument document: PPS pn exposure maps (EXPMAP3000) and calibrated event lists (PIEVLI) of 0404910301, 0084230701 and 0821810801, row profiles by tools/pn_edge_rows/pps_row_profile.py: the map relative to rows 16-20 is 0.00 at RAWY 1-12, 0.09-0.10 at 13 and 0.83 at 14, and no event lies at RAWY <= 12; XMM-Newton Users Handbook Sect. 3.3.2, Table 3, note 5

Code: tools/pn_edge_rows/pps_row_profile.py

instrument.sas_ready_products¶

Kind: validation

The mocks write the five PPS bands (0.2-0.5, 0.5-1, 1-2, 2-4.5, 4.5-12 keV), the pn band 1 as the PPS image (single-pixel events kept with the CCF fraction FRAC_S/FRAC_SD) and the header keywords SAS tasks read, so that ESA’s edetect_chain runs identically on mocks and real data.

Value: product_bands pps-1-5; pn_band1_events single-pixel; sas_headers on; header_style v4

Used by: instrument.product_bands, instrument.pn_band1_events, instrument.sas_headers, instrument.header_style

Evidence: rosen2016 p. 7 (Sect. 3.5 (EPIC bands 1-5: 0.2-0.5, 0.5-1, 1-2, 2-4.5, 4.5-12 keV)); webb2020 p. 3 (Sect. 2 (source detection simultaneous on bands 1-5 of the three cameras))

Validation: SAS-ready products: photons from 0.2 keV, PPS band 1, SAS headers, SAS detection – full-frame single-pixel fraction 1.00 at 0.2 keV, 0.96 at 0.3, 0.84 at 0.5 keV (mean 0.93-0.96 over a soft spectrum); the DSS keywords of the donor PPS image P0551830101PNS003IMAGE_1000 give PATTERN == 0 && RAWY > 12

Instrument document: CCF EPN_QUANTUMEF_0018 (FRACTION_ENERGY: FRAC_S, FRAC_SD); PPS band definitions of the 4XMM catalogue

instrument.exposure_maps¶

Kind: validation

Exposure maps are built by the exact inverse of the event transform (sub-sampled 4 x 4 per output pixel), vignetted at the centre of the band (instrument.vig_energy_rule = ‘band-centre’, the InstrumentSpec default and SAS eexpmap’s documented rule, which is how the PPS exposure maps of the donors are made; the stage’s own InstrumentOptions default is ‘count-weighted’, which the bias audit preferred for wide bands but which makes the maps flatter than the PPS ones), with CRPIX/CLUSTX/CLUSTY a half pixel lower (FITS 1-based pixel centres) and MOS2 reading its own mirror CCF (XRT2). Regime of the band-centre rule: it matters most for the widest band (band 5, 4.5-12 keV), where the two energies differ most. The PPS band-5 maps of two fixture observations, divided by their 0.5-1 keV maps, follow the CCF vignetting at 8.26-8.29 keV, the band centre (2026-11-04; all three cameras, rms 0.0006-0.0028 out to 13 arcmin).

Value: expmap_method inverse-map; expmap_subsamples 4; vig_energy_rule band-centre (production) | count-weighted (stage default); half_pixel_fix true; mos2_mirror XRT2

Used by: instrument.expmap_method, instrument.expmap_subsamples, instrument.vig_energy_rule, instrument.half_pixel_fix, instrument.mos2_mirror

Evidence: chexmate_sb2023 p. 6 (Sect. 4.1 (mock XMM-Newton images of simulated clusters: vignetting from the calibration files, then PSF and Poisson counts)); xmm_sas_eexpmap (eexpmap Description page (node3), https://xmm-tools.cosmos.esa.int/external/sas/current/doc/eexpmap/node3.html)

Validation: – bias audit section 5 items 7 and 9 (band-5 vignetting energy; WCS half-pixel offset); the PPS band-5 maps of the fixtures over their 0.5-1 keV maps follow the CCF vignetting at 8.26-8.29 keV, the band centre (2026-11-04); W2 inventory F5, F7; indistinguishability (2026-10-20): count-weighted band-5 maps are 35 % flatter than the PPS ones at 10’ (stacked envelope, 30 donor / replica pairs: PPS 0.39-0.43, mock 0.54); vig_energy_rule band-centre, now a switch (InstrumentOptions), reproduces them within 1-3 % for MOS1 and pn (6 views; MOS2 7 % low)

Instrument document: SAS eexpmap documentation (evaluates QE, filter and vignetting at the mean of the PI boundaries); CCF XRT1/XRT2 vignetting and effective-area files

instrument.effective_area¶

Kind: instrument

The instrument stage folds the fit’s effective area: the on-axis ARF of massfit.build_responses (mirror area times CORRAREA, filter transmission and quantum efficiency, with the MOS area correction) on the energy bins of the camera’s RMF, times the XRTn_XAREAEF vignetting interpolated linearly in off-axis angle and energy (instrument.effective_area), and no area above the RMF, where XSPEC folds nothing either. The upstream calc_arf sampled the same CCF curves at the centres of 20 eV bins: the bin 0.52-0.54 keV took the area below the O-K edge (0.532 keV) for all of it, 33 per cent too much for MOS, so the stage’s MOS 0.5-1 keV area was 1.1-1.2 per cent above the fit’s (pn 0.2 per cent). Evaluated on the RMF’s bins, calc_arf agrees with the fit’s ARF to 2e-4: the grid made the difference, not the interpolation. With this entry and fit.band_channels, the stage’s expected responses and the fit’s responses agree within 0.25 per cent in every camera and band of the closure cells (r < 10 R500, z = 0.06-0.5); with release 1.2 the MOS 0.5-1 keV band of the stage was 2.4 per cent above the fit.

Value: arf_model = fit-linear | None (xmm_simulator calc_arf on 20 eV bins, releases up to 1.2)

Used by: instrument.arf_model

Evidence: xmm_cal_tn0018 p. 13 (Sect. 4.5 (Vignetting), p. 13 of Issue 3.14); xmm_cal_tn0018 p. 15 (Sect. 5.2 (EPIC-pn energy redistribution), p. 15 of Issue 3.14)

Instrument document: CCF XRTn_XAREAEF (EFFECTIVE_AREA, VIGNETTING), XRTn CORRAREA, EMOSn and EPN QUANTUMEF, FILTERTRANSX (the files massfit.build_responses reads); energy bins of the canned EPIC RMFs

Code: src/tngxmm/instrument/effective_area.py, src/tngxmm/instrument/load_responses.py

instrument.generator_versions¶

Kind: validation

Algorithm-version tags of the generator and instrument stage fixed since V3.1 (per-particle photon sampler, camera partition of the photons, ARF-weighted EMSCE exposure, photon normalisation v3, the corrected PSF radial sampler) are inherited unchanged; the legacy presets reproduce V3.2 byte for byte.

Value: psf_fix true; sampler per-particle; camera_partition true; exposure_convention arf-weighted-emsce; phot_norm_version 3

Used by: instrument.psf_fix, instrument.sampler, instrument.camera_partition, instrument.exposure_convention, instrument.phot_norm_version

Validation: Generation decisions after the node pilot – legacy presets keep the legacy behaviour (v32 products byte-exact: test_sim_exact, test_instrument_exact); W2 inventory U1 documents the upstream PSF sampler lacking the 2 pi r Jacobian

instrument.output_conventions¶

Kind: convention

Output bookkeeping of the instrument stage: file-name style, gzip determinism, per-camera products and the pts_sources list.

Value: obsid_style v4; deterministic_gzip true; write_percam true; write_source_lists true

Used by: instrument.obsid_style, instrument.deterministic_gzip, instrument.write_percam, instrument.write_source_lists

instrument.image_band¶

Kind: literature

The soft band 0.5-2 keV in which the merged mock image, the fit images and the Lx are defined, the band of XMM cluster surveys and of Chandra cluster luminosities (the effective area changes little over it for cluster spectra). The imaging band of the X-COP XMM analysis is different (0.7-1.2 keV, chosen to maximise the source-to-background ratio); the point-source detection of that analysis uses 0.5-2 keV.

Value: emin_image_kev 0.5, emax_image_kev 2.0; emin_lx_kev 0.5, emax_lx_kev 2.0

Used by: instrument.emin_image_kev, instrument.emax_image_kev, sim.emin_lx_kev, sim.emax_lx_kev

Evidence: pacaud2016 p. 4 (Sect. 3.2 (XXL cluster selection operates in the narrow [0.5-2] keV band, XMM effective area almost flat)); vikhlinin2009_cccp2 p. 1 (p. 1 (conventions of the paper, luminosities and fluxes in the 0.5-2 keV band)); pratt2009 p. 3 (p. 3 and Appendix B (REXCESS luminosities in [0.1-2.4] and [0.5-2] keV)); ghirardini2019 p. 3 (Sect. 2.2.3 (point sources detected in the soft [0.5-2] and hard [2-7] keV bands))

sim.rng_pairing¶

Kind: convention

The random streams are keyed by sim.rng_campaign, not by the preset name, so that a variant preset made for a sensitivity test can keep the production streams and differ from production in the field under test only. The v1 presets use the key of the committed target lists: ‘v4’ for TNG-Cluster and ‘v4_tng300’, ‘v4_tng100’, ‘v4_tng50’ for the boxes (entry run.random_streams).

Value: rng_campaign = 'v4' (v1); 'v4_tng300' / 'v4_tng100' / 'v4_tng50' (box presets)

Used by: sim.rng_campaign

photometry.lx_estimator¶

Kind: literature

The campaign Lx is measured with point sources masked in the aperture and the background annulus (the fit’s source lists), masked aperture pixels filled with the mean of the unmasked pixels in the same 8 arcsec ring (the aperture analogue of measuring the surface brightness in annuli on the unmasked area), a sector-clipped annulus mean and no silent clipping at zero: NaN unless the aperture flag is ok. Apertures more than 50 % masked are flagged.

Value: lx_bkg masked-clipped; lx_mask_aperture true; lx_fill_ring_arcsec 8; lx_max_masked_frac 0.5

Used by: photometry.lx_bkg, photometry.lx_mask_aperture, photometry.lx_fill_ring_arcsec, photometry.lx_max_masked_frac, photometry.unmeasurable_value

Evidence: vikhlinin2006 p. 2 (Sect. 2 (point sources masked, surface brightness in concentric annuli)); vikhlinin2009_cccp2 p. 6 (Sect. 3.2 (sources masked, azimuthally averaged profiles)); jennings_dave2023 p. 6 (Sect. 3 (total over un-masked pixels))

Validation: Campaign Lx background: masked-clipped, no silent clipping – mask injection on 42 views (6480 trials): ring fill median 1.000 up to 0.5 masked fraction (scatter +-5 %), a global area scaling is biased high by 2.5-42 %; measured/intrinsic scatter at 1 Mpc 0.215 -> 0.096 dex

photometry.lx_mask_radius¶

Kind: instrument

Point sources are masked out to a radius from the EPIC pn on-axis King PSF (core radius 5.495”, slope 1.588; at least 30” which holds EEF 0.87; until the wing of the source counts falls to 0.1 of the local level; at most 150”). The source list is the fit’s, the same rule on mocks and real data.

Value: min 30 arcsec; max 150 arcsec; wing fraction 0.1; King r0 5.495 arcsec, alpha 1.588

Used by: photometry.lx_mask_min_radius_arcsec, photometry.lx_mask_max_radius_arcsec, photometry.lx_mask_wing_frac, photometry.lx_mask_king_r0_arcsec, photometry.lx_mask_king_alpha

Validation: Campaign Lx background: masked-clipped, no silent clipping – a fixed 30 arcsec mask left the wings of a 2.2e-13 erg/s/cm2 AGN in the annulus (s045 h7122163 R500 2.3x); that AGN gets ~100 arcsec

Instrument document: XMM CCF XRT3_XPSF (pn on-axis King parameters, row 0 of KING_PARAMS)

photometry.lx_core_protection¶

Kind: instrument

A listed source within one half-energy width of the on-axis PSF (16.487 arcsec, the pn King profile of the CCF, r0 = 5.495 arcsec, alpha = 1.588: HEW = 2 r0 sqrt(2^(1/(alpha-1)) - 1)) of the aperture centre is not masked: at that separation the source and the cluster peak are not resolved by the telescope, so the detection cannot tell a cool core or a central AGN from a field source, and masking it would cut out the peak the aperture is centred on. The HEW is a diameter used here as a separation; that choice (rather than the FWHM, about 6 arcsec on axis) is a design choice with no citation: within it the two sources’ half-energy discs overlap the peak. No fraction of the aperture limits it (lx_mask_protect_core_frac = 1, inert: every aperture is larger than 16.5 arcsec). The same rule masks the fit’s sources (massfit.protect_core_arcsec). Until release 1.2 the radius was 90 arcsec with a limit of 0.4 of the aperture, chosen on mock views; both are gone.

Value: lx_mask_protect_core_arcsec 16.487; lx_mask_protect_core_frac 1.0

Used by: photometry.lx_mask_protect_core_arcsec, photometry.lx_mask_protect_core_frac

Instrument document: XMM CCF XRT3_XPSF, KING_PARAMS row 0 (on axis, 225 eV): r0 = 5.495 arcsec, alpha = 1.588; HEW from the King encircled fraction 1 - (1 + (r/r0)^2)^(1-alpha)

Code: src/tngxmm/massfit/mask_point_sources.py, src/tngxmm/photometry/estimate_lx_background.py

photometry.lx_background_clip¶

Kind: validation

The annulus background is the mean of 12 azimuthal sectors with whole sectors clipped beyond 3 times the larger of the Poisson error and 1.4826 MAD, the backstop for undetected sources and wings.

Value: lx_bkg_clip_sigma 3.0; lx_bkg_sectors 12

Used by: photometry.lx_bkg_clip_sigma, photometry.lx_bkg_sectors

Evidence: ansarifard2020 p. 7 (p. 7 (X-ray flux maps divided into 12 sectors around the peak, one surface-brightness profile per sector, to measure the azimuthal scatter)); ghirardini2019 p. 4 (p. 4 (the azimuthal median surface brightness in concentric annuli is robust against outliers of the gas density distribution; used for the X-COP profiles)); ghirardini2019 p. 3 (p. 3 (a sigma-clipping technique applied to the light curve excludes time intervals of enhanced background))

Validation: Campaign Lx background: masked-clipped, no silent clipping – a per-pixel clip at 0.5 counts/px removes the Poisson tail (9 % low); the sector clip is the version validated on 42 views

photometry.arf_band_mean¶

Kind: instrument

Mean effective area of each camera in the Lx band, used to convert counts to flux.

Value: MOS1 395, MOS2 400, PN 1259 cm^2

Used by: photometry.arf_band_mean_cm2

Evidence: epic_mos p. 2 (Sect. 1 (geometric area of 1500 cm2 per telescope; the gratings leave 44 per cent of the flux to the two MOS cameras))

Instrument document: XMM CCF effective-area files (XRT ARF with the camera quantum efficiency and filter transmission)

photometry.inactive_constants¶

Kind: convention

Constants of the photometry that no V4 result reads: the centre search radius, the redshift floor, and the image pixel (the PPS image scale, see instrument.canvas_and_placement_constants).

Value: pix_arcsec 4; center_search_arcmin 3.5; z_floor 0.01

Used by: photometry.pix_arcsec, photometry.center_search_arcmin, photometry.z_floor

photometry.legacy_apertures¶

Kind: validation

Legacy V3 constants of the aperture photometry: the background annulus in aperture units and in arcmin (angular-background seed).

Value: bkg_annulus_aperture_frac (1.1, 1.6); bkg_annulus_arcmin (11, 14)

Used by: photometry.bkg_annulus_aperture_frac, photometry.bkg_annulus_arcmin

Validation: SENS job results – S6: alternative annuli move the seed R500 by at most 6%; seed R500 x0.8 / x1.25 change ln(blue/red) by -0.004 [-0.013, +0.009] (PASS) / -0.002 [-0.018, +0.013] (INCONCLUSIVE), 43 views

fit.model¶

Kind: literature

The hydrostatic fit is the forward model of mbproj2d (J. Sanders): a single-component modified-beta gas density (Vikhlinin et al. 2006, gamma = 3), an NFW halo with a flat logarithmic prior on the concentration (1-10), pressure integrated inwards from an outer boundary pressure, Poisson likelihood, emcee sampling started from the maximum-likelihood position. mbproj2d has no paper of its own; Sanders et al. (2018) describes its one-dimensional predecessor and the same priors.

Value: ProfileVikhDensity single, gamma 3; NFW M500 x c200; emcee

Used by: massfit.fit_hydrostatic

Evidence: sanders2018 p. 4 (Sect. 2.5 (affine-invariant sampler, started around the maximum-likelihood position)); vikhlinin2006 p. 3 (Eq. 3); sanders2018 p. 21 (Appendix B, step iii (temperature from the total pressure with about 1.83 particles per electron))

Code: src/tngxmm/massfit/fit_hydrostatic.py

fit.molecular_weight¶

Kind: literature

The particle count of the fit is that of a fully ionised plasma of the abundance table’s composition (mu about 0.60), replacing mbproj2d’s built-in count of electrons plus hydrogen nuclei (about 1.83 particles per electron, mu = 0.641). This departs from the constant of the published code and is documented as a deviation.

Value: mu_convention full_ionisation; mu_composition abund-table

Used by: massfit.mu_convention, massfit.mu_composition

Evidence: pearce2020 p. 5 (Eq. 5 (mu = 0.59, primordial fully ionised plasma)); vikhlinin2006 p. 5 (Eq. 7 (coefficient 3.71e13 corresponds to mu = 0.600)); sanders2018 p. 21 (Appendix B, step iii (about 1.83 particles per electron, the constant we depart from)); ettori2019 p. 3 (Sect. 2, Eq. 1 (mu of the fully ionised plasma from X, Y, Z)); ettori2013 p. 8 (Sect. 2, Eq. 6 (the pressure of the hydrostatic equation: P_gas = rho_gas kT / (mu m_u) = n_gas kT)); ettori2013 p. 9 (Sect. 2, Eqs. 6-9 (mu = (2X + 3/4 Y + 1/2 Z)^-1 ~ 0.6 for the hydrostatic mass; n_gas = n_e + n_p ~ 1.826 n_e; mu_e = 1.155 and mu = 0.6 for a cosmic H/He mix with 0.3 solar metals)); yeung2024 p. 15 (Sect. 4.1.4, Eqs. 8-10 (thermal pressure of a hot, fully ionised plasma: n = n_e + the sum of the ion densities, about 1.92 n_e)); nagai2007 p. 4 (Sect. 3.2, Eq. 6 (hydrostatic mass of mock Chandra clusters with mu = 0.592, the fully ionised H-He plasma of the simulations)); martino2014 p. 6 (Eq. 8 (LoCuSS hydrostatic masses from XMM-Newton and Chandra with mu = 0.5964)); seppi2026 p. 13 (Appendix A, Eq. A.1 (gas mass of the CHEX-MATE-like mocks with mu_e computed by hydromass from the chosen abundance table, Asplund et al. 2009: 1.146). arXiv v1; the same passage is in the version of record (A&A 711, A213, Appendix A, p. 14), in the past tense)

Validation: – bias audit section 5 item 6: the correction raises every mass by +0.03 to +0.05 dex (G3 mock bias 0.65 -> 0.73)

fit.metallicity¶

Kind: validation

One global metallicity is fitted (flat prior 0-2 Z_sun, start 0.3) on mocks and real data alike, because a fixed 0.3 biased the temperature of the mocks and of real clusters in opposite senses. The fixed and per-population values are options not used by V4.

Value: metallicity_mode free; free prior (0.0, 2.0); fixed value 0.3 and per-population (mock 0.3, obs 0.3) unused

Used by: massfit.metallicity_mode, massfit.metallicity_free_prior, massfit.metallicity_zsun, massfit.metallicity_per_population

Evidence: sanders2018 p. 6 (Sect. 2.5.10 (metallicity fixed at 0.3 Z_sun; effect weak for hot massive clusters)); scheck2023 p. 4 (Sect. 3.4 (constant metallicity 0.3 Z_sun in MBProj2)); ghirardini2019 p. 7 (Sect. 2.4.2 (X-COP XMM-Newton spectra: absorbed single-temperature APEC per annulus with temperature, emission measure and metal abundance free)); mernier2017 p. 3 (Sect. 2 (XMM-Newton RGS+EPIC spectral model; the abundances are free parameters of the gdem model, with Z<=7 elements fixed to proto-solar and the rest tied to Fe, and allowed positive and negative values)); mernier2017 p. 6 (Table 2 (average radial Fe abundance profile of the CHEERS sample, relative to the proto-solar table of Lodders et al. 2009; 0.80-0.83 in the core, 0.20 at 0.55-1.22 r500, scatter up to 0.26)); sanders2018 p. 2 (Introduction (spectral fitting would allow the metallicity to be fitted given sufficient data quality)); sanders2022_mbproj2d p. 18 (Sect. 2.12 (hydrostatic MBProj2 fit of the eROSITA data of A3266: NFW mass model, metallicity parametrised at six radii with a flat prior between 0 and 1 Z_sun)); sanders2022_mbproj2d p. 2 (Sect. 1 (abundances relative to Asplund et al. 2009, about 50 per cent above the Anders and Grevesse 1989 values for iron)); ghizzardi2021 p. 2 (Sect. 2.2 (X-COP XMM-Newton spectra: absorbed APEC with temperature, emission measure and metal abundance free; in XSPEC the APEC abundance has a hard lower boundary at 0, which has significant implications, Sect. 2.3.3)); ghizzardi2021 p. 6 (Sect. 2.3.3 (a hard limit at 0 can bias small, poorly measured abundances; negative values are the obvious solution; refits with MEKAL, which allows them, found no significant bias for X-COP)); sanders2018 p. 21 (Appendix B, step (iv) (MBProj2 emissivities tabulated at 0 and 1 Z_sun and scaled to other metallicities)); molendi2016 p. 1 (Abstract (mean abundance of the ICM of massive clusters within r180: 0.06-0.26 Z_sun of Anders and Grevesse 1989)); molendi2016 p. 2 (Sect. 1 (iron abundances relative to Anders and Grevesse 1989 convert to the Lodders 2003 and Asplund et al. 2009 tables by factors of 1.59 and 1.48)); seppi2026 p. 5 (Sect. 4.1 (spectra of mock XMM-Newton observations made with xmm_simulator, fitted with the X-COP pipeline: temperature, normalisation and abundance free). arXiv v1; the same sentence is in the version of record (A&A 711, A213, p. 5)); bulbul2019 p. 4 (Sect. 2.2 (XMM-Newton spectra of SPT clusters within R500 and 0.15-1 R500: one absorbed APEC with free metallicity and temperature; metallicity fixed at 0.3 Z_sun for low-count observations))

Validation: Science choices that fix the V4 campaign – fixed 0.3 reads mock temperatures 2-5 % low and real ones 5-10 % high (TNG Fe 0.13-0.23, real clusters about 0.48 in the lodd table); the 0-2 range itself has no separate test

fit.priors_low_signal¶

Kind: literature

At every signal level the concentration has the same prior (the published relation of entry fit.cm_prior since 2026-11-12; before, flat in log c200 over 1-10 after Sanders et al. 2018) and the free metallicity the flat prior over 0-2 Z_sun. No prior depends on a count threshold. Until 2026-11-03 fits below 20 000 net counts took a log-normal c-M prior about Dutton and Maccio (2014) and a Gaussian Z prior 0.3 +- 0.1 Z_sun; the threshold had been chosen on mocks, so both are now diagnostics (fitfix cm_prior = dutton14, z_prior = gaussian); the Gaussian Z prior is set in no preset, and the concentration prior in its present form, at the sampled mass and at every signal level, is entry fit.cm_prior. A low-signal fit is therefore wider, and runs to the prior edges more often (QC flags prior_edge_c200, prior_edge_Z). The Z range is a bound, not a measured prior: the mean and average core abundances lie well inside it (fit.metallicity); single cores are not covered by these averages.

Value: cm_prior dutton14; metallicity_prior_mode flat; cm_prior_scatter_dex 0.11 and metallicity_gauss (0.3, 0.1) only for the diagnostic priors

Used by: massfit.cm_prior, massfit.cm_prior_scatter_dex, massfit.metallicity_prior_mode, massfit.metallicity_gauss

Evidence: sanders2018 p. 4 (Sect. 2.5.1 (flat prior 0 <= log10 c <= 1 on the NFW concentration)); sanders2018 p. 6 (Sect. 2.5.10 (the flat log prior on c, and a test widening it to 0.5-20 that changed no average result)); scheck2023 p. 4 (Sect. 3.4 (a wider flat prior, -2 <= log10 c <= 2, in the eROSITA mock analysis with MBProj2)); ghirardini2019 p. 7 (Sect. 2.4.2 (X-COP: abundance free in the XMM-Newton spectral fits)); dutton2014 p. 8 (Eq. 8 and the 0.11 dex scatter (the c200 start, fit.seed_lx; the dutton14 prior, fit.cm_prior))

Validation: No tuned factors: the last replacements (2026-11-03) – on the V4 closure the dutton14 / Gaussian-Z priors moved 11 of 13 low-signal fits toward the truth (median +0.05); that gain is given up; its size on the current configuration is measured by closure B1

fit.cm_prior¶

Kind: literature

The fit has a prior on the NFW concentration from a published concentration-mass relation (massfit.cm_prior = dutton14): ln c200 is normal about the relation of Dutton and Maccio (2014), with their scatter of 0.11 dex. The relation is log10 c200 = a + b log10(M200 / 1e12 h^-1 Msun), b = -0.101 + 0.026 z, a = 0.520 + (0.905 - 0.520) exp(-0.617 z^1.21). Mass and radius are those at 200 times the critical density. It was fitted to NFW concentrations of relaxed haloes, about 80 per cent of all haloes at z = 0, in N-body simulations with the Planck 2013 parameters (Omega_m = 0.3175, h = 0.671, sigma_8 = 0.8344). The scatter is the one the paper gives for those haloes at z = 0; the paper gives none at other redshifts. The prior is evaluated at every step of the sampler at the M200 of the sampled NFW halo, not at the first-guess mass, and in the same way for mocks and real observations. The mass enters in h^-1 Msun with the h of the pipeline cosmology. The halo of the fit is the dark matter; the gas adds its own gravity, so the halo’s M200 is below the total by the gas mass. The slope b is about -0.1, so a mass 15 per cent lower moves the centre of the prior by 0.006 dex, a twentieth of its width. With this prior the hard range of c200 is 0.5-20, the wider range Sanders et al. (2018) tested; at cluster masses both bounds are more than five standard deviations from the relation. Every preset uses this prior since 2026-11-12; the flat prior of Sanders et al. (2018) over 1-10 stays as the option cm_prior = flat. Before 2026-11-12 the option centred the prior on the first-guess mass and cut it at 1-10; no preset used it. How this differs from the reference analysis: Lovisari et al. (2020) fitted an NFW “with the relation from Bhattacharya et al. (2013) between the Dark Matter concentration c500 and R500 as a prior” and point to Schellenberger and Reiprich (2017) for the details. In that paper the concentration is not free: c200 = (M200 / 2.519e22 Msun)^-0.08 ties it to the mass, with no scatter and no redshift term, the NFW is fitted to the outermost three to five bins of the mass profile, and R500 is the only free parameter. The slope -0.08 is the z = 0 power law of Bhattacharya et al. for all haloes; the normalisation is not printed in Bhattacharya et al. Whether Lovisari et al. changed any of this is not stated in their paper. Here the concentration stays free within the published scatter, and the relation has a printed redshift dependence and a Planck cosmology. For M500 from 2e14 to 1.5e15 Msun and z from 0.05 to 0.6 the all-halo relation of Bhattacharya et al. (Table 2, Omega_m = 0.25, sigma_8 = 0.8) is 4 to 15 per cent (0.2 to 0.65 standard deviations of the prior) below the Dutton and Maccio relation, and the power law of Schellenberger and Reiprich is within 0.37 standard deviations of it (our evaluation of the printed formulae). Bhattacharya et al. find a wider scatter for all haloes, 0.16 dex against 0.12 dex for relaxed ones; a run with massfit.cm_prior_scatter_dex = 0.16 would test the width. No hydrostatic analysis we read uses a log-normal prior with the scatter of the simulations: Sanders et al. (2018) and Scheck et al. (2023) use flat priors, Eckert et al. (2022) a normal prior c200 = 4 +- 4 between 0 and 15 and note that a concentration-mass prior can be set for data of lower quality, Schellenberger and Reiprich fix the concentration on the relation. The weak-lensing masses of Hoekstra et al. (2015), one of the reference sets, are NFW fits with the concentration fixed on the same Dutton and Maccio relation.

Value: option dutton14 of massfit.cm_prior: ln c200 normal about ln c(M200, z) of Dutton & Maccio (2014), width cm_prior_scatter_dex = 0.11, c200 within 0.5-20; the presets have cm_prior = dutton14

Used by: massfit.cm_prior, massfit.cm_prior_scatter_dex, massfit.fit_hydrostatic

Evidence: dutton2014 p. 8 (Sect. 4.1, eqs. 7 and 8 (the z = 0 relation for relaxed haloes, 200 rho_crit definition, pivot 1e12 h^-1 Msun)); dutton2014 p. 9 (Sect. 5, eqs. 10 and 11 (redshift dependence of the slope and of the zero point)); dutton2014 p. 11 (Table 3 (NFW, Delta = 200: zero point and slope fitted at each redshift; the row at z = 0.5 is boxed)); dutton2014 p. 8 (Sect. 4.1, last paragraph (the distribution about the relation: log-normal, intrinsic scatter 0.11 dex)); dutton2014 p. 2 (Table 1 (cosmological parameters of the Planck simulations)); dutton2014 p. 5 (Sect. 2.3 (the selection of relaxed haloes and their share of the sample)); lovisari2020 p. 4 (Sect. 3.3 (NFW fit of the mass profile with the Bhattacharya et al. relation between c500 and R500 as a prior; details in Schellenberger & Reiprich 2017)); schellenberger2017 p. 14 (Sect. 4.1, ‘NFW Freeze’, eqs. 32-35 (c200 tied to M200 by a power law from Bhattacharya et al. 2013; R500 the only free parameter; the default masses)); bhattacharya2013 p. 8 (Table 2 (c200 against peak height: all haloes D^0.54 5.9 nu^-0.35, relaxed D^0.53 6.6 nu^-0.41, standard deviation 0.33 c) and Sect. 4.3 (log-normal fit: 0.16 dex for all haloes, 0.12 dex for relaxed ones)); bhattacharya2013 p. 7 (Sect. 4.1 (at z = 0 the relation is a power law of slope -0.08 for all haloes and -0.084 for relaxed ones)); hoekstra2015 p. 15 (Sect. 4.1 (NFW fits of the CCCP lensing signal with the Dutton & Maccio 2014 relation)); eckert2022 p. 5 (Sect. 3.2 and Table 1 (hydromass: normal prior on c200 with mean 4 and sigma 4 between 0 and 15; a mass-concentration prior named as the option for data of lower quality)); sanders2018 p. 6 (Sect. 2.5.10 (the flat prior on c widened to 0.5-20 as a test))

Code: src/tngxmm/massfit/cm_prior.py, src/tngxmm/massfit/fit_qc.py

fit.outer_pressure¶

Kind: validation

The pressure at the outer boundary of the model is a free parameter (flat in ln between -37 and -18); fits whose posterior median exceeds -30 are flagged as run-away. No paper read (Sanders et al. 2018, 2022; Scheck et al. 2023; Ettori et al. 2010, 2019; Eckert et al. 2022) freezes it at a floor. The backward method of Ettori et al. (2010) fixed it, but at the pressure measured at the outermost radius of the data, and the X-COP code of Eckert et al. (2022) fits it within a Gaussian prior on the measured SZ pressure; these fits have no measurement of the outer pressure.

Value: pout_logergpcm3_fixed = None

Used by: massfit.pout_logergpcm3_fixed

Evidence: sanders2018 p. 4 (Sect. 2.5.1 (wide, flat logarithmic priors on the outer pressure)); scheck2023 p. 4 (Sect. 3.4 (outer pressure parametrised logarithmically, 1e-16 to 1e-8 erg/cm^3)); sanders2018 p. 6 (Sect. 2.5.10 (flat logarithmic prior on the outer pressure)); sanders2018 p. 19 (Appendix B (MBProj2: the hydrostatic model is parametrised by the electron density, the dark-matter mass profile, the outer pressure and a metallicity profile; the pressure is summed inwards from P_out)); sanders2022_mbproj2d p. 17 (Sect. 2.12 (hydrostatic MBProj2 masses of A3266 from eROSITA: NFW mass model with flat priors in log space; the outer pressure parametrised by a log value with flat priors)); eckert2022 p. 5 (Sect. 3.2, Eq. 7 and Table 1 (hydromass: P(r) = P0 plus the integral of rho g from r to r0; P0 = P(r0), the integration constant at the outer boundary, fitted within a Gaussian prior on the outermost SZ pressure, truncated at 2 sigma)); ettori2010 p. 6 (Sect. 3, Method 1, Eq. 3 (the backward method: the boundary condition of the hydrostatic equation fixed to the pressure measured at the outermost point of the gas density profile, with the temperature there extrapolated if needed)); ettori2010 p. 9 (Sect. 4.2 (the Method 1 masses depend on the boundary condition; freeing the outer pressure over a grid of values from the best-fit density and temperature moves the mass by about 3 per cent, dataset ‘Pout’ of Table 4)); ettori2010 p. 19 (Table 4, notes (dataset ‘Pout’: the outer value of the pressure is not fixed)); eckert2022 p. 6 (Sect. 4.1 (mock clusters for the validation of hydromass: pressure profiles integrated from 5 R500 to avoid uncertainties from the integration constant P0))

Validation: V4 fit configuration – free outer pressure with the runaway flag (P_out > -30 in result.json), decision 1 of 2026-10-13; frozen at -37 changed a real cluster mass (MACS J1347) by 20 %

fit.optimiser¶

Kind: validation

Two optimiser stages (stage 2 up to 10 loops, backgrounds seeded from the r > 2 R500 annulus kept frozen in stage 1 only), then emcee on the whole stored (already post-burn) chain.

Value: bg_stage1 free-unseeded; stage2_maxloops 10; chain_burn stored

Used by: massfit.bg_stage1, massfit.stage2_maxloops, massfit.chain_burn

Evidence: emcee p. 6 (Sect. 3 (ensemble sampler, K walkers evolved together))

Validation: Fit-fix switches for the hydrostatic fitter – closure grid v4c diagnosis: all 216 z = 0.06 images had a constant ln SB = -15 background seed and the stage-1 start produced the run-away mode; the legacy double burn dropped 150 further steps

fit.background_seed¶

Kind: literature

The presets fit the background as XMM-ESAS and X-COP do (fit.particle_background_esas): the particle level is fixed from the observation’s corners and the sky and soft-proton levels are free with no prior, so no seed enters as a prior; their optimiser starts are half the image’s mean surface brightness after the fixed templates (the sky) and 5 per cent of that (the soft protons). The alternative mixture model (closure arm v1_mix) seeds each image from its r > 2 R500 annulus with a Gaussian prior of 0.05 dex, the order of the 10 per cent background scaling of Sanders et al. (2018), widened to fallback_bg_prior_dex where the seed did not come from a clean annulus. The typical EPIC level that seeded fits without an annulus (median of the closure grid’s background-only fits, a level calibrated on mocks) and the donor-table seed built on it were removed on 2026-11-03.

Value: qpb_model esas-fwc-corners: no background prior; mixture (diagnostic): bg_seed annulus, 0.05 dex prior, fallback_bg_prior_dex 0.3

Used by: massfit.bg_seed, massfit.fallback_bg_prior_dex

Evidence: sanders2018 p. 3 (Sect. 2.4 (background rescaled to the surface brightness at large radius))

Validation: No tuned factors: the last replacements (2026-11-03) – ESAS background in the presets; the mixture model’s 0.3 dex fallback width has no test of its own and enters no preset

fit.closure_clean_background¶

Kind: validation

In the closure grid the fit background of a clean cell (no sky, no particle background in the mock) is frozen at zero.

Value: closure_clean_bg_zero = true

Used by: massfit.closure_clean_bg_zero

Evidence: rasia2012 p. 6 (p. 6 (mock Chandra images without a background contribution; Rasia et al. 2006 showed the net effect of the background is to enlarge the error on the masses without introducing an extra bias)); barnes2021 p. 5 (p. 5 (Mock-X images assume perfect signal to noise; the lack of background reduces the scatter slightly but does not bias the results, after Rasia et al. 2008))

Validation: Closure grid: factorial data modes and SAS-masked fit arms – the factorial arms (clean, real, bgonly, agnonly) separate ‘no background in the data’ from ‘background frozen in the model’; real/clean closure ratio 0.916 at z = 0.1

fit.pixel¶

Kind: validation

The fit pixel is the preset’s 8 arcsec, coarsened where needed so that the first-guess R500 spans at least 40 pixels (v4: 8 arcsec down to z about 0.1), one rule for the CLI, the Kedro node, the real-fit plan and the closure grid.

Value: target_pixsize_arcsec 8; fit_pixels_per_r500 40

Used by: massfit.target_pixsize_arcsec, massfit.fit_pixels_per_r500

Evidence: ansarifard2020 p. 7 (p. 7 (surface-brightness profiles in 12 sectors, radial bins from 0.4 to 1.2 R500 linearly equi-spaced by 5 percent of R500))

Validation: Fit fix and real-fit plan merged – 8 arcsec in every G3/V3.2 fit job and in the V4 runs; coarsened to R500_guess/40 where larger (fit_hydrostatic.effective_pixsize)

fit.rebin_centre¶

Kind: convention

The cluster position on the rebinned fit grid is the centre of the f x f block that holds it, (o + 0.5) / f - 0.5, the convention of mbproj2d’s own Image.binUp.

Value: rebin_centre_convention block-centre

Used by: massfit.rebin_centre_convention

fit.crop¶

Kind: validation

The fit images cover 3 R500 about the first-guess R500 (clipped to the detector, no cap on the angular size).

Value: crop_r500 3.0; crop_cap_arcmin None

Used by: massfit.crop_r500, massfit.crop_cap_arcmin

Evidence: ghirardini2019 p. 4 (Sect. 2.2, p. 4 (sky background from the regions at R > 2 R500)); sanders2018 p. 3 (p. 3 (profiles truncated where the cluster is not distinguishable from the background))

Validation: Fit crop stays 3 R500 – crop 5 - crop 3 (paired median ln M, 95%): closure real +0.0015 [-0.005, +0.009], background-only -0.003 [-0.012, +0.008]; TNG views -0.009 [-0.026, +0.000] (J1c, job 1887630); recorded as the crop systematic

instrument.cluster_psf_onaxis¶

Kind: instrument

The cluster photons of the mocks are scattered with the King PSF of the CCF (KING_PARAMS) interpolated in the photon’s energy and the off-axis angle of its detector position (instrument.psf_model = ‘king-energy-angle’; energy rows whose slope is not above 1 dropped; header PSFMODEL), as a real cluster at the donor’s core offset is seen. Since 2026-11-04 the fit convolves the model with the same table at each pixel’s off-axis angle and the band energy (fit.psf, massfit.psf = ‘king-angle’), on both sides; until then it used the on-axis King, which cost a cluster that fills the field 4 per cent of its mass (STATUS B13). Until release 1.2 the generator used the fit’s own on-axis King, which made the mock fits PSF-exact; the paired closure arm of release 1.1.3 (v1_psfea, the same cells drawn with the energy- and angle-dependent PSF) moved the closure by +0.001 (-0.021 to +0.019) at the depth of those cells. The AGN get the off-axis ellbeta PSF (instrument.agn_psf_ellbeta). The closure arm v1_psfon keeps the on-axis generator as the reverse study.

Value: psf_model = 'king-energy-angle' (cluster photons); fit: the King of the same table at each pixel's off-axis angle (fit.psf)

Used by: instrument.psf_fix, instrument.psf_model, massfit.psf, sim.generate_views

Validation: Release 1.1.3, continued: the node work (2026-10-27) – v1_psfea - v1 paired closure difference +0.001 (-0.021 to +0.019)

Instrument document: XMM CCF XRT1/2/3_XPSF (KING_PARAMS: 10 energies x 8 off-axis angles 0-14 arcmin; ELLBETA_PARAMS)

Code: src/tngxmm/instrument/simulate_instrument.py, src/tngxmm/massfit/fit_psf.py

fit.psf¶

Kind: instrument

The model image of a band is convolved with the King PSF of the camera’s XPSF calibration (KING_PARAMS) at the off-axis angle of each emitting pixel: one kernel per tabulated angle (0-14 arcmin in steps of 2 arcmin; beyond 14 arcmin the last), each applied to the image times the pixel’s linear (hat) weight between the two nearest angles, so a photon gets the PSF of where it enters the mirrors, as the instrument stage draws it (instrument.psf_model = ‘king-energy-angle’). Energy: since 2026-11-05 each node is the sum of the King profiles at three energies of the band, each part of the band (a third in ln E) at its count-weighted mean energy and with its share of the counts (the image’s ARF times the bremsstrahlung spectrum at the virial temperature of the first-guess mass; fit_psf.band_energy_mixture), summed into one kernel; on Asimov data made with eight energies per band it returns M500 within 0.1 per cent, where the band’s sqrt(E_min E_max) (52d132c) gave +0.3 to +0.8 per cent. A mock made with the on-axis King (closure arm v1_psfon) is fitted with that on-axis King. Off axis the King slope falls (MOS1 at 6 keV: 1.37 on axis, 1.25 at 12 arcmin), so a larger share of the flux lies in the wings. With the on-axis King (the fit until 2026-11-04) the deep 8e14 Msun closure cell at z = 0.06, which fills the field, was fitted at 0.960 +- 0.016 of its mass with its counts within R500 matching the model at the truth; drawn with the on-axis King instead (the same photons) it was fitted at 0.994, and with this PSF in the fit the original cell gives 0.995 +- 0.018. Over the seven clean cells the mean of the fitted masses is 1.017 (0.996 with one shell per pixel and the on-axis King), every cell within 1.4 errors of the truth; the closure grid B1 measures the net effect. A model evaluation takes 2.6-3.4 times as long as with the on-axis King. Ellipticity, spokes and the Gaussian core of the ELLBETA description are not modelled (closure arm v1_psfell).

Value: psf = king-angle (KING_PARAMS at each pixel's off-axis angle, three count-weighted energies per band) | king-onaxis (until 2026-11-04)

Used by: massfit.psf

Evidence: read2011 p. 2 (Sect. 1 (the SAS 1-D descriptions of the EPIC PSF: the ‘Extended’ mode, a King profile as a function of EPIC instrument, energy and off-axis angle, stored in the XRTi_XPSF CCFs)); sarazin1988 (the thermal bremsstrahlung continuum of the intracluster gas (book, no page proof)); read2011 p. 4 (Sect. 2.1 (stacked source images in energy and off-axis-angle bands fitted with a 2-D King profile, beta2d)); croston2008 p. 2 (Sect. 2.2 (REXCESS surface-brightness profiles deprojected and PSF-deconvolved with the Ghizzardi parametrisation, weighted by the camera contribution)); chexmate_sb2023 p. 4 (Sect. 3.3.1 (CHEX-MATE surface-brightness profiles corrected for the PSF with the Ghizzardi model))

Validation: – deep 8e14 Msun cell at z = 0.06 (maximum likelihood, background zero, 8 model shells per pixel): M500 / truth 0.960 +- 0.016 with the on-axis King, 0.995 +- 0.018 with king-angle; the same photons drawn with the on-axis King and fitted with it 0.994 +- 0.017; Asimov with eight energies per band: 0.9991-1.0005 on four cells (one energy per band: 1.0031-1.0083)

Instrument document: XMM CCF XRT1/2/3_XPSF KING_PARAMS (r0 and alpha on 9-10 energies x 8 off-axis angles 0-14 arcmin)

Code: src/tngxmm/massfit/fit_psf.py, src/tngxmm/massfit/prepare_images.py

fit.model_shells¶

Kind: literature

The hydrostatic cluster of the fit is computed on spherical shells a fit pixel / massfit.model_shells_per_pixel wide (8), not one pixel wide. mbproj2d (commit ff497101, cluster.ClusterBase) builds the model of an image on shells one image pixel wide, each holding the density and temperature of its centre at constant density, as its predecessor MBProj2 does (Sanders et al. 2018, App. B), and paints the projected annuli onto the pixels by linear interpolation between annulus centres. A shell one pixel wide is a midpoint rule for the emission measure; at z = 0.5 the 8 arcsec pixel is 50 kpc against core radii of 134-169 kpc, and the model predicted 1.3-2 per cent more counts within R500 than the clean closure cells hold. With 8 shells per pixel the counts within R500 of the seven clean closure cells against the model at the truth are 0.991-1.007 (before 0.980-1.002), and doubling to 16 changes none by more than 0.03 per cent. The annuli are still painted at the pixel centres rather than integrated over each pixel: halving the fit pixel changes the counts within R500 by at most 0.02 per cent, but not the radial shape, and data integrated over the pixels to second order (f + Laplacian(f) / 24) are fitted 0.1-0.4 per cent high in M500 (Asimov, three cells; review of 52d132c). That term is left in the model and expected in the closure grid (STATUS B1); sub-pixel painting would remove it at the cost of a finer image. The images, the PSF and the likelihood stay on the fit pixel; the model evaluation takes 1.2-1.8 times as long (a finer fit pixel: 3-4 times at z = 0.5).

Value: model_shells_per_pixel = 8 | None (one shell per pixel, mbproj2d as released)

Used by: massfit.model_shells_per_pixel

Evidence: sanders2018 p. 19 (App. B (MBProj2 modelling): the bin pressures computed at the centres of the radial bins); sanders2018 p. 21 (App. B, steps (i) and (v): acceleration and pressure for shells of constant density; projection through the volume of each shell visible in each annulus)

Validation: – counts within R500 / model at the truth, seven clean closure cells, 1 -> 8 shells per pixel: 0.9960 -> 1.0001, 0.9963 -> 0.9987, 1.0021 -> 1.0067, 0.9977 -> 1.0001, 0.9998 -> 1.0024, 0.9798 -> 0.9913, 0.9872 -> 1.0066; 8 -> 16 within 0.0003; pixel-centre painting against pixel integration, Asimov M500 +0.0013 (8e14 z = 0.5), +0.0008 (8e14 z = 0.2), +0.0039 (4e14 z = 0.5)

Code: src/tngxmm/massfit/model_shells.py, src/tngxmm/massfit/band_response.py, src/tngxmm/massfit/fit_hydrostatic.py

fit.pixel_average¶

Kind: literature

Within 24 fit pixels of the cluster centre the painted model is the mean of the profile over each pixel (massfit.model_pixel_average = 8: an 8 x 8 grid of sub-pixels), taken before the PSF. mbproj2d (commit ff497101) reads the projected profile at each pixel’s centre. A detector pixel holds the integral of the surface brightness over its area, and for a profile that rises steeply towards the centre the value at the pixel’s centre is above the mean of the central pixels and below it in the ring around them, so the model of a cool core was too peaked on coarse pixels. Evaluated at the true parameters on the 54 clean closure cells, the pull on ln M500 of the cool-core cells fell from +8.0 to +1.4 per cent at z = 0.06 and from +2.9 to +0.1 per cent at z = 0.2, and the non-cool-core cells did not move. In the refits of the grid (job 1896730) the cool-core masses moved by -4.3, -1.2 and -7.4 per cent at z = 0.06, 0.2 and 0.5 (medians of nine clean cells each) and the non-cool-core masses by 0.0, -0.3 and +2.3 per cent. Outside the square the profile is flat across a pixel and is read at the centre as before. A model evaluation takes longer: the fits of the grid took 1.82 times as long.

Value: model_pixel_average = 8 | None (the profile at each pixel's centre, mbproj2d as released)

Used by: massfit.model_pixel_average

Evidence: peng2002 p. 3 (Sect. 3.1 (Azimuthal Shape and Pixel Sampling), eq. 4: the model value of a pixel is the integral of the profile over the pixel)

Validation: – 16 sub-pixels per side or a 48-pixel half-width change ln M500 by at most 0.21 per cent on three real closure cells; pull at the truth, cool-core clean cells: z = 0.06 +8.0 -> +1.4 per cent, z = 0.2 +2.9 -> +0.1 per cent

Code: src/tngxmm/massfit/model_shells.py, src/tngxmm/massfit/band_response.py, src/tngxmm/massfit/fit_hydrostatic.py

fit.band_channels¶

Kind: instrument

The rate tables of a band hold the RMF channels the stage images in that band: those whose energy, the centre of the channel’s EBOUNDS, lies in [lo, hi) (instrument.simulate_instrument.in_band). mbproj2d builds its tables after XSPEC’s ‘ignore **:**-lo,hi-**’, which keeps only the channels lying wholly inside the band: the 0.5-1 keV table of the canned MOS RMF ended at 0.995 keV and the pn tables lost both channels that straddle an edge, so the model lacked 1.2 (MOS) and 0.85 (pn) per cent of the 0.5-1 keV counts, 0.26 per cent of 1-2 keV and 0.09 per cent of 2-4.5 keV. The patch layer now notices the image’s channels by number after mbproj2d’s commands, and the rule enters the key of the rate cache. The PPS images select PI in (lo:hi] (PI in eV; the pn 4.5-12 keV image without (7800:8200]), which on 5 eV channels on multiples of 5 eV is the same channel set but for one PI value at each edge: -0.21 per cent of the pn 0.5-1 keV counts, +0.1 per cent of the pn 1-2 keV ones, at most 0.12 per cent of a MOS band (kT = 2-10 keV).

Value: band_channels = image-pi | None (XSPEC energy ignore, releases up to 1.2)

Used by: massfit.band_channels

Evidence: heasoft_xspec p. 138 (Sect. 5.4.8 (ignore), XSPEC 12.15.0 manual); heasoft_xspec p. 139 (Sect. 5.4.9 (notice), XSPEC 12.15.0 manual)

Instrument document: EBOUNDS of the fit’s RMFs; the evselect expression in the headers of the PPS band images (e.g. P0404910301M1S001IMAGE_2000: PI in (500:1000]); XSPEC 12.15 ignore and notice

Code: src/tngxmm/massfit/band_response.py, src/tngxmm/massfit/patch_apec.py

fit.vignetting_energy¶

Kind: instrument

The cluster model of a band carries the vignetting at the photons’ energies, before the PSF: model_b = [PSF * sum_j SB_bj V(theta, E_j)] x U. The E_j are the nodes of a piecewise-linear representation of the CCF vignetting in energy, within massfit.vignetting_tolerance of the table at every tabulated energy up to 10 keV and off-axis angle up to 15 arcmin (five times that from 10 to 12 keV, where a cluster puts 0.2-3 per cent of its 4.5-12 keV counts); SB_bj is the projected emission of node j’s rate table, the ARF weighted by the node’s hat function (the hats sum to the ARF); V is the CCF vignetting the stage’s effective area uses (instrument.effective_area); U is the unvignetted exposure, the 0.5-1 keV exposure map divided by the vignetting at 0.75 keV and carrying every mask and scale of the band’s own map. Mocks and real data take the same model, and no band’s exposure map enters it as the model exposure. Before, a band’s model was the on-axis rate times the band’s exposure map after the PSF. That map is vignetted at one energy of the band (SAS eexpmap: the mean of the PI boundaries, 8.25 keV for 4.5-12 keV, where a cluster’s counts sit at 5-7 keV; the PPS maps follow that rule, as do the stage’s), so the hard band was under-predicted by 6 per cent at 3 arcmin and up to 45 per cent at 12-15 arcmin, and the light the PSF scatters from the core was vignetted where it landed rather than where it entered the mirror. Asimov data (the fit model at truth times the ratio of the stage’s to the fit’s response per camera, band and off-axis angle, so that every spatial term cancels and only the response is tested) of six closure cells return Z = 0.293-0.305 for 0.3 and M500 within 0.7 per cent; with release 1.2 they returned Z = 0.35-0.66 and M500 -9 to +15 per cent. This is not an end-to-end closure: fits to the cells’ own counts (clean background, single Poisson realisations) are listed in STATUS B11.

Value: vignetting_model = sub-band; vignetting_tolerance = 0.01 | None (the band's exposure map after the PSF, releases up to 1.2)

Used by: massfit.vignetting_model, massfit.vignetting_tolerance

Evidence: xmm_uhb (Sect. 3.2.2.2 (Off-axis effective area) and Fig. 13, https://xmm-tools.cosmos.esa.int/external/xmm_user_support/documentation/uhb/effareaoffaxis.html); xmm_sas_eexpmap (eexpmap Description page (node3), https://xmm-tools.cosmos.esa.int/external/sas/current/doc/eexpmap/node3.html); xmm_cal_tn0018 p. 15 (Sect. 5.2 (EPIC-pn energy redistribution), p. 15 of Issue 3.14)

Instrument document: CCF XRTn_XAREAEF VIGNETTING (VIGNETTING_FACTOR on an energy grid and on off-axis angles to 15.6 arcmin); SAS eexpmap (a band map is vignetted at the mean of the band’s PI boundaries)

Code: src/tngxmm/massfit/band_response.py, src/tngxmm/massfit/prepare_images.py, src/tngxmm/massfit/fit_hydrostatic.py

fit.centre¶

Kind: literature

Each cluster is centred on its X-ray peak, the maximum of the 0.5-2 keV count image smoothed with a 16 arcsec Gaussian, by one procedure for mocks and real data (guess_mass.centre_and_seed). The search starts at a nominal position and covers max(first-guess theta500, r90) about it, r90 the 90 per cent radius of the catalogue position: the rule by which PSZ2 accepts an X-ray (MCXC) counterpart of a detection, a separation within the cluster’s size or within the Planck positional uncertainty. A real target’s nominal position is its cluster’s PSZ2 or ACT DR6 entry, with that entry’s published error (PSZ2 POS_ERR, a 90 per cent radius; ACT DR6 a Rayleigh distribution of sigma = 1.428 arcmin / q, from source injection); table catalogue_positions_v4.csv. A real catalogue position is an SZ detection: the peak of the sky map filtered with the catalogue’s matched filter, whose template is the universal pressure profile of Arnaud et al. (2010) convolved with the instrument beam, plus the detection’s measurement error. A mock’s nominal position is made the same way (sim.catalogue_centre): the Compton-y weight of its gas (electron count x T per cell) is binned on a map, the map is filtered with the template of the replicated cluster’s catalogue (ACT DR6: theta500 = 2.4 arcmin, the reference filter its positions are reported from, and the 1.4 arcmin beam of f150; PSZ2: the angular R500 of the entry’s mass MSZ and the 7.22 arcmin beam of 143 GHz; the profile integrated through 5 R500, the radius of Y5R500). The catalogue entry is the detection of the halo the mock is centred on: the search starts at the local maximum of the filtered map reached from the halo centre by steepest ascent, and each catalogue’s own rule follows. PSZ2 merges candidates separated by less than 10 arcmin and assigns a merged detection the position of the highest one, so its position is the reference of the start’s group (the maxima taken from the highest down, each taking those within 10 arcmin not yet taken; rule version 4), refined to a fraction of a pixel by a quadratic fit. ACT DR6 builds its objects and measures their positions on the pixels above S/N 3 of the reference map, and the ACT finder places an object at the centre of mass of its pixels above the threshold: the position is the S/N-weighted centre of the S/N > 3 segment that holds the start, the map scaled so that the halo’s own maximum has the entry’s S/N (fixed_SNR; DR6 does not say at which pixel it is read), on the map filtered with tau B / (C_l B^2 + N_w): DR6 forms its noise term from the maps, dominated by the CMB on large scales and white noise on small ones, and the model here is the lensed CMB TT spectrum of the Planck 2018 best fit (the Planck Legacy Archive spectrum to l = 2508, CAMB at the same parameters beyond, one packaged table) plus the median f150 depth of the DR6 maps, 14 uK arcmin, for a single band with a 1.4 arcmin beam. A subcluster inside the detection moves the position, as in a catalogue; an object outside it does not. A view is flagged when the highest filtered point lies outside the detection (global-elsewhere), when the detection’s highest maximum is not the one reached from the halo centre (merged), when the position lies outside the halo’s projected R500 (off-halo) and when the position moves by more than a tenth of the entry’s published sigma (or of the map pixel, when larger) as the map is cut to three quarters of the cylinder’s radius, a test made where the kernel reaches beyond the emitting cylinder (edge); the flags select nothing, and off-halo views stay in every comparison, which is also given without them; header SZCENX/SZCENY with SZRULE, SZTHETA, SZBEAM, SZSNR, SZNOISE, the rule version CATPOSV and the flag CPFLAG; obs_meta.json catalogue_position, with the share of the kernel beyond the cylinder, the shift of the truncation test and, for a merged ACT segment, the S/N of the saddle between the two maxima. A view made under another rule version, filter scale, beam, S/N, noise level or X-ray smoothing than its entry’s rule is refused at target build. That position is then moved by an error drawn from the published error of the entry of the cluster the mock replicates (its physics donor). The physical offset between the SZ peak and the X-ray peak (mergers) comes from the simulation, as on the real side it comes from the sky, and nothing measured on the real peak finder is copied. The published ACT error is noise only (the paper says so); the astrophysical part is the simulated SZ-to-peak offset. The PSZ2 filter assumes white noise (the matched filter is then the template itself), while PSZ2’s multi-filter also suppresses the large scales where the primary CMB dominates; the difference this makes to its positions was measured on TNG-Cluster halos and is stated, as are, for ACT DR6, one band for three, one depth for the whole survey and no foregrounds or 1/f noise in the noise model, one beam for each catalogue’s channels, PSZ2’s MMF3 positions on map pixels, which are not modelled again (the MMF3 POS_ERR was tested against injections, whose recovered MMF3 positions are on the pixels), and the 90 per cent reading of POS_ERR, which the catalogue’s column note gives as a 95 per cent interval (OPEN_ITEMS P). The image’s point sources (its SAS list, the one the photometry masks) are masked in the search except inside the catalogue’s error circle (at least the PSF half-energy width), where a cool core can be detected as point-like. Three MACS clusters in neither SZ catalogue use their own X-ray position with no published error (search radius theta500); those positions lie within 4.1 arcsec of the coordinates of Mantz et al. (2010b), who centre their clusters on the centroid of the 0.7-2.0 keV emission after masking point sources. Their mocks start at the peak of the 0.5-2 keV emission smoothed with the 16 arcsec Gaussian of the peak finder, reached from the halo centre (header XRCENX/XRCENY with XRSMOOTH), without a drawn error; the peak-to-centroid difference is stated in OPEN_ITEMS P. Each mock records its catalogue entry (physics donor, catalogue, r90, the position flag), the offset of its position from the true centre, the drawn catalogue error and the move of its centre (sz_offset_arcsec, catalogue_error_arcsec, move_arcsec of the targets table and of the closure records). validation.catalogue_moves compares the moves with the real catalogue-to-centre moves per catalogue (fractions beyond r90 and 2 r90, median in r90 units, bootstrap intervals over clusters) under a rule fixed before the regeneration: each test is on the difference mock minus real, its 95 per cent interval from a bootstrap of the real clusters and the mock donors together; ACT DR6 two-sided on the share beyond r90 and the median move, PSZ2 on the share beyond 2 r90, failing only on a mock excess, because the drawn PSZ2 error alone already fixes the share beyond r90 and the median (nominal false-failure rate 5 per cent per test). A failure adds a centring arm whose offset size is a declared design parameter and never rescales the drawn error. The centre is not a free parameter of the model.

Value: free_centre false; mock_centre xray-peak; real_centre xray-peak; search radius max(theta500, r90); mock nominal position: on the y map filtered with the catalogue's beam-convolved universal pressure profile (ACT DR6 2.4' / 1.4', PSZ2 theta500(MSZ) / 7.22'), the detection reached from the halo centre, PSZ2 its peak after the 10' merge, ACT DR6 the S/N-weighted centre of its S/N > 3 segment; plus a drawn catalogue error

Used by: massfit.free_centre, massfit.mock_centre, massfit.real_centre, photometry.recentre_observations, params:photometry.centre_method, sim.generate_views, validation.generate_closure_views

Evidence: sanders2018 p. 2 (Sect. 1 (the X-ray peak, small aperture, as the cluster centre)); psz2 p. 3 (Sect. 2.1 (each detection’s position uncertainty from its Gaussian position likelihood)); psz2 p. 19 (Sect. 7.2 and Fig. 23 (theta_err is a 90 per cent radius; an MCXC counterpart is kept unless its separation exceeds both theta500 and theta_err)); act_dr6 p. 6 (Sect. 2.4 (offsets of injected clusters follow a Rayleigh distribution with sigma = 1.428 arcmin / q)); psz2 p. 3 (Sect. 2.1 and eq. 5 (the matched multi-filter: the normalised profile of Arnaud et al. 2010 convolved with the Planck beams; the peaks of the filtered maps are the positions; Y5R500 integrated within 5 theta500)); psz2 p. 4 (Table 1 (mean FWHM of the Gaussian beams assumed for the extraction; 7.220 arcmin at 143 GHz)); psz2 p. 3 (Sect. 2.1 (candidates of the filtered maps separated by less than 10 arcmin are merged into one)); psz2 p. 15 (Sect. 6.2 (MMF3 positions are map pixels, 1.7 arcmin wide)); psz2 p. 33 (Catalogue column notes, note 3 (POS_ERR as the 95 per cent interval of the radial displacement)); act_dr6 p. 4 (Sect. 2.2.2 (a 3 sigma threshold for the object segmentation map and for measuring the object properties, position included)); act_dr6 p. 3 (Sect. 2.2 (the noise term of the DR6 matched filter is formed from the maps, which the CMB dominates on large scales and white noise on small scales)); naess2025 p. 15 (p. 15, the depth of the DR6 maps (the median depth inside the exposed area is 14/14/64/9.6 uK arcmin for day+night at f090/f150/f220/ftot)); act_dr5 p. 8 (Sect. 2.2 (the cluster coordinates are the centre of mass of the pixels above the S/N threshold of each detected object in the filtered map)); mantz2010b p. 3 (Sect. 2 (each cluster’s centre is the centroid of its 0.7-2.0 keV Chandra emission after masking point sources)); psz2 p. 4 (Sect. 3.1 (in a merge, the highest S/N detection is the reference position and the detections within reach are identified with it)); psz2 p. 32 (App. C.2 (the MMF3 positional error was corrected and tested against the quality-assessment injections)); psz2 p. 8 (Sect. 4.4 (positional recovery of injected clusters; MMF3 67 per cent bound 1.52 arcmin)); psz2 p. 32 (App. C.1 (MMF1 positions are sub-pixel in PSZ2, from posteriors marginalised over the other parameters)); melin2006 p. 10 (Sect. 4 (multiband surveys remove the primary CMB with their spectral information)); melin2006 p. 3 (Fig. 1 caption (the multi-band matched filter weights in space and frequency to extract the cluster from the CMB and instrument noise)); chexmate_sb2023 p. 3 (Sect. 3.1.1 (CHEX-MATE removes extended sources other than the cluster by eye before the X-ray peak is found)); act_dr6 p. 8 (Sect. 3.1 (for comparison with the SZ positional error: 95 per cent of cluster centres lie within 0.5 Mpc in the optical/IR miscentring model of Ding et al. 2025)); psz2 p. 9 (Sect. 4.5 (in merging clusters the position of the most bound particle can be significantly offset from the centre of the peak of the SZ distribution)); act_dr6 p. 2 (Sect. 2.1 (the f090, f150 and f220 maps have resolutions of 2.1, 1.4 and 1.0 arcmin)); act_dr6 p. 4 (Sect. 2.2.2 (the universal pressure profile convolved with the ACT beam is the filter template; positions are reported from the reference 2.4 arcmin filter scale map)); arnaud2010 p. 8 (Sect. 5, eq. 11 (the generalised NFW form of the universal pressure profile)); arnaud2010 p. 9 (Sect. 5, eq. 12 (c500, gamma, alpha and beta of the universal pressure profile))

Code: src/tngxmm/massfit/guess_mass.py, src/tngxmm/photometry/find_centre.py, src/tngxmm/photometry/catalogue_positions.py, src/tngxmm/sim/catalogue_centre.py, src/tngxmm/validation/catalogue_moves.py

fit.catalogue_redshift¶

Kind: literature

A real fit uses its cluster’s catalogue redshift; 64 of the 208 ACT DR6 targets have a photometric one (ACT DR6 types every redshift, spec or phot, and gives the uncertainty of the photometric ones). A mock is fitted at the redshift its catalogue entry would give it: z_fit = z_obs + delta, delta drawn from a Gaussian of the published error of the physics donor’s redshift when that redshift is photometric (redrawn until z_fit > 0), 0 for a spectroscopic one, from a stream keyed by the view’s id like the drawn position error. The catalogue lists redshiftErr only as the uncertainty of the redshift; reading it as a Gaussian standard deviation is an assumption, and the three WaZP values (0.030 each) look nominal (OPEN_ITEMS P). The seed, the centre search, the fit and the realism apertures use z_fit; the targets table and result.json record z_obs, z_offset and z_type beside it (the truth tables take a mock’s truth at z_obs), and closure cells take the same rule with their donor (the donors of the current closure plan have spectroscopic or untyped redshifts, so the gate cells are unchanged). PSZ2 gives one redshift per detection with its counterpart (zID) and publishes neither its type nor its error. Where the counterpart’s catalogue types its redshifts, the PSZ2 value is typed from that catalogue’s published entry (photometry.catalogue_positions.psz2_redshift, redshift rule 3, table column z_rule): spec when it is the counterpart’s spectroscopic redshift (within 5e-4), phot when it is its photometric one (within 5e-4, or for redMaPPer within the published z_lambda error, because PSZ2 took its redMaPPer redshifts from the authors’ proprietary v5.10 run), untyped otherwise. A phot row carries the counterpart’s own published error where the catalogue gives one (redMaPPer DR8 v5.10 Z_LAMBDA_ERR, 26 donors, read from NED) and the error the catalogue’s paper publishes where it does not (redMaPPer clusters absent from the public v5.10, 2: sigma_z 0.006 at z ~ 0.1, 0.02 at z ~ 0.5, the value at the nearer of the two quoted redshifts; WHL, 2: 0.018, the paper’s upper bound used as the standard deviation, so these two mocks probably get more scatter than the real redshifts have; MaxBCG, 1: 0.01). Of the 40 photometric counterparts 31 are phot, 5 spec (the WHL BCG redshift), 4 untyped (WHL counterparts whose PSZ2 redshift is neither the WHL photometric nor a spectroscopic value of the catalogue). The 17 SPT counterparts are typed from the SPT-SZ catalogue of Bleem et al. (2015), the one PSZ2 matched to, which quotes spectroscopic redshifts without an uncertainty and red-sequence redshifts with one: 13 spec, 4 untyped (PSZ2 gives another value), none photometric. The 3 ACT counterparts are typed from their ACT DR6 entry (the nearest within the 10 arcmin PSZ2 matched ACT clusters in): 1 spec, 2 untyped. The rest stays untyped because nothing published types it: the MCXC counterparts (270; MCXC takes each redshift over from its input catalogue without a type), the other X-ray and database counterparts (46; PSZ2 calls the NED values the least reliable of its assignments) and the 51 donors whose entry names no counterpart (values of the PSZ1 compilation and the Planck follow-up, with no record of the source). These, and the three MACS positions, get no drawn error, so the mock redshift errors remain a lower bound for them: they enter as exact-redshift donors, stated, and every realism and centring comparison is also given without the untyped donors on both sides (decided 2026-11-04). A table built under an earlier rule (no z_rule column, or rule 1 or 2) is refused where a mock redshift is drawn, and the table’s provenance must name the pinned PSZ2, ACT DR6 and counterpart files.

Value: z_fit = z_obs + N(0, z_err) (z_fit > 0) for a photometric redshift (ACT DR6 redshiftErr; a PSZ2 redshift typed phot from its counterpart: catalogue Z_LAMBDA_ERR, SPT-SZ e_z, ACT DR6 redshiftErr, or the catalogue paper's sigma_z); z_fit = z_obs for a spectroscopic or untyped one

Used by: massfit.fit_hydrostatic, validation.fit_closure_views
