Evidence: act_dr6 p. 18 (Appendix A, catalogue columns redshift, redshiftErr and redshiftType); psz2 p. 23 (Sect. 7.6 (one redshift per detection, from the highest-priority counterpart)); psz2 p. 20 (Sect. 7.3.1 (the redMaPPer counterparts: the authors’ proprietary v5.10 catalogue)); psz2 p. 24 (Sect. 7.6 (redshift priorities of the counterparts)); rykoff2016 p. 4 (Sect. 2.2 (versions of the SDSS DR8 catalogue: RM1 v5.2, RM4 v5.10, this paper v6.3)); rykoff2014 p. 35 (Table 1 (columns of the DR8 catalogue: Z_LAMBDA_ERR)); rykoff2014 p. 1 (Abstract (photometric-redshift scatter of the DR8 catalogue against spectroscopy)); wen2012 p. 4 (Sect. 2.4 (redshift uncertainty: z_phot - z_spec of the BCGs, Gaussian fits in four redshift ranges)); koester2007 p. 16 (Sect. 6.1 (tests of the photometric redshifts against BCG spectroscopy)); psz2 p. 22 (Sect. 7.5.1 (the SPT counterparts: the SPT-SZ catalogue of Bleem et al.)); bleem2015 p. 32 (Table 4 note (redshifts of the confirmed SPT-SZ clusters)); psz2 p. 23 (Sect. 7.5.2 (the ACT counterparts and the radius PSZ2 matched them in)); psz2 p. 21 (Sect. 7.3.2 (the database counterparts)); piffaretti2011 p. 5 (Sect. 3.1 (coordinates and redshifts of the MCXC))

Code: src/tngxmm/photometry/catalogue_positions.py, src/tngxmm/massfit/build_fit_targets.py, src/tngxmm/validation/closure_grid.py

instrument.vignetting_psf_calibration¶

Kind: literature

Since B11 the fit’s vignetting is the generator’s own function (instrument.effective_area.vignetting, the CCF XAREAEF table) and the fit convolves with the on-axis row of the King table the cluster photons are drawn from, so a mock fit meets no vignetting calibration error and no error of the King model itself, while a real cluster was vignetted by the true mirror response and blurred by the true PSF. Two pairs of closure arms bound the terms, each generated with the change and fitted with the nominal model (the exposure maps and templates stay nominal, as SAS makes them from the calibration). Vignetting: v1_vighi / v1_viglo multiply the effective area of every camera (cluster, sky and AGN photons) by g(theta) = 1 + 0.05 theta / 10 arcmin or 1 / g. The EPIC calibration status gives about 5 per cent as the residual flux difference of off-axis sources for each camera after the telescope-axis correction (for details the document refers to Lumb et al. 2004); it is not the vignetting curve measured at one angle, so a radially coherent g(theta) of that size at 10 arcmin is the conservative reading. The document gives no angle or energy dependence of it, so the linear form through it (1 on the axis, continued beyond 10 arcmin) and the same factor at every energy are declared choices (OPEN_ITEMS P). The same document reports larger MOS-to-pn flux differences off axis (up to 20 per cent at about 12 arcmin) and says the vignetting calibration is still being investigated; that relative term is not an arm. PSF: v1_psfell draws the cluster photons with the ELLBETA envelope of the same CCF (the SAS default 2-D PSF, Read et al. 2011, which the AGN are drawn with) at each photon’s energy and detector position, fitted with the on-axis King: the King model’s error against a second calibration of the same PSF, which v1_psfon (the King table’s own off-axis dependence) does not measure. The ELLBETA rows are least secure at high energy: Read et al. (2011) could not fit the two highest-energy stacks stably and combined them, and some CCF rows have a slope near 1 (pn 1.009 at 8 keV and 15 arcmin), so the arm is to be reported per band; no cap is put on the slope. The arms are paired with the base cells in the parent photon list (and, for v1_psfell, the PSF draw); the later draws are not. No arm is in a preset; the sizes are not measured (they need the closure node run).

Value: instrument.area_perturbation = None; instrument.psf_model = 'king-energy-angle' | closure arms: area_perturbation 'vignetting-tn18:+1' (v1_vighi), 'vignetting-tn18:-1' (v1_viglo), psf_model 'ellbeta' (v1_psfell), fitted with the v1 fit

Used by: instrument.area_perturbation, instrument.psf_model

Evidence: xmm_cal_tn0018 p. 13 (Sect. 4.5 (vignetting: G21.5-0.9 at about 10 arcmin over four azimuths; flux differences of off-axis sources with the current telescope axis)); xmm_cal_tn0018 p. 6 (Sect. 2.3 (ongoing calibration topics: MOS-to-pn flux ratios rising off axis, up to 20 per cent at about 12 arcmin)); read2011 p. 9 (Sect. 3.1 (the ELLBETA_PARAMS extension of the PSF CCF and its five parameters)); read2011 p. 4 (Sect. 2.1 (the 10.25 and 13.25 keV stacks: no stable 2-D fits, combined))

Code: src/tngxmm/instrument/area_perturbation.py, src/tngxmm/instrument/simulate_instrument.py, src/tngxmm/validation/closure_grid.py

fit.point_sources¶

Kind: validation

Point sources are masked in the fit with the ESA edetect_chain list (five bands, three cameras jointly; point-like) identically for mocks and real data, with the PSF-derived radius of the Lx photometry; mock sources below a per-donor flux threshold that reproduces the donor’s PPS point-source density are left in.

Value: source_list sas; source_masking true; source_mask_radius psf; mock_source_min_flux_cgs per-donor-density

Used by: massfit.source_list, massfit.source_masking, massfit.source_mask_radius, massfit.mock_source_min_flux_cgs

Evidence: webb2020 p. 3 (Sect. 2 (pipeline: simultaneous detection on bands 1-5 of the three cameras)); lovisari2020 p. 3 (Sect. 2 (point-like sources detected with edetect_chain and excluded)); ghirardini2019 p. 3 (Sect. 2.2.3 (point-source detection in two bands and exclusion))

Validation: V4 fit configuration – SAS edetect_chain list EXT == 0, one code path for mocks and real data; mask radius 30-150 arcsec from the King wing instead of a fixed 30 arcsec

fit.point_source_thresholds¶

Kind: convention (OPEN: no citation and no validation yet)

A source is masked when its detection likelihood DET_ML exceeds 10; sources within one PSF half-energy width (16.487 arcsec, entry photometry.lx_core_protection) of the centre are never masked: the telescope does not resolve them from the cluster peak.

Value: source_det_ml_min 10; protect_core_arcsec 16.487

Used by: massfit.source_det_ml_min, massfit.protect_core_arcsec

Evidence: webb2020 p. 11 (p. 11, sensitivity-map paragraph (EP_8_DET_ML = 6 is the detection criterion of the 4XMM catalogue)); webb2020 p. 13 (p. 13, before Sect. 11 (a clean catalogue, about 5 sigma, uses EP_8_DET_ML > 14)); ghirardini2019 p. 3 (Sect. 2.2.3 (X-COP masks only sources above the completeness count rate, a different rule)); chexmate_sb2023 p. 3 (Sect. 3.1.2 (CHEX-MATE masks point sources found by ewavdetect, S/N 5, above a threshold in the LogN-LogS distribution; fainter ones are left in on purpose)); snowden2008 p. 14 (Sect. 5 (cluster catalogue: bright point sources excluded to a flux level of a few 1e-13 erg/cm2/s, varied with the brightness and extent of the cluster)); pacaud2016 p. 18 (p. 18, completeness discussion (a sub-population of clusters with centrally peaked profiles, cool cores or central AGN contamination, can be classified as point-like by the XXL pipeline; at most about 1 per cent of the point-like sources above the flux limit are clusters)); seppi2022 p. 1 (Abstract (the cool core bias affects mostly faint clusters classified as point sources in the eROSITA digital twin)); nagai2007 p. 3 (Sect. 3.1 (clump detection by wavelets on a mock with realistic background; thresholds chosen to allow 3-4 false detections per image; detected clumps are masked, as routinely done by observers))

Instrument document: SAS emldetect: DET_ML = -ln P_random (10 corresponds to P_random = 4.5e-5)

fit.mock_agn¶

Kind: literature

Production (instrument.agn_lognlogs = ‘moretti03-soft’, entry instrument.agn_counts_band): the field AGN of the mocks are drawn from the SOFT-band (0.5-2 keV) integral counts of Moretti et al. (2003), and the fit masks the part above a per-donor flux threshold that reproduces the donor’s PPS point-source density; the threshold is solved with the soft-band counts (header AGNLNLS of pts_sources.fits). The hard-band (2-10 keV) parameters of the same paper (alpha1 = 1.57, alpha2 = 0.44, S0 = 4.5e-15 erg/s/cm^2, N = 5300 per deg^2), the set of the xmm_simulator, are used only under the stage option ‘moretti03-hard’, which no v1 preset selects.

Value: production: Moretti et al. 2003 soft-band counts (agn_lognlogs 'moretti03-soft'); the hard-band set alpha1 1.57, alpha2 0.44, S0 4.5e-15, N 5300 deg^-2 only under 'moretti03-hard'

Used by: massfit.mock_source_min_flux_cgs

Evidence: moretti2003 p. 12 (Eq. 2 and the 2-10 keV fit parameters); moretti2003 p. 11 (Sect. 7.1, soft 0.5-2 keV fit (alpha1 = 1.82, alpha2 = 0.60, S0 = 1.48e-14, N = 6150 per deg^2), the set that matches the band of the images)

instrument.agn_counts_band¶

Kind: literature

instrument.agn_lognlogs = ‘moretti03-soft’ (the InstrumentSpec default, the production value; the stage’s own default is ‘moretti03-hard’, the xmm_simulator draw of fit.mock_agn) draws the field AGN from Eq. 2 of Moretti et al. (2003) with the 0.5-2 keV best-fit parameters (alpha1 = 1.82, alpha2 = 0.60, S0 = 1.48e-14 erg/s/cm^2, N = 6150 per deg^2), the band of the images, between 1e-15 and 1e-12 erg/s/cm^2; S is the observed 0.5-2 keV energy flux of each source’s own model (PhAbs(N_H, z) x power law), integrated per source. Under instrument.agn_population = ‘ueda14’ (the production value since 2026-11-03) each source’s redshift, intrinsic log N_H and photon index are drawn from the AGN population of Ueda et al. (2014) at its observed soft flux: their Compton-thin hard XLF with luminosity-dependent density evolution, their absorption function including the Compton-thick AGN (f_CTK = 1), their photon index per class (1.94 +- 0.09 below log N_H = 22, 1.84 +- 0.15 above), on a (log L, z, log N_H) grid whose cells carry the observed 0.5-2 keV flux of the stage’s spectrum (point_sources.ueda14_population); Ueda’s reflection and scattered components are left out, as in the stage’s spectra. On that grid the population’s own soft counts are 1.02 and 0.99 times Moretti’s above 1e-15 and 3e-15, 0.74 and 0.54 above 1e-14 and 3e-14 (Moretti’s counts include the extended sources and stars there); its 2-10 keV counts are 0.91, 0.71, 0.58 times Moretti’s above 1e-14, 3e-14, 1e-13. Only the conditional distributions are taken from it; the numbers and fluxes stay Moretti’s. The drawn sources have median z 1.36 (90th percentile 2.6) and 30 per cent with log N_H > 22. Under ‘xmm_simulator’ (the earlier draw): Gamma ~ N(1.9, 0.2), z uniform 0-1.5, log N_H uniform in [20.5, 22]. Measured (2 deg^2 of the default generator): the hard draw, converted with the population’s own spectra, has 1.74, 1.39, 0.97, 0.82 and 0.55 times the Moretti soft counts above 1e-15, 3e-15, 1e-14, 2e-14 and 1e-13 erg/s/cm^2, i.e. too many sources near the XMM detection limit; on 60 donors and their count-matched replicas the counts per unit 0.5-2 keV flux of bright sources (1e-14 to 1e-13) agree with the donors’ PPS sources (mock/real 0.86 within 5 arcmin, 0.96 at 5-10 arcmin), so the excess is in the number counts, not in the flux-to-count conversion. The N_H cap is a choice (2 deg^2 of the soft generator): with it the soft-drawn population has 1.15 and 1.08 times the Moretti hard counts above 3e-14 and 1e-13 (with a cap of 23, 2.5 and 5.0 times), and lacks the obscured sources that are faint below 2 keV (0.5 times the hard counts at 3e-15). That N_H range (the cap at 22) was calibrated against the hard counts, a tuned number; it stays only in the ‘xmm_simulator’ draw, which no preset selects. Normalisation: instrument.agn_norm = ‘moretti03’ draws the published counts at their own normalisation, the same for every donor; a donor-dependent normalisation was tried and rejected (post hoc, no isolated effect) and has no option. Regime: the counts of the whole field population (point and extended sources, Moretti et al. Sect. 7.1) between 1e-15 and 1e-12 erg/s/cm^2; Galactic stars are not drawn separately.

Value: agn_lognlogs = moretti03-soft (production) | moretti03-hard (stage default); agn_norm = moretti03 (published counts, same for every donor); moretti03-soft: alpha1 1.82, alpha2 0.60, S0 1.48e-14, N 6150 deg^-2, S = 0.5-2 keV observed flux; agn_population = ueda14 (z, N_H, Gamma from Ueda et al. 2014 at the flux) | xmm_simulator

Used by: instrument.agn_lognlogs, instrument.agn_norm, instrument.agn_population, sim.generate_views, instrument.replay_views

Evidence: ueda2014 p. 32 (Table 4 (best-fit LDDE parameters of the hard XLF)); ueda2014 p. 11 (Eq. 14 (local XLF); Eqs. 15-19 the evolution); ueda2014 p. 7 (Eqs. 3-4 (absorbed fraction psi(L, z))); ueda2014 p. 32 (Tables 2-3 (absorption-function and photon-index parameters)); ueda2014 p. 10 (Eq. 10 (photon index per class)); moretti2003 p. 1 (Abstract (number counts in the 0.5-2 and 2-10 keV bands)); moretti2003 p. 11 (Eq. 2 and Sect. 7.1 (soft band fit, alpha1 = 1.82, alpha2 = 0.60, S0 = 1.48e-14, N = 6150)); carrera2007 p. 13 (Table 6 note e and Sect. 5.1 (stellar contribution to the soft and ultra-hard intensity of the AXIS sample)); moretti2003 p. 12 (Sect. 7.1 end (the fitted Log N-Log S gives the 0.5-2.0 keV integrated flux) and Sect. 7.2 (hard-band parameters of the default))

Code: src/tngxmm/instrument/point_sources.py

instrument.galactic_column_total¶

Kind: literature

instrument.galactic_nh = ‘willingale13’ (the InstrumentSpec default, the production value; the stage’s own default is ‘hi4pi’: the photons stay absorbed by the donor field’s HI4PI column, the 21-cm column of neutral atomic hydrogen, used as TBabs N_H) absorbs by the total column N_H,tot = N_HI + 2 N_H2, with the molecular column of Willingale et al. (2013, Eq. 7) from N_HI and the raw SFD E(B-V) of Schlegel et al. (1998) at the donor position (IRSA DUST service, reference pixel, column ebv_sfd of the table, not the Schlafly & Finkbeiner 2011 recalibration ebv_sandf, which is 0.86 times it; Willingale et al. use the SFD values; table instrument.galactic_column, 638 donors: N_H,tot / N_HI median 1.12, 90th percentile 1.35, maximum 2.05). The stored list, absorbed by N_H,view, is thinned with probability exp(-sigma(E) dN), dN = N_H,view (ratio - 1), which is exact; the sky background and the AGN take N_H,view x ratio. The fit uses the same total column (massfit.fit_galactic_nh = ‘willingale13’), for mocks and real clusters alike. Measured on count-matched replays against the donors (net band ratio 1-2 / 0.5-1 keV within R500, median paired mock - donor, 195 donors, round-3 instrument with mos_soft_ccd): by tercile of N_HI the deficit was -0.11 / -0.12 / -0.16 and becomes -0.09 / -0.07 / -0.08, so the column-dependent part of the spectral gap closes (the spectral features’ C2ST 0.628 / 0.580 -> 0.587 / 0.552). The remaining offset, independent of the column, scales with the replica’s temperature: the paired band-ratio difference grows as 0.46 ln(T_sl,mock / T_donor) (465 donors, T_donor the seed temperature of obs_seed_lx_v4; 17 sigma) and is -0.06 in the quartile where the two temperatures agree. Regime: the relation was calibrated on 493 Swift afterglows with the LAB N_HI and E(B-V) rebinned to 0.75 deg pixels (rms 0.087 dex in the fitted excess column); the donors use HI4PI N_HI and the E(B-V) at the donor position, which the paper does not test. Asymmetry: a mock is absorbed by exactly the column its fit assumes, a real cluster by its true column, which the relation only predicts. Willingale et al. publish no per-sight-line error of the prediction: their test compares groups of 13-29 afterglows, and the residual scatter between groups of 26 (0.087 dex) equals what the afterglows’ own intrinsic columns alone give (about 0.09 dex), so the group means show no error of the Galactic column but a single field’s is not bounded. A drawn column error would need a width nobody has measured. The closure arm v1_nhhi fits the cells with the atomic HI4PI column alone, dropping the whole molecular term (median 12 per cent of the column, 90th percentile 35): it measures the effect of a column too low by that term, one direction of one term, not the error of the HI4PI column itself (node run pending, STATUS).

Value: galactic_nh = willingale13 (production) | hi4pi (stage default); willingale13: N_H2 = 7.2e20 [1 - exp(-N_HI E(B-V) / 3.0e20)]^1.1, N_H,tot = N_HI + 2 N_H2, per donor; the view's jitter of N_H is kept

Used by: instrument.galactic_nh, massfit.fit_galactic_nh, sim.generate_views, instrument.replay_views

Evidence: willingale2013 p. 1 (Abstract (absorption needs N_Htot = N_HI + 2 N_H2; 21 cm gives the atomic part only)); willingale2013 p. 5 (Sect. 3.1 (setting N_Htot = N_HI from 21-cm surveys in tbabs is strictly an incorrect use)); willingale2013 p. 8 (Sect. 3.3, Eq. 7 and its constants (N_H2max = 7.2e20, N_c = 3.0e20, alpha = 1.1) with the SFD E(B-V) map); willingale2013 p. 8 (Sect. 3.3 and Table 1 (rms 0.087 dex of the fitted excess column between groups of 26 afterglows, against about 0.09 dex expected from their intrinsic columns)); wilms2000_tbabs p. 1 (Sect. 1, Eq. 2 (N_H of the absorption model is the total hydrogen column)); hi4pi p. 1 (Abstract (HI4PI is the neutral atomic hydrogen column))

Code: src/tngxmm/instrument/galactic_column.py, src/tngxmm/instrument/simulate_instrument.py

instrument.mos_soft_ccd¶

Kind: validation

instrument.mos_soft_ccd = ‘donor’ (the InstrumentSpec default, the production value; the stage’s own default is ‘off’: every MOS CCD has the camera’s flat particle level) gives the replica the donor’s MOS CCDs in the anomalous state, a soft excess on whole CCDs (instrument.donor_soft_ccd): per donor, MOS camera, CCD and PPS band the counts of the background region (beyond 1.5 R500, or the outer 15 per cent of the field) are compared with the camera’s vignetted plus flat model fitted on the other CCDs; a CCD whose 0.5-1 keV excess exceeds 5 Poisson sigma and whose 1-2 keV excess rate is below a quarter of it is anomalous, and its 0.2-0.5 and 0.5-1 keV excess rates (counts / s per 4 arcsec pixel over the donor’s on-axis live time) are added on the replica’s same CCD over its live time, with the shape of the particle spectrum within the band. Measured: the 0.5-1 keV background of 40 donors cut in 24 cells per MOS camera scatters 3.7 (MOS1) and 3.9 (MOS2) times the Poisson variance (median; 90th percentile 33 and 43) against 1.5 in the replicas, and about 2 in both above 1 keV; the departing cells are whole CCDs. Of 195 donors, 111 have at least one such CCD, and 134 of the 145 detections are MOS1-4, MOS1-5, MOS2-2 or MOS2-5, the four CCDs Kuntz & Snowden list. Only those four CCDs are given to the replica (the 11 other detections, possibly sky structure, are dropped). Regime: MOS only, and only these four CCDs, whose anomalous states Kuntz & Snowden (2008) list; the soft excess is detected on the donor’s PPS images (a soft-only excess, so a CCD with a genuine soft sky structure would be taken for anomalous) and added as a rate over the replica’s live time. The papers do not support the fraction of donors affected (measured here: 111 of 195), the rate model, or that the state of a given epoch persists.

Value: mos_soft_ccd = donor (production; only MOS1-4, MOS1-5, MOS2-2, MOS2-5) | off (stage default); donor: soft-only CCD excess (> 5 sigma in 0.5-1 keV, 1-2 keV rate < 0.25 of it) of the donor, as a rate, on the replica's same CCD in 0.2-0.5 and 0.5-1 keV

Used by: instrument.mos_soft_ccd, sim.generate_views, instrument.replay_views

Evidence: snowden2008 p. 14 (Sect. 3 (each observation’s 0.2-1.0 keV images examined for MOS CCDs in the anomalous state; such CCDs excluded)); snowden2008 p. 5 (Sect. 2 (CCDs 4 and 5 of MOS1 in their anomalous states)); kuntz2008 (Sect. 3.3 Anomalous States (NASA preprint NTRS 20070018227, pp. 9-11)); esas_cookbook (Cookbook v22.0, section on MOS CCDs in anomalous states (emanom))

Validation: Realism, release 1.1 – 195 donors, count-matched round-3 replays with galactic_nh = willingale13 and mos_soft_ccd = donor: 17 image statistics 0.771 / 0.812 -> 0.742 / 0.795 (logistic / boosting), background group 0.736 / 0.750 -> 0.722 / 0.716, PN/MOS background ratio paired difference +0.063 -> +0.030; MOS 0.5-1 keV background dispersion (24 cells, 40 donors) mock 1.30 / 1.50 -> 1.82 / 2.49 (MOS1 / MOS2) against 3.25 / 4.90 real

Code: src/tngxmm/instrument/donor_soft_ccd.py, src/tngxmm/instrument/simulate_instrument.py

instrument.donor_corner_qpb¶

Kind: validation

With instrument.qpb_level = ‘corners’ (the InstrumentSpec default since the integration of 2026-10-30; the stage’s own default ‘in-fov’ is allowed only with background ‘nominal’) and background ‘donor’, each PPS band’s QPB factor of every camera is the donor’s particle level in that band measured in the unexposed corners of its PPS event lists (out-of-FOV flag, the camera’s quality mask without it, PATTERN <= 12 MOS / <= 4 pn, within the PPS flare GTIs reconstructed from the flare time series) over the corner rate of the CCF filter-wheel-closed lists on the same CCDs in the same band (the MOS corners without the regions of Kuntz & Snowden 2008, Table 3: field-of-view edge, light leaks of CCDs 2 and 7, MOS1 scattered light; since 2026-11-04, FWC rule epoch-normal-2), divided by the stage’s conversion (its nominal QPB per area over the CCF FWC in-FOV rate, all CCDs: MOS1 0.667, MOS2 0.675, 0.64-0.68 per band; a ratio of two calibration data sets, not a tuned number). The in-FOV / corner ratio of the QPB is a detector property, so the FWC spectrum is scaled band by band with the corner data, as Snowden et al. (2008) scale it spectral bin by spectral bin and XMM-ESAS mosback / pnback do. The pn corner counts have the out-of-time events of the in-FOV X-rays subtracted column by column. The table is tables/donor_qpb_corners.csv (638 observations, job 1890054); a camera without a usable corner level (11 pn lists: the Large and Small Window exposures, whose corners are not read out, and lists with too few corner counts; 5 MOS1 and 3 MOS2 lists) takes the FWC level of the CCF, header QPBLEVEL = fwc. Until release 1.1.3 the pn kept the in-FOV image-decomposition level of tables/donor_background.csv, which was divided by a replay calibration (pn 0.947 in 4.5-12 keV) and shrunk between bands with hand-chosen priors; both are removed. Known consequence: the pn background below 4.5 keV no longer carries the flat share of the residual soft protons that the in-FOV level held (release 1.1 measured the replicas’ pn 1-4.5 keV background 0.13-0.16 dex below the donors’ with the corner level); no calibrated pn soft-proton level exists to add.

Value: qpb_level corners (production, every camera): QPB scale of band b = (corner rate_b / FWC corner rate_b) / conv_b; FWC level without a corner level | in-fov (stage default, nominal background only)

Used by: instrument.qpb_level, sim.generate_views, instrument.replay_views

Evidence: deluca2004 p. 4 (Sect. 3 (in-FOV and out-of-FOV events separated by the out-of-FOV flag)); deluca2004 p. 4 (Sect. 3.1 (a region of MOS CCDs 2 and 7 removed from the out-of-FOV events)); kuntz2008 p. 14 (Table 3 (Corner pixel selection criteria; NASA preprint NTRS 20070018227, p. 14): the ‘!corner’ expression of each MOS camera); deluca2004 p. 5 (Sect. 3 (the in-FOV / out-of-FOV 8-12 keV surface brightness ratio)); deluca2004 p. 16 (Fig. A.6 caption (the shape of the quiescent particle spectrum is stable)); leccardi2008 p. 18 (App. B, Eq. B.1 (the ratio of the NXB normalisations of two detector regions does not depend on the observation)); snowden2008 p. 6 (Sect. 3.1.1 (the FWC spectra are scaled to each observation with the corner data))

Validation: Realism, release 1.1 – 195 donors (PPS event lists, HEASARC): corner scale of the 2.5-12 keV MOS / 5-12 keV pn selection median 1.20 / 1.19 / 1.26 (MOS1 / MOS2 / pn), MOS1/MOS2 1.02, pn/MOS2 1.14, pn OoT share of corner counts 0.9 per cent (max 4.4); corner QPB over the in-FOV donor QPB 0.82 / 1.04 / 1.35 (the in-FOV decomposition’s MOS1 / MOS2 flat ratio is 1.15-1.39 against 1.02 here). The band-by-band form together with the soft-proton component is evaluated in instrument.background_three_components.

Code: src/tngxmm/instrument/donor_corner_qpb.py, src/tngxmm/instrument/simulate_instrument.py

instrument.background_three_components¶

Kind: validation

The in-FOV background of a donor as three measured components, each from data other than the C2ST features. (1) QPB: qpb_level = ‘corners-band’ (the production value, instrument.donor_corner_qpb), the corner rate of each PPS band over the CCF FWC corner rate, within the PPS flare GTIs reconstructed from the flare time series (the quietest bins summing to the image ONTIME; matches the image screened time to < 1 per cent for 97-100 per cent of the cameras), converted to the stage band by band (MOS 0.64-0.68, pn 0.77-0.81). It carries the soft particle excess the FWC shape lacks: corner 0.5-1 keV / 4.5-12 keV scales relative to the FWC are 0.97 (MOS1), 0.87 (MOS2) and 1.26 (pn). (2) Residual soft protons: soft_protons = ‘inout’ (the production value, instrument.donor_inout_sp; the stage’s own default is ‘off’), the 10-12 keV in-FOV excess over the corner QPB and the CXB beyond the cluster (De Luca & Molendi IN/OUT; R median 1.30 / 1.29 / 1.32), through the Leccardi & Molendi QSP spectrum, flat over the FOV. MOS1 and MOS2 agree (excess ratio 0.98, 16-84 per cent 0.66-1.40; median difference -0.16 sigma). (3) The sum is checked against each donor’s own 4.5-12 keV counts in the same region: predicted / observed 1.015 (MOS1) and 1.023 (MOS2), with QPB 75 per cent, SP 26 per cent and CXB 3 per cent; for the pn it is 1.25 (the corner QPB alone gives 0.97), so the pn IN/OUT excess does not carry the MOS QSP spectrum; and with the pn corner QPB the replicas’ pn 1-4.5 keV background is 0.13-0.16 dex below the donors’ (an unmeasured pn in-FOV component). The pn was at first left at the in-FOV donor factors and the three-component model used for the MOS only; since the integration of 2026-10-30 every camera, the pn included, carries all three components: the pn QPB at its corner level, the pn soft protons as the IN/OUT excess, a lower bound (entry instrument.pn_soft_protons_inout). MOS1 reproduces the donors in every band within 0.04 dex; MOS2 is 0.06 dex high in 2-12 keV (the flat SP template over-predicts the outer field), not tuned away.

Value: qpb_level = corners-band + qpb_corner_table, soft_protons = inout + soft_proton_table, all three cameras (data-package tables donor_qpb_corners.csv and donor_mos_background.csv)

Used by: instrument.soft_protons, sim.generate_views, instrument.replay_views

Evidence: deluca2004 p. 1 (Abstract (CXB photon index 1.41 and about 11.6 photons cm^-2 s^-1 sr^-1 keV^-1 at 1 keV)); deluca2004 p. 5 (Sect. 3 (the in-FOV / out-of-FOV surface brightness ratio flags residual soft protons)); leccardi2008 p. 18 (App. B (quiescent soft-proton broken power law, slopes 0.4 and 0.8, break 5 keV; MOS data)); snowden2008 p. 6 (Sect. 3.1.1 (the FWC spectra are scaled to an observation with its corner spectra)); kuntz2008 (Sect. 6 (NASA preprint NTRS 20070018227, p. 27))

Validation: Realism, release 1.1 – 195 outskirts views, same photons, factors and seeds, agn_norm moretti03 in both: statistics 0.755 / 0.824 -> 0.726 / 0.834 (logistic paired -0.029 +- 0.015, donor bootstrap), background group 0.704 / 0.716 -> 0.683 / 0.721, DINO PCA-32 0.916 -> 0.926 (+0.009 +- 0.010); large-field (theta500 >= 5 arcmin) log_bkg_rate deficit -0.079 +- 0.010 -> -0.030 +- 0.009; with the pn also on corners and IN/OUT SP (q3C) statistics 0.839 / 0.907

Code: src/tngxmm/instrument/donor_corner_qpb.py, src/tngxmm/instrument/donor_inout_sp.py, src/tngxmm/instrument/simulate_instrument.py

instrument.pn_soft_protons_inout¶

Kind: literature

With soft_protons = ‘inout’ the pn gets residual soft protons in the same way as the MOS. The donor table (donor_tables.mos_background, cameras MOS1, MOS2 and PN) holds the De Luca & Molendi IN/OUT excess of the pn, measured with the selection, region and 10-12 keV band of the MOS: the in-FOV rate beyond max(1.5 R500, 3’) of the cluster and within 12’ of the pointing, minus the corner-scaled FWC in-FOV rate and the CXB. The band lies above the Cu K-alpha gap (7.8-8.2 keV) of the PPS pn hard band, so the pn fluorescence lines do not enter it. The stage converts the excess with the quiescent soft-proton spectrum of Leccardi & Molendi (2008) and spreads it flat over the field of view (instrument.background_three_components). The pn value is an UNVALIDATED LOWER BOUND. It is a lower bound because the pn corners are not shielded from the sky: they receive focused soft protons and photons, most near the edge of the field of view (Marelli et al. 2021), so the corner scale overestimates the pn particle level, the subtracted in-FOV particle rate is too high and the excess too low, the more so the stronger the soft protons. It is unvalidated because no source calibrates a pn IN/OUT excess against a soft-proton level (the method papers use MOS data only) and no measured pn quiescent soft-proton spectrum exists, so the MOS spectrum is used for the pn. The data package has carried the pn columns since 2026-11-03 (donor_mos_background.csv: the 638 donors measured on 2026-10-30, sha256 4c7c0118, then measured again about the final guarded centres on 2026-11-03, sha256 25b3c8ba, IN/OUT columns of 72 donors changed). A table without them, such as the MOS-only one measured before the pn was added, is refused when the pn is simulated; it used to leave the pn without soft protons. What has been measured (one replay with the table of 2026-10-30, not a validation): on 199 TNG views replayed with and without the pn rate (job 1891399; 638 donors measured, 511 with a finite pn rate), the median log10 of the pn background beyond 8’ over the donor’s went from -0.095 to +0.003 dex in 4.5-12 keV, from -0.079 to +0.011 dex in 1-2 keV and from -0.021 to +0.025 dex in 0.5-1 keV; the soft-band background ratio pn / (MOS1 + MOS2) went from 1.05 to 1.18 against 1.32 in the real data, and the background hardness from 1.63 to 1.72 against 1.68. The pn ratio staying below the real one fits a lower bound; the hardness overshoot says the MOS spectrum is somewhat too hard for the pn.

Value: soft_protons = inout; params donor_tables.mos_background.cameras = [MOS1, MOS2, PN]; pn columns sp_rate_pn etc. of the donor table (a lower bound); no pn columns: refused when the pn is simulated

Used by: instrument.soft_protons, params:donor_tables.mos_background.cameras

Evidence: marelli2021 p. 1 (Abstract (the so-called out-of-field-of-view region of the pn is exposed to the sky)); marelli2021 p. 3 (Sect. 2 (the whole pn outFOV area receives photons and soft protons, most in its inner part; unlike the MOS)); marelli2021 p. 12 (Sect. 7.1, Proxies of the pn background (using the pn corner data for the in-FOV unconcentrated background)); deluca2004 p. 5 (Sect. 3 (the in-FOV / out-of-FOV surface brightness ratio flags residual soft protons)); deluca2004 p. 2 (Sect. 1 (the method is developed on the MOS)); leccardi2008 p. 19 (App. B (a precise characterisation of the quiescent soft-proton component of the pn is not possible))

Code: src/tngxmm/donor_tables/mos_background.py, src/tngxmm/instrument/donor_inout_sp.py

instrument.sp_rate_unmeasured¶

Kind: validation

A camera whose donor has no IN/OUT soft-proton rate takes the measured rate of the nearest donor of the conditions pool, in the same donor fold, that has one for that camera (distance in the condition space of the conditions donor, ties by obsid), and the view records the donor that lent it (SPRFLAG and SPSUBST in the camera headers, soft_protons in obs_meta.json and in the conditions record). The rate is missing where 1.5 R500 of a nearby cluster covers the 12’ circle about the pointing (112 of the 588 pool donors, all three cameras) or the pn has no usable corner level (4 more, pn only). Such an observation had soft protons like any other: 98.7 to 99.2 per cent of the measured rates are positive, so a rate of 0 is not a measurement but the lowest possible value, and it would fall on the nearby clusters (no rate for 90 per cent of the pool donors at z < 0.1, 1 per cent at z >= 0.2). The rate is a property of the observation (its orbit phase and flare screening), not of the cluster: the lent rates have the median of the measured ones. No published method transfers an IN/OUT rate between observations; the rule is ours. The alternative ‘refuse’ stops such a view.

Value: sp_rate_unmeasured = nearest-in-fold (alternative: refuse); never 0 for want of a measurement

Used by: instrument.sp_rate_unmeasured

Validation: – 116 of the 588 pool donors take a substitute (112 in all three cameras, 4 pn only); substitute distance median 2.63 (10-90th 1.72-3.93, max 5.68) against 3.34 for the same donors’ fifth condition neighbour; lent rates median 7.05e-4 / 6.86e-4 / 1.81e-3 counts/s/arcmin2 (MOS1 / MOS2 / pn) against 6.54e-4 / 6.49e-4 / 1.82e-3 measured; 458 of the 2835 replica views and about 1850 of the 9584 maximal-list views had a rate of 0 before

Code: src/tngxmm/instrument/donor_inout_sp.py, src/tngxmm/sim/generate_view.py

instrument.soft_proton_spatial¶

Kind: instrument (OPEN: no citation and no validation yet)

Spatial form of the residual soft protons that soft_protons = ‘inout’ adds. The presets (soft_proton_map = ‘flare’, since 2026-11-03; flat before) draw them on the soft-proton flare maps of the CCF XMM_FLARE (the maps the SAS task proton co-adds; built by Kuntz & Snowden 2008, Sect. 4.3) for the camera’s filter (Thin2 takes the Medium map) and the event’s energy band (0.3-0.75, 0.75-1.25, 1.25-2, 2-4, 4-8, 8-12 keV in the order of the rows B1-B6), each map normalised to mean 1 over the pixels in which the donor’s IN/OUT excess is measured: within 12 arcmin of the pointing (donor_tables.mos_background.MAX_OFFAXIS_ARCMIN) and beyond max(1.5 theta500, 3 arcmin) of the donor’s cluster (the region excluded from that measurement, at the donor’s position on the detector), so the mock carries the measured rate in that region. The MOS flare maps fall by about 15 per cent from 4-6’ to 10-12’ (0.75-1.25 keV), against about 35 per cent for the photons over the realism ring radii; the flat form puts too many soft protons into the outer field. The CCF maps are images of stacked flare events with a pixel-to-pixel scatter of about 75 per cent; they are smoothed with the 1’ kernel of the FWC densities before use, by normalised convolution over the pixels with data. The maps are flare counts minus the scaled non-flare counts, so negative pixels are the noise of that difference (55 per cent of the pn pixels in the field, Medium, 0.75-1.25 keV) and enter the mean; a pixel exactly 0 has no data (outside the field, or a chip gap or bad column of the stacked exposures) and inside the field takes the smoothed value of its neighbourhood; the result is clipped at 0. Before 2026-11-05 only the positive pixels were averaged and every other pixel stayed 0: the template was a speckle (65 per cent of the pn pixels in the field empty), biased upwards where the signal is weak, and the dead columns shared by the maps and the observation were removed twice, once by the map and once by the mock’s efficiency map or the fit’s exposure map (soft-proton counts on partly exposed pixels 0.24-0.73 of the exposure map’s prediction in a soft-proton-bright view; 0.95-1.13 after, as in the real data). The detector maps are placed on the mask grid by SAS’s own DET -> sky solution of the observation behind the masks (donor_background.DET_TO_MASK), as the FWC densities.

Value: soft_proton_map 'flare' (production: XMM_FLARE per filter and band, smoothed 1') | 'spdetmap' (XMM_SPDETMAP, one map) | None (flat)

Used by: instrument.soft_proton_map, instrument.soft_protons, instrument.replay_views

Evidence: kuntz2008 (Sect. 4.3 and Figs. 17-18 (NASA preprint NTRS 20070018227, pp. 18-19)); esas_proton (Sect. 8 (Algorithm) and Sect. 9 (Comments), pp. 3-4 of the SAS 22.0 task document); deluca2004 p. 5 (Sect. 3 (the in-FOV excess the level comes from))

Instrument document: CCF XMM_FLARE_0001 (soft-proton flare maps per camera, filter and band; the maps of the SAS task proton) and XMM_SPDETMAP_0001

Code: src/tngxmm/instrument/donor_background.py, src/tngxmm/instrument/simulate_instrument.py

instrument.agn_galactic_nh¶

Kind: literature

instrument.agn_galactic_nh = ‘view’ (the InstrumentSpec default, the production value; the stage’s own default is ‘off’, the xmm_simulator model: the AGN spectra carry only their intrinsic absorption, while the cluster photons and the sky background are absorbed by the view’s Galactic column) also absorbs the AGN photon spectra by the view’s Galactic N_H (PhAbs at z = 0), after the flux normalisation, so the drawn flux stays the flux before Galactic absorption, the convention of the number counts. At the median N_H of the 195 donors (2.8e20 cm^-2) this removes 8 per cent of the 0.5-2 keV energy flux of a Gamma = 1.9 source, 17 per cent at the 90th percentile (6.4e20 cm^-2). Regime: the number counts used are fluxes before Galactic absorption (Mateos et al. correct their XMM-Newton fluxes for the Galactic absorption along each line of sight); the drawn flux is the intrinsic one for any donor column.

Value: agn_galactic_nh = view (production: PhAbs(N_H of the view, z = 0) on every AGN spectrum) | off (stage default)

Used by: instrument.agn_galactic_nh, sim.generate_views, instrument.replay_views

Evidence: mateos2008 p. 5 (Sect. 2.4 (energy conversion factors between count rates and fluxes, computed for each observation with its Galactic column)); moretti2003 p. 10 (Sect. 6 (conversion factors recomputed per source with its power-law index at the appropriate column density))

Code: src/tngxmm/instrument/point_sources.py, src/tngxmm/instrument/simulate_instrument.py

instrument.agn_psf_ellbeta¶

Kind: literature

instrument.agn_psf = ‘ellbeta’ (the InstrumentSpec default, the production value; the stage’s own default is ‘king-onaxis’: the AGN photons get the on-axis King profile of KING_PARAMS row 0, as the cluster) scatters each AGN photon with the SAS default 2-D PSF envelope of the camera’s XRT*_XPSF CCF (ELLBETA_PARAMS: King core radius and slope, ellipticity, Gaussian core FWHM and peak ratio) at the nearest tabulated energy to the photon’s and the source’s off-axis angle (linear in theta), major axis tangential; the spokes and the large-scale azimuthal modulation are not drawn. The cluster keeps the on-axis King profile (fit.psf), as does the fit, so the off-axis broadening of a cluster that sits at the donor’s core offset is not represented (it is a point-source PSF switch). Measured on the 195 donors and their count-matched replicas: the central concentration of compact sources (net counts within 8 arcsec over within 24 arcsec) falls off-axis in the real fields (0.75, 0.66, 0.62 at 0-6, 6-10, >10 arcmin) and stays flat in the mocks (0.72, 0.70, 0.70).

Value: agn_psf = ellbeta (production) | king-onaxis (stage default); ellbeta: CCF ELLBETA_PARAMS envelope, energy nearest, theta linear, tangential major axis, drawn inside the 15 arcmin NORM_AT radius

Used by: instrument.agn_psf, sim.generate_views, instrument.replay_views

Evidence: read2011 p. 2 (Sect. 2 (the off-axis PSF is tangentially stretched, due to the Wolter I optic)); read2011 p. 5 (Sect. 2.1 (trends of the fitted envelope parameters with energy and off-axis angle)); read2011 p. 9 (Sect. 3.1 (the ELLBETA_PARAMS extension of the PSF CCF and its five parameters))

Code: src/tngxmm/instrument/point_sources.py

fit.optical_axis¶

Kind: validation

The vignetting-free exposure map of the fit is computed about the true optical axis (OPTAXX/OPTAXY header, fallback CRPIX), for mocks and real data.

Value: optical_axis = header

Used by: massfit.optical_axis

Evidence: rosen2016 p. 10 (Sect. 6 (matching two observations of the same field: different boresight positions give sources different vignetting factors)); pacaud2016 p. 3 (Figure caption on p. 3 (cluster detection in a 13 arcmin radius around the average optical axis of each pointing)); xmm_uhb (Sect. 3.3 (EPIC), detector layout and Fig. 19 caption, https://xmm-tools.cosmos.esa.int/external/xmm_user_support/documentation/uhb/epic.html)

Validation: – bias audit section 5 item 8: expmap_novig about the true optical axis for mocks; store the axis pixel in the header

fit.seed_lx¶

Kind: literature

The first guess of every fit (its crop radius, pixel size, model extent, centre search radius and optimiser start) comes from the target’s own image through a published observed relation, the same way for mocks, closure cells and real observations: the core-included 0.5-2 keV luminosity inside R500 against M500 of Bulbul et al. (2019), XMM-Newton data of SPT clusters, in the band and aperture of our photometry, iterated as for the MCXC (Piffaretti et al. 2011): R500 from the mass, the luminosity measured inside it with the campaign estimator (masked point sources, local clipped background, ECF at the virial temperature of the trial mass), the mass from the inverted relation, until it changes by less than 0.1 per cent. The iteration keeps the bracket of the root that the signs of the mass change establish and bisects it when a step would leave it (a noisy or clumpy image). An unmeasurable trial bounds the search: before any trial was measurable the mass steps down a decade (its R500 shrinks into the field; most nearby clusters start there), after one the search bisects back towards the last measurable trial. A root beyond what the image can measure keeps the largest measurable mass (seed_status = field-edge); an image with no measurable trial keeps the pivot (pivot). An aperture that leaves the field is ring-filled from its covered part, the rule that already replaces masked pixels, up to the same limit of half the aperture (both sides; the release 1.2 real table had 62 / 638 R500 apertures uncovered, 30 unconverged and 45 pivots stopped at the first trial). The status counts of both populations are recorded in the provenance of the real seed table and of every mock targets table. The c200 start is the Dutton and Maccio (2014) median at that mass; its prior is flat (fit.priors_low_signal). Neither the true nor the published mass enters. Until 2026-11-03 the guess came from relations fitted on the TNG mocks themselves.

Value: seed_lx_photometry = campaign; Bulbul et al. 2019 L = 4.12e44 (M/6.35e14)^1.89 (E/E(0.45))^2 ((1+z)/1.45)^-0.20, iterated to \|d ln M\| < 1e-3; pivot 6.35e14 when unmeasurable; c200 start Dutton & Maccio 2014

Used by: massfit.seed_lx_photometry

Evidence: bulbul2019 p. 22 (Sect. 7 (the core-included 0.5-2 keV luminosity-mass-redshift relation, normalisation 4.12e44 erg/s)); bulbul2019 p. 9 (Sect. 5 (pivots of the scaling relations)); bulbul2019 p. 8 (Sect. 4 (the core-included aperture r < R500)); piffaretti2011 p. 7 (Sect. 3.4 (L500 and R500 iterated through an L-M relation))

Validation: No tuned factors: the last replacements (2026-11-03) – the iteration’s contraction factor is about (1/1.89)(dln L/dln R)/3 < 0.1, so it converges in a few steps (unit test with a power-law profile: <= 8 steps, mass recovered to 0.5 per cent)

Code: src/tngxmm/massfit/guess_mass.py, src/tngxmm/massfit/build_fit_targets.py, src/tngxmm/photometry/build_obs_seed_lx.py

method.snapshot_to_zobs¶

Kind: validation

A view is a halo of snapshot z_snap observed at the redshift z_obs of its reference observation (with the 3 per cent relative jitter of the replica draw, sim.z_obs_source = ‘replica’). The gas, its structure and its temperature are those of z_snap; the distances, the angular size, the K-correction of the photons and the estimands are at z_obs: the true mass and the intrinsic hydrostatic mass are evaluated at 500 rho_c(z_obs) (m500c_zobs_msun, method.estimand_fixed_radius), so the true and the measured mass refer to one density contrast. The temet-exact reference luminosities keep 1/(1+z_snap), as the official TNG products do. The snapshots are the nine full snapshots 99, 91, 84, 78, 72, 67, 59, 50, 40 (z = 0 to 1.5; 50 and 40 are 0.5 apart), and the epoch window of the replica draw is +-0.15 with a fallback to the nearest snapshot. The gas itself is not evolved from z_snap to z_obs. Its effect on the hydrostatic bias is measured on the production truth (internal/ledger_tests/c4_gas_state_zobs.py, 7,374 views of 350 TNG-Cluster zoom targets). Per halo-snapshot a line through ln(M500c,HSE / M500c) of its views against z_obs - z_snap separates the definition at fixed gas (slope +0.52 per unit z in the median) from the bias at the definition of z_snap (intercept). Between neighbouring snapshots the intercept less the definitional change gives the gas-only slope k: -0.61 per unit z in the median (bootstrap 95 per cent -0.66 / -0.53; 16-84 per cent between clusters -3.2 / +2.1). The view term k (z_obs - z_snap) has mean -0.0012 (-0.0033 with the median view for halo-snapshots too small for a line), since the views are displaced both ways; a redshift bin moves by at most 0.01, and 0.033 if every view were displaced the same way. Stated in the paper as measured, with the mean k dz of every reported subsample; a subsample where it exceeds 0.03 carries it as an arm under the pre-registered arm rule. MACSIS has the same sign and a smaller size (T-M normalisation 15 per cent lower at z = 1). The direct test (v1_common, |dz| median 0.006) is deferred.

Value: z_obs = z of the reference observation x exp(N(0, 0.03)); estimands at 500 rho_c(z_obs); \|z_obs - z_snap\| median 0.033, p90 0.088 (replica list, 2,840 views)

Used by: sim.z_obs_source, sim.snaps

Evidence: barnes2017macsis p. 9 (Sect. 3.4 (X-ray temperature-mass: the normalisation of the combined sample is 15 per cent lower at z = 1 than at z = 0, attributed to growing non-thermal pressure))

Validation: Release 1.1.3 (unreleased): the review of the documentation – gas-state term (c4_gas_state_zobs.py, 2026-11-04): gas-only d ln(M500c,HSE / M500c) / dz = -0.61 (95 per cent -0.66 / -0.53) between neighbouring snapshots at a fixed definition (definitional slope +0.52), view term k dz mean -0.0012 to -0.0033, |k dz| median 0.044, same-direction bound 0.033. Replica list (campaigns/v4, 2,840 views): |z_obs - z_snap| median 0.033, p90 0.088, 432 views outside the +-0.15 window (epoch fallback). Maximal list as generated in the v1.0.1 trees (9,774 views of four simulations): |dz| median 0.055, p90 0.119; ln[M500c(500 rho_c(z_obs)) / M500c(500 rho_c(z_snap))] of the same particles median +0.001, 16-84 per cent -0.036 / +0.042, 5-95 per cent -0.062 / +0.073, negative when z_obs > z_snap (the density contrast rises with z)

method.estimand_fixed_radius¶

Kind: validation

Claims A and B compare each curve’s own spherical-overdensity M500 (observers report SO masses). Claim C compares the masses inside one FIXED radius, the true R500c at 500 rho_c(z_obs). The black, red and blue curves are evaluated at the observed redshift; the blue mass inside any radius comes from the fit’s per-sample cumulative profile (NFW plus gas self-gravity). This estimand was fixed by the author after seeing the v2 results and is said so in DESIGN v3.

Value: SO crossing at 500 rho_c(z_obs); fixed radius R500c(z_obs) with the band 0.5, 0.8, 1.2 R500c

Used by: massfit.fit_hydrostatic, viz.render_fits

Evidence: ansarifard2020 p. 10 (Sect. 4.3.6, Eq. 12 (1 - b_HE(r) = M_HE(r) / M_true(r) at the same radius))

Validation: Estimands at the observed redshift; node task queue in the package – the 500 rho_c(z_obs) crossing of the particle profile reproduces the campaign column m500c_zobs_msun exactly; the blue crossing reproduces the fit’s M500 (1.469 against 1.486 [1.20, 1.84] on mock_40_13701560_v2)

Code: src/tngxmm/analysis/estimands.py, src/tngxmm/massfit/fit_qc.py

method.qc_flags¶

Kind: validation

Fit QC flags are diagnostics of the fit itself and are identical for mocks and real data: a posterior median in the outer part of a hard prior range (c200, Z), the fitter’s P_out runaway, the guarded-peak centre flag, and bg_fallback (no on-detector background annulus beyond 2 R500). Flagged fits are reported and excluded from the science samples. No flag is tuned on the outcome. The concentration prior was changed on 2026-11-12 from flat to the published relation (fit.cm_prior) after the pilot, in which 18 of 67 in-domain fits ended at a bound of the flat prior; the relation and its width are the printed ones.

Value: prior_edge_c200, prior_edge_Z, pout_runaway, centre flag, bg_fallback

Used by: massfit.fit_hydrostatic

Evidence: gelman2020 p. 35 (p. 35, paragraph on the shrinkage between prior and posterior); nagai2007 p. 1 (Abstract (mock Chandra analyses of cosmological simulations with a model and procedure essentially identical to that of real data)); sanders2018 p. 5 (Sect. 2.5.9, p. 5 (adding a central point-mass component to the NFW fits: in 10 clusters the fit gave largely unconstrained central temperatures and unphysical central masses)); mantz2016 p. 16 (p. 16, figure caption (the scaling relation is fitted to all clusters and, separately, with the mis-centred and otherwise problematic clusters excluded; the normalisation agrees with an independent weak-lensing relation only in the latter)); snowden2008 p. 14 (Sect. 5 (cluster sample: a few clusters excluded from the processing because of extreme asymmetry or strong substructure obviating the circular assumption))

Validation: Blue mass at any radius, fit QC flags, multi-start optimiser – author decision 3 of PLAN_V41 (2026-10-17): every flag a diagnostic of the fit itself; none tuned on the outcome

Code: src/tngxmm/massfit/fit_qc.py

method.qc_prior_edge_fraction¶

Kind: validation

A posterior median counts as piled at a hard prior bound when it lies in the outer 2.5 % of the prior range (c200 flat in ln c over 1-10; Z over the free-Z prior range). The fraction is a hand-chosen diagnostic threshold, applied identically to mocks and real data; its effect was measured.

Value: 2.5 % of the prior range

Used by: massfit.fit_hydrostatic

Evidence: sanders2018 p. 6 (Sect. 2.5.10 (flat logarithmic concentration prior between c = 1 and 10)); gelman2020 p. 35 (p. 35, paragraph on the shrinkage between prior and posterior (a check on whether the data are informative for a parameter))

Validation: – R1: 1 % -> 5 % changes the excluded set by 7 of 172 production fits (27 -> 31 %) and the median ln_br at 1.0 R500c by +0.016, equal to the run-to-run sd of the median (0.018-0.027); 2.5 % is within 0.009 of both neighbours

Code: src/tngxmm/massfit/fit_qc.py

method.background_fallback_fov¶

Kind: validation

A fit is flagged bg_fallback when some image has no on-detector background annulus beyond 2 R500, i.e. the fitter’s own requirement of a measured background; it is evaluated from geometry before the fit and applies identically to mocks and real targets. It is not a tuned theta500 number.

Value: n_bg_fallback > 0; background annulus r > 2 R500; fewer than 50 exposed pixels count as none

Used by: massfit.fit_hydrostatic

Evidence: ghirardini2019 p. 4 (Sect. 2.2, p. 4 (X-COP, sky background from the regions at R > 2 R500, where the cluster emission is negligible)); eckert2022 p. 3 (Sect. 2.2 (X-COP spectral analysis: the sky-background model fitted to a region far outside the cluster, beyond 2 R500)); seppi2026 p. 5 (Sect. 4.1 (mock XMM-Newton observations: background spectrum from 1.5-2.0 R500c, capped at 15 arcmin; where the region falls out of the field of view, a circle of about 3 arcmin in a corner of the field instead). arXiv v1; the version of record (A&A 711, A213, p. 5) says the same in the past tense (‘fell outside the FoV’))

Validation: Blue mass at any radius, fit QC flags, multi-start optimiser – at z = 0.06 the field ends at 0.6-1.0 R500 and the background seed ‘none’ (constant ln SB = -15) produced the run-away mode (closure grid v4c); PLAN_V41 author decision 2

Code: src/tngxmm/massfit/fit_qc.py, src/tngxmm/massfit/fit_hydrostatic.py

method.reference_families¶

Kind: validation

The real-fit reference masses (claim A) are like-for-like: hydrostatic M500c at its OWN r500c from the curated catalogue only. Families: SZ (PSZ2, ACT DR6), hydrostatic (Lovisari et al. 2020 XMM, their Table A1; the copy in Wicker et al. 2023 is kept as a comparison family from its superseded rows), lensing NFW fits (Okabe and Smith 2016, Hoekstra et al. 2015, Herbonnet et al. 2020). Not families: Mantz et al. 2010b (Mgas/fgas, not hydrostatic), Mantz et al. 2016 (lensing inside a gas-mass r500), Mahdavi et al. 2013 (hydrostatic inside the LENSING r500), Martino et al. 2014 (its r500 definition is not verified), Applegate et al. 2014 (mass inside a fixed 1.5 Mpc sphere). A double cluster listed per component is excluded and reported, never chosen.

Value: REF_FAMILIES: PSZ2, ACT DR6, Lovisari+20 (Wicker+23 copy for comparison), Okabe & Smith 16, Hoekstra+15, Herbonnet+20

Used by: massfit.fit_hydrostatic, obs.build_observations

Evidence: lovisari2020 p. 17 (Table A1 caption, Cols. (3)-(4) (the hydrostatic M500 of each cluster, the hydrostatic family)); wicker2023 p. 11 (Sect. 5 (the XMM hydrostatic masses of Lovisari et al. 2020, copied in Table A.1; comparison family)); mantz2010b p. 7 (Sect. 2.2.3 (total masses from the measured gas mass and fgas, not hydrostatic)); mahdavi2013 p. 1 (Abstract (hydrostatic masses compared at the weak-lensing r500))

Validation: Real-fit reference masses: like-for-like, from the curated catalogue – against the old table SZ is identical, lensing within 0.1 %, Wicker+23 +3.1 % after the cosmology conversion; the Chandra-vs-Wicker gap of x1.6 came largely from comparing different quantities

Code: src/tngxmm/massfit/build_realfit_targets.py, src/tngxmm/obs/reference_masses.py

method.cosmology_conversion¶

Kind: literature

Every reference mass is re-expressed in the pipeline cosmology (Planck 2015) with cited converters. SZ masses are re-solved from their own scaling relation for the same observed signal (Planck 2013 XX Eqs. 7 and 9 with log Y* = -0.19, alpha = 1.79, beta = 2/3, theta* = 6.997’; the published cosmology of PSZ2 and ACT DR6 is flat H0 = 70, Om = 0.3); lensing masses keep the same shear profile (M at fixed angle scales as D_l D_s / D_ls); the WtG masses inside 1.5 Mpc are converted to M500c with the published c200c = 4 NFW. The published value is kept next to the converted one.

Value: mass_conversion.sz_m500_to_cosmology; m500c_to_cosmology(kind wl/hse); wtg_aperture_to_m500c

Used by: massfit.fit_hydrostatic, obs.build_observations

Evidence: planck2013_xx p. 5 (Table 1 and Eqs. 7, 9 (Y500-M500 relation and theta500)); act_dr6 p. 2 (Sect. 1-2 (flat cosmology H0 = 70, Om = 0.3 of the catalogue masses)); act_dr6 p. 7 (Sect. 3, after Eq. 4 (the normalisation 10^A0 = 3.0e-5 is chosen to give masses consistent with the stacked weak-lensing masses of Robertson et al. 2024)); planck2015_xxiv p. 3 (Sect. 2, footnote 3 (the PSZ2 mass proxy MYz is computed with h = 0.7, Omega_Lambda = 0.7)); robertson2024 p. 2 (Sect. 1 (the weak-lensing masses that set the DR6 normalisation assume flat H0 = 70, Om = 0.3)); applegate2014 p. 8 (Sect. 4 (the mass is measured inside a 1.5 Mpc aperture)); applegate2014 p. 9 (Sect. 4 (c200 = 4 with 0.11 dex log-normal scatter))

Code: src/tngxmm/obs/mass_conversion.py

method.multistart_optimiser¶

Kind: validation

Optional multi-start optimiser: the two optimiser stages run from the default start and from 6 starts (M500 seed x 0.5, 1, 2 times c200 = 3, 7); the highest log posterior seeds the MCMC and every start’s optimum is recorded. It is not adopted by any preset (no spec field; starts=single is the identical previous computation): the J1 diagnostic decides.

Value: OPTIMISER_START_GRID, --fitfix starts=grid (legacy: single)

Used by: massfit.fit_hydrostatic

Evidence: scheck2023 p. 4 (Sect. 3.4 (MBProj2 interpolation model: the fit can become trapped in local minima, so plausible starting values are obtained first from a modified beta model)); emcee p. 10 (p. 10, initialisation paragraph (start the walkers in a tight ball around a point expected to be close to the maximum probability point; this is more effective when walkers can get stuck in low-probability modes of a multi-modal landscape)); gelman2020 p. 13 (Sect. 3.1 (initial values are not supposed to matter in the asymptotic limit, but they can matter in practice, and a wrong choice can threaten the validity of the results)); gelman2020 p. 25 (Sect. 5.6 (disjoint posterior volumes: the minor modes can be avoided by judicious choices of initial values, by prior information or hard constraints, or pruned by estimating the mass in each mode)); gelman2020 p. 60 (Sect. 11.2 (chains from different starts converge to different local modes; the authors examine all the chains, work out how the local modes arise, and only then focus the computation on the mode holding the probability mass)); vehtari2021 p. 2 (Sect. 1.1 (convergence diagnostics are most effective with multiple chains initialised at a diverse set of starting points, which reduces the chance of falsely diagnosing mixing))

Validation: Blue mass at any radius, fit QC flags, multi-start optimiser – 6 dispersed starts + the default; no preset changes its spec hash; the grid values themselves are not tuned on a result

Code: src/tngxmm/massfit/fit_hydrostatic.py

method.closure_factorial¶

Kind: validation

The closure grid separates what the CHECKS closure gap confounds: “no background in the data” against “background frozen in the model” and background against AGN, in four data modes (clean, real, bgonly, agnonly) with SAS-masked fit arms (v4sas, v4sas_bgfree) built from the arm table; the default arms (v4, v32) and the gate are unchanged.

Value: DATA_MODES clean, real, bgonly, agnonly; arms v4, v32 (default); v4sas, v4sas_bgfree

Used by: validation.plan_closure_grid, validation.generate_closure_views, validation.fit_closure_views, validation.analyse_closure_grid, params:validation.closure.arms

Evidence: rasia2006 p. 1 (Abstract (the hydrostatic mass of simulated clusters is re-estimated after the Chandra-level background is reduced by a factor of 100, and the estimated mass underestimates the true mass profiles)); nagai2007 p. 3 (Sect. 3.1 (a first mock set with realistic exposure and background is used only to detect and mask clumps, with detection thresholds set from a false-detection count; all further analysis uses a second, very long and background-free set)); meneghetti2010 p. 6 (p. 6 (X-ray mock: the background is not included a priori since Rasia et al. 2006 showed that it does not induce any systematic on the bias)); gelman2020 p. 16 (Sect. 4.1 (fake-data simulation: check whether the procedure recovers the correct parameter values when fitting simulated data of the same size, shape and structure as the real data))

Validation: Closure grid: factorial data modes and SAS-masked fit arms – CHECKS closure real/clean 0.916, worst for NCC at z = 0.1; a ‘real’ cell regenerated in a new root is bit-identical to the CHECKS one

method.hse_crossing_rule¶

Kind: validation

The hydrostatic M500 of the truth is the innermost downward crossing of 500 rho_c beyond 0.4 R500c (log-log interpolation); the number of crossings is recorded in hse_crossing. Barnes et al. (2021) say only that the profile is interpolated; the handling of several roots and the inner exclusion are ours, and their effect was measured.

Value: innermost crossing, r > 0.4 R500c

Used by: sim.generate_views

Evidence: barnes2021 p. 9 (Sect. 2.4 (r500,est found by interpolating the profile to the radius where it crosses 500))

Validation: – R3: 9 of 7389 production views (0.12 %, 3 halo-snapshots) cross more than once; for them the outermost crossing would be ln 0.73-1.20 more massive; the inner exclusion 0.2-0.5 R500c changes no headline mass of 2463 truth files

Code: src/tngxmm/sim/compute_hse_mass.py

method.chain_budget¶

Kind: validation

Quick MCMC chains (450 steps) for every mock view, long chains (2,000 steps) for real clusters and the showcase mocks, to fit the compute budget.

Value: quick true (params:massfit.fit); long for real clusters

Used by: massfit.fit_hydrostatic, params:massfit.fit.quick

Evidence: sanders2018 p. 4 (Sect. 2.5 (emcee: 2000 steps, 1000 burn-in, 800 walkers)); scheck2023 p. 4 (Sect. 3.3 (MBProj2 chains of 15 000 steps, 5000 burn-in))

Validation: Science choices that fix the V4 campaign – Gate Q: quick and long agree to 0.002 dex in the median with 0.013 dex scatter; ~11 h instead of ~28 h on one node

method.chain_convergence¶

Kind: validation

The chains of pass 2 have a fixed length, the same for mocks and real observations, and their convergence is judged on the ensemble and on repeat fits, not per walker. Proposed and not yet confirmed: the first 2,000 steps are discarded and the next 2,000 kept, with emcee’s differential-evolution move. In the pilot of 2026-11-26 (22 pass-1 mocks, three samplers, 500 burn-in and 6,000 kept steps each) no chain mixed per walker: the rank-normalised split R-hat of ln M500 over walkers stays near 2 in every window, and emcee’s autocorrelation time is a tenth of the window at every length, so it is not measured. Five further samplers started from the end of those chains (differential evolution on parameter subsets, with and without reflection at the bounds, and an ensemble slice sampler) do not mix either. What is stable is the ensemble: from step 2,000 the median of ln M500 moves by less than 0.01 in the median fit (0.03 to 0.08 at most) and the 16-84 per cent width no longer grows. The check of every production run is a sample of fits run a second time with another seed; the difference of the two medians is reported beside the fit uncertainty and corrects nothing. The criteria of the emcee authors (a chain of 10 to 50 autocorrelation times, every walker crossing the posterior) are not met, and the paper says so. The preset fields that carry the length and the repeat sample are written with the pass-2 preset.

Value: proposed: 2,000 steps discarded + 2,000 kept, sampler_moves de; repeat fits with a second seed, reported only

Used by: massfit.fit_hydrostatic

Evidence: li2024 p. 4 (Sect. 3.2 (MBProj2D with emcee: 190 walkers, 5000 iterations, the first 1000 as burn-in)); sanders2018 p. 4 (Sect. 2.5 (MBProj2 with emcee: 2000 steps, 1000 burn-in, 800 walkers)); ettori2019 p. 3 (Sect. 3 (X-COP hydrostatic masses with emcee: 10000 steps, about 100 walkers, 5000 discarded)); hogg2018 p. 15 (Sect. 5 (long enough when runs initialised differently and with different seeds give substantially the same posterior inferences)); emcee_autocorr p. 1 (Introduction of the page (the Gelman-Rubin statistic over the walkers of one ensemble)); emcee_autocorr p. 5 (Section on the estimators (an estimate of tau needs more than about 50 tau samples)); sanders2014 p. 9 (Sect. 6.2 (MBProj: the chain should be about 10 autocorrelation periods long; acceptance ideally 0.2 to 0.5)); hogg2018 p. 29 (Sect. 9 (a converged ensemble run shows every walker crossing the parameter space many times))

Validation: The chains of pass 2 – pilot of 22 mocks: R-hat of ln M500 over walkers about 2.0 (DE) and 2.6 (stretch) after 6,000 steps; median of ln M500 stable to 0.006 (median) and 0.03-0.08 (largest) from step 2,000; DE and scaled-start DE agree to 0.01 (median), 0.03 (largest)

Code: src/tngxmm/massfit/fit_hydrostatic.py, src/tngxmm/massfit/sample_posterior.py

method.sampler_switches¶

Kind: literature

Every preset fit samples with mbproj2d’s emcee sampler unchanged: the stretch move of Goodman & Weare (2010) and max(2n + 2, 4n) walkers started in a Gaussian ball of width 0.01 about the optimum. Two fit-fix switches that no preset sets change it for the closure arms of STATUS Q18 (v1sas_de, v1sas_scaled, v1sas_delong). sampler_moves = de uses emcee’s differential-evolution move alone, with its default settings (the jump factor 2.38 / sqrt(2d) of ter Braak & Vrugt 2008). emcee’s snooker move is not used: in emcee 3.1.6 DESnookerMove’s direction is not a unit vector and its Metropolis factor has 0.5 (d - 1) where eq. 4 of ter Braak & Vrugt (2008) has d - 1, so it does not keep the target (variance 0.54 of the true one on a 34-dimensional Gaussian). walker_start = scaled draws the walkers about the same optimum from the normal approximation of the posterior, so that they start at the posterior’s scale instead of expanding from the ball; parameters at a prior bound or without information keep the ball. How the covariance is computed is a design choice (OPEN_ITEMS section P). Every fit records the convergence of its kept steps in result.json (chain_diagnostics): for ln M500 the rank-normalised split R-hat over walkers and the bulk effective sample size of Vehtari et al. (2021), and emcee’s integrated autocorrelation time with the number of steps over it, both given only where R-hat is below 1.01 and the estimator truncated (“below resolution” otherwise); the largest split R-hat of any parameter; the rise of the mean log posterior from the first to the last tenth of the steps; the acceptance fraction; the quantiles of ln M500 over all kept draws. None of them changes a fit or selects one: no threshold of R-hat, ESS, N/tau or acceptance is applied, and the QC column chain_lp_rising is reported, not in QC_EXCLUDING.

Value: sampler_moves stretch (de: DEMove alone); walker_start ball (scaled: normal approximation); chain_diagnostics recorded, no cut

Used by: massfit.fit_hydrostatic

Evidence: goodman2010 p. 6 (Sect. 2, journal p. 69 (the stretch move: the simplest affine-invariant ensemble move, and the best of the authors’ moves on the Rosenbrock test)); goodman2010 p. 7 (Sect. 2, journal p. 70 (the moves sample correctly from any general ensemble, one not confined to a lower-dimensional hyperplane)); emcee_docs p. 3 (Moves page (the mixture of moves of emcee 3, for models where the stretch move is not well suited, such as high dimensional or multi-modal probability surfaces)); emcee_docs p. 4 (Moves page, DEMove (default mean jump factor 2.38 / sqrt(2 ndim))); terbraak2008 p. 3 (Sect. 2.1, journal p. 437 (the differential-evolution jump factor 2.38 / sqrt(2d), emcee’s DEMove default)); terbraak2008 p. 4 (Sect. 2.3, journal p. 438 (the snooker update: a jump along the unit direction through the walker and another chain’s state, eq. 3)); terbraak2008 p. 5 (Sect. 2.3, eq. 4, journal p. 439 (the Metropolis ratio of the snooker update carries ||x* - z||^(d-1) / ||x_i - z||^(d-1))); terbraak2008 p. 9 (Sect. 5, journal p. 443 (differential-evolution MCMC works in 50 to 100 dimensions)); emcee p. 10 (p. 10, initialisation (walkers spread over a reasonable range in parameter space, or in a very tight ball about the expected maximum, which expands to fill the posterior in a few autocorrelation times)); emcee p. 8 (p. 8, the acceptance fraction (between 0.2 and 0.5 as a rule of thumb)); emcee p. 11 (p. 11 (a run is long enough only when the autocorrelation time is much shorter than it, say by a factor of 10)); vehtari2021 p. 9 (Sect. 4.1-4.2 (the bulk ESS of rank-normalised draws; the larger of the rank-normalised split R-hat and its folded version, reported per parameter)); vehtari2021 p. 4 (Sect. 2, recommendations for practice (use the sample only if R-hat < 1.01)); vehtari2021 p. 5 (Sect. 2, recommendations for practice (a rank-normalised ESS above 400))

Code: src/tngxmm/massfit/sample_posterior.py, src/tngxmm/massfit/fit_hydrostatic.py, src/tngxmm/massfit/fit_qc.py, src/tngxmm/validation/closure_grid.py

method.pairing_caliper¶

Kind: literature

Mass window of the release 1.2 pairing (donors.pair_projections): a caliper of 0.25 standard deviations of the log10 fit mass of the real observations of the pool (sample standard deviation over the observations with a finite fit mass and redshift), computed from the reference table alone before any halo is paired, recorded with the pairing and never widened; the redshift window dz 0.25 and the distance widths are those of the matched draw. It replaces the 0.10 dex rung of the earlier ladder (decision of 2026-11-04). On the 167 V4 real fits the standard deviation is 0.280 dex, so the caliper would be 0.070 dex; the value of the release is that of the refitted pool (B3). A fixed window is a sensitivity arm.

Value: PairingSpec.mass_window_dex None = caliper_sd 0.25 x sd(log10 M_fit, reference pool); dz_max 0.25

Used by: params:donors.pairing.caliper_sd

Evidence: stuart2010 p. 7 (Sect. 2.2 (distance measures: Rosenbaum and Rubin 1985b suggest a caliper of 0.25 standard deviations)); stuart2010 p. 7 (Sect. 2.2 (when the variance in the treatment group is twice as large as that in the control group, a caliper of 0.2 standard deviations removes 98% of the bias in a normally distributed covariate; with a much larger variance smaller calipers are necessary))

Code: src/tngxmm/donors/pair_projections.py

method.maximal_list_donors¶

Kind: validation

Donor assignment of the maximal list: the 10 nearest donors in (z, mass), each donor used at most 2 times the mean demand. The cap factor is a declared design parameter (OPEN_ITEMS.md section P, decision of 2026-11-04): it bounds how often one observation is reused, which Stuart (2010) says must be monitored, and no source gives a number for it. 1.5 and 3 are carried as a stated arm of the maximal list. The matched comparison of release 1.2 does not use the maximal list’s donors: its views are paired on the fit mass by the caliper rule (method.pairing_caliper), which has no reuse cap but reports the reuse (max_views_per_observation).

Value: n_nearest 10; cap_factor 2.0

Used by: donors.build_max_targets, params:donors.max.n_nearest, params:donors.max.cap_factor

Evidence: seppi2022 p. 5 (p. 5 (the eRASS1 simulation uses the attitude file and good time intervals of the real survey; p. 23 resamples the real background maps)); gianfagna2021 p. 5 (Sect. 4.2 (the MUSIC NIKA2 twin sample, simulated clusters chosen to reproduce in mass and redshift the clusters of the real NIKA2 SZ large program)); stuart2010 p. 9 (Sect. 3.1.3 (with or without replacement: replacement can decrease bias, the treatment effect may then rest on few controls, and the number of times each control is matched should be monitored)); stuart2010 p. 8 (Sect. 3.1 (k:1 nearest-neighbour matching can give poor matches; imposing a caliper and selecting a match only within it avoids them, at the price that some treated units get no match)); austin2011 (Abstract (definition of the caliper width; Monte Carlo recommendation of its size))

Validation: Sensitivity tests S1, S3, S4, S5, S6 – S5: n_nearest is immaterial (median donor distance -1.2% at 5, +2.4% at 20); the cap factor moves it by +16% at 1.5 and -15% at 3, beyond the 10% tolerance: kept at 2.0 and stated as an arm of every result read off the maximal list; no mass effect is claimed

method.halo_groups¶

Kind: convention (OPEN: no citation and no validation yet)

The unit of the folds and of the bootstrap of a mock statistic is the halo, not the view: every view of one physical halo, at every snapshot and along every line of sight, is in one halo fold and is resampled with the others. The halo is the split unit of the view table (zoom_target_id): the TNG-Cluster zoom target (GroupOrigHaloID) and the z = 0 object of a halo of a TNG box (the z = 0 FoF group its central ends in). The FLAMINGO, SIMBA and EAGLE tables carry the snapshot’s halo id there; a simulation whose identity column equals halo_id at several snapshots is refused unless a column that follows a halo (FLAMINGO’s HBT+ track) or halo-snapshot groups are asked for explicitly. The rule of every simulation is stored in the halo-fold table and read back wherever folds are attached, and a table is read only against its pinned sha256. The halo folds are added beside the donor folds, which do not change and must have the same number of folds: fold f trains on the views whose halo and whose donor are both outside f and tests on those whose halo and donor are both in f, so no halo and no donor is on both sides. Only views whose halo fold equals their donor fold are ever tested.

Value: 5 halo folds, seed 20261103, blocks of 5 over the groups sorted by stratum (simulation, frozen split) and decreasing largest M500c; candidate table campaigns/halo_folds/halo_folds_v4_max_box.csv (not chosen); the bootstrap draws halo groups with replacement and keeps all their views, seed 20261103, no interval below two halos (certification rules: resample_by)

Used by: params:bank.folds.halo_folds, params:bank.folds.halo_folds_sha256, params:analysis.mock.views, params:analysis.mock.halo_folds, params:analysis.mock.halo_folds_sha256, bank.build_manifest, analysis.mock_fit_table

Code: src/tngxmm/donors/halo_groups.py

ESA’s standard source detection edetect_chain (SAS 22.1) runs on mocks and real observations alike, in the five PPS bands (PI 200-500, 500-1000, 1000-2000, 2000-4500, 4500-12000 eV) with the three cameras fitted jointly, with a fixed calibration date (2026-10-09, the day the CCF set was downloaded) so that the CIF does not depend on the day of the run.

Value: bands [1, 2, 3, 4, 5]; analysis date 2026-10-09

Used by: instrument.run_sas_detection, params:instrument.sas_detect.bands

Evidence: webb2020 p. 3 (Sect. 2 (detection simultaneously on all images and bands 1-5 of the three cameras))

Validation: pn readout-edge rows in every band; fixed SAS calibration date – DetectSettings.analysisdate = 2026-10-09T00:00:00, manifest sha256 d29ec7a2…e740d recorded in sas_detect/provenance.json
