method.bank_filter¶

Kind: validation

The ML bank leaves out of training the mock rows whose SEED PHOTOMETRY measured no positive luminosity (rule seed_unmeasured: lx500_seed <= 0 or missing), and the same rule flags a real row out of the domain at prediction (bank.filter_rows.out_of_domain), so that one observable, measured by the same code on a mock fit target and a real observation, defines both the training set and the domain. Until 2026-11-04 the rule (lx_zero) read the generation’s lx_r500_erg_s, measured in the true R500 at the true centre, a quantity no real observation has; the fixture bank keeps it by name. A first guess limited by the field measured a luminosity and stays in training.

Value: filter rules [seed_unmeasured] (fixtures: [lx_zero])

Used by: bank.filter_rows, params:bank.filter.rules

Evidence: kelly2007 p. 2 (Sect. 1 (non-detections are a separate case from measured values; the usual astronomical regression estimators BCES and FITEXY do not apply to them))

Validation: – rule seed_unmeasured on lx500_seed (mock fit targets and the real seed table, one code); the real seed photometry measures a luminosity for 637 of 638 targets

Code: src/tngxmm/bank/filter_rows.py

method.realism_domain_gap¶

Kind: validation

A mock that replicates a donor in its observing conditions is not thereby indistinguishable from the real observation, and indistinguishability is a separate requirement from an unbiased hydrostatic estimand. The realism of the bank is measured by a classifier two-sample test (C2ST, validation/realism.py): real observations against their replicas, the same features and the same render for both, folds grouped by donor. The null of Lopez-Paz & Oquab (2017) is a null for the classification ACCURACY (Binomial(n_te, 1/2), about N(1/2, 1/(4 n_te)) on a held-out set), not for the AUC: the null of the AUC is the permutation null (the out-of-fold AUC with the real / mock labels shuffled between donors), centred near 0.5 with a spread set by the number of donors (the audit measured a sampling standard error of 0.014 for the logistic and 0.010 for the boosting classifier at 465 donors, about 0.02 at 195). A model trained on mock images or embeddings (paper part D) and applied to real clusters is extrapolating while the C2ST separates the two, because the target error is bounded only by the source error plus the divergence a domain classifier measures. Count matching (thinning each mock’s cluster photons to the donor’s net 0.15-1 R500 counts) changes the counts and S/B channel only; the background level and spectrum, the compact-source density, the exposure-map footprint (chip gaps and bad columns without attitude smoothing) and the gas morphology are untouched by it.

Value: gate before any real-cluster ML result: condition-matched C2ST AUC <= 0.75 (W8 proposal); measured on campaign_v1_full with 17 image statistics: AUC 0.990 (logistic) / 1.000 (boosting) before count matching (498 donors, 1463 views); the 2026-10-20 instrument fixes reduce it but do not reach the permutation null (validation.number)

Used by: embed.extract_embeddings, bank.render_bank

Evidence: lopezpaz2017 p. 1 (Abstract (the classifier two-sample test: under P = Q the held-out accuracy stays at chance)); lopezpaz2017 p. 3 (Sect. 3.1 (null distribution of the C2ST accuracy: Binomial(n_te, 1/2), approximately N(1/2, 1/(4 n_te)))); lopezpaz2017 p. 4 (Sect. 3.3 (the statistic is interpretable as the share of samples told apart)); bendavid2010 (Sect. 4.2, Theorem 2 and the comment after it, printed p. 158: ‘the bound shows that source error and unlabeled HΔH-divergence are important quantities in computing the target error’; Sect. 4.1 after Lemma 1, printed p. 156: ‘Label each unlabeled source instance with 0 and unlabeled target instance as 1’); cannon2022 p. 14 (Sect. 6, Conclusion (neural SBI fails under misspecification for every method tested)); schmitt2023 p. 4 (Sect. 1 (amortized posteriors degrade with the simulation gap)); schmitt2023 p. 3 (Sect. 1 (misspecification measured as a distribution mismatch in summary space, MMD)); ciprijanovic2021 p. 1 (Abstract (a model trained on simulated galaxy images loses accuracy on observed images)); huertascompany2023 p. 46 (Sect. 7.1 (models trained on simulations are sensitive to domain shift))

Validation: – V3.1 DINOv3 embeddings: linear-probe C2ST AUC 1.000 (W8 S3, A10 gate proposed at <= 0.75); campaign_v1_full, 195 donors, one replica each, grouped by donor: 17 image statistics 0.993 / 0.999 (logistic / boosting) stored, 0.985 / 0.998 count-matched, 0.796 / 0.958 with every instrument fix of 2026-10-20; DINOv3 ViT-L CLS (PCA-2 / 8 / 32) 0.973 / 0.998 / 1.000 stored, 0.512 / 0.773 / 0.918 with the fixes; 465 donors with the round-3 fixes (vignetting centre, pn extent, MOS RGA added): statistics 0.777 / 0.865, DINOv3 0.540 / 0.765 / 0.913, cluster region only 0.560 / 0.691 / 0.825, field only 0.502 / 0.615 / 0.791; gas statistics alone (shape + band ratios) 0.664 / 0.677; permutation null: sampling standard error 0.014 / 0.010 (logistic / boosting) at 465 donors, about 0.02 at 195 (the earlier ‘null spread 0.47-0.52’ was the spread of a cross-validation shuffle, not the permutation null); all numbers are of older configurations, the final one is re-measured by the frozen evaluation on an untouched donor split

Code: src/tngxmm/validation/realism.py

method.c2st_donor_bootstrap¶

Kind: convention (OPEN: no citation and no validation yet)

The standard error of the C2ST AUC is the standard deviation of the AUC over resamples of the DONORS drawn with replacement (all rows of a drawn donor kept; the out-of-fold scores are resampled, the classifiers are not refitted), the bootstrap estimate of the standard error of Efron (1979). The donor, not the row, is the resampled unit because the rows of one donor (its real observation and its replicas) are not independent.

Value: n_boot = 200 donor resamples (realism.c2st.n_boot); se_boot = SD (ddof 1) of the resampled mean AUCs; resamples without both classes are skipped

Used by: params:realism.c2st.n_boot, realism.c2st_report, realism.dino_c2st_report

Code: src/tngxmm/realism/c2st.py

method.c2st_permutation_p_value¶

Kind: literature

The p-value of the C2ST AUC against its within-donor permutation null is (1 + b) / (1 + n), where b is the number of permuted AUCs at least as large as the observed one and n is the number of permutations (realism.c2st.null_summary). It is never zero, and it is the exact p-value of the randomisation test for a Monte Carlo null (Phipson & Smyth 2010, Sect. 4), a valid and slightly conservative one for permutations drawn with replacement (Sect. 6.2). The unbiased b / n would be an invalid p-value that can be exactly zero.

Value: p = (1 + #{null >= auc}) / (1 + n_perm), n_perm = 200 (realism.c2st.n_perm), so p >= 1/201

Used by: params:realism.c2st.n_perm, realism.c2st_report, realism.dino_c2st_report

Evidence: phipson2010 p. 6 (Sect. 4 (exact p-value for Monte Carlo tests: p_u = P(B <= b) = (b + 1)/(m + 1), where B counts the simulated statistics t_sim >= t_obs)); phipson2010 p. 7 (Sect. 5 (permutations sampled without replacement: p_u = (b + 1)/(m + 1), b the number of permuted statistics exceeding the observed one)); phipson2010 p. 8 (Sect. 6.2 (p_u = (b + 1)/(m + 1) is valid but conservative when permutations are drawn with replacement, and converges to the exact value as the number of distinct permutations grows)); phipson2010 p. 3 (Sect. 1 (a permutation test could never yield an exactly zero p-value if all permutations were enumerated))

Code: src/tngxmm/realism/c2st.py

viz.core_class¶

Kind: literature

The core class written into the mock-against-simulation figure (viz.plot_mock_truth) is the central-entropy criterion of the TNG-Cluster cool-core study: K0 inside 0.012 r500c, a strong cool core at or below 22 keV cm^2, a non-cool core above 150 keV cm^2, a weak cool core between (the thresholds of Hudson et al. 2010). K0 is read from the truth profile: the gas-mass-weighted mean of the hot-phase shell entropies (K_mw = T_mw n_e,vw^-2/3) over the shells inside the aperture. The product differs from the paper’s measurement in three ways, stated in the figure’s docstring: the hot phase is the non-star-forming gas above 5.5 MK with no cooling cut (the paper: non-star-forming, cooling, T > 1e6 K gas bound to the cluster), n_e is volume-weighted within a shell, and the shells start at 0.01 r500c. A label only: no number of the analysis depends on it.

Value: aperture 0.012 r500c; strong CC <= 22, NCC > 150 keV cm^2

Used by: params:viz.mock_truth.core_class

Evidence: lehle2024 p. 4 (Table 1 (central entropy K0 inside 0.012 r500c: SCC <= 22, WCC 22-150, NCC > 150 keV cm^2)); lehle2024 p. 3 (Sects. 2.2 and 2.3 (gas bound to the cluster, non-star-forming, cooling, T > 1e6 K; mass-weighted means; the potential minimum as the centre)); hudson2010 p. 9 (Sect. 3.1.4 (the K0 distribution of HIFLUGCS divides at 22 and 150 keV cm^2 into SCC, WCC and NCC))

fit.particle_background_esas¶

Kind: literature

massfit.qpb_model = ‘esas-fwc-corners’ (the presets’ background since 2026-11-03) models the particle background of each fitted image as XMM-ESAS and X-COP do. The quiescent particle background (QPB) is the filter-wheel-closed (FWC) image of the camera and band, CCD by CCD (MOS) or quadrant by quadrant (pn), with its level fixed before the fit by the observation’s own unexposed corners (the pn corner counts with the out-of-time events subtracted). The sky is a vignetted component with a free level and no prior, the residual soft protons a separate component with a free level on the CCF flare maps of the image’s filter and band (as current ESAS proton co-adds them; the same maps the mock stage draws its soft protons on, instrument.soft_proton_spatial), and the pn out-of-time events have their own template. The template of a real image is built from the observation’s own corners and event list. That of a mock (instrument.qpb_model_images) is built the same way and is not the QPB the stage drew: its levels are the mock’s own corner measurement, Poisson counts drawn with the conditions donor’s corner statistics at the mock’s live time (donor_corner_qpb.simulated_corner_measurement), and its spatial form comes from the half of the FWC events the draw did not use (even and odd events, donor_background.FWC_HALVES), so the template carries a counting error and calibration noise of its own, as a real one does. What a mock does not carry: (1) the error of predicting the in-field QPB from the corners (in a mock both follow the same FWC-scaled model), including soft protons reaching the pn corners (Marelli et al. 2021); (2) a soft-proton shape other than the CCF flare map, which is also the fit’s template. (2) is measured for one other shape, in one direction, by the closure arm v1_spflat (flat protons, the flare template); that is not a bound, and the arm has not run. For (1) no published accuracy exists: Kuntz & Snowden (2008) assume the ratio of the QPB in two regions of a chip constant in time, there being too few FWC data to test it; Leccardi & Molendi (2008) find it independent of the observation in closed observations, without a size; Snowden et al. (2008) say the method works with, in general, quite high reliability. We measure it on calibration data (tools/fwc_qpb_level.py). The CCF FWC event lists keep every closed exposure apart (105 MOS1, 104 MOS2 and 50 pn full-frame exposures, revolutions 230-4027), so each one can be treated as an observation: its in-field QPB is predicted from its own corners as the fit’s template is built and compared with its in-field counts. The FWC reference follows the state of the CCDs (instrument.fwc_state, rule epoch-normal-1): an epoch is a run of closed exposures with the same working CCDs, read from the exposure table of each list (MOS1 revolutions 230-914, 1005-2380 and 2430-4027, around the losses of CCD 6 and CCD 3, instrument.mos_ccd_loss; MOS2 and the pn one epoch each); an observation takes the epoch of its working CCDs and its revolution, and the reference of the epoch is built from its rows with NFLAG 0. Rows with NFLAG 1 or 2 have 0.5-1 keV corner counts per hard count 1.1-5.7 times those of the same CCD’s rows with NFLAG 0 (medians), so we read them as the anomalous state of Kuntz & Snowden (2008), which occurs in MOS1 CCDs 4 and 5 and MOS2 CCDs 2 and 5 (NFLAG 3 marks a CCD that is off). The corner scales leave out the corners of the CCDs the fit leaves out of its images (the anomalous MOS CCDs of the soft-CCD table), as ESAS scales each CCD with its own corners. Before this rule the reference averaged every exposure and row: MOS1 CCD 4, which after revolution 2382 records 41 per cent fewer corner and 20 per cent fewer field counts per unit exposure (it lost RAWY < 150 with CCD 3) while CCDs 2, 5 and 7 do not change, had its per-pixel template 26 per cent low after that revolution and 8 per cent high before it, and the anomalous rows entered the 0.5-1 keV reference. Each exposure is now predicted with the reference of the other exposures of its epoch in the same way. The scatter of the ratio beyond counting noise is the level error d of a real template (a mock’s template already carries the counting noise of the corners). Over the fit regions of the real targets (the circle inside each square crop of half-width 3 theta500, root mean square) d is 0.030 (68 per cent 0.024-0.036) for MOS1 at 2-4.5 keV and 0.017 at 4.5-12 keV, 0.026 and 0.010 for MOS2, 0.022 and 0.010 for the pn; at 0.5-1 keV 0.080, 0.027 and 0.029, at 1-2 keV 0.020, 0.022 and 0.004 (MOS1, MOS2, pn). The circle that holds each crop gives up to 0.010 less (pn 2-4.5 keV 0.001 more); a crop much smaller than the median (high z, within 5 arcmin of the axis) sees up to 1.7 times these values (MOS1 2-4.5 keV 0.051 there, 0.022 over the whole field). Closed exposures cannot show the soft protons in the pn corners; their published bound, a revolution-to-revolution difference of at most 2.3 per cent (Marelli et al. 2021), added in quadrature gives the pn 0.037, 0.023, 0.032 and 0.025 in the four bands. d is a lower bound: closed exposures also miss the cluster’s own photons in the pn corners (Marelli et al. 2021 give no size), the out-of-time events the pn model leaves in the corners, and the photons and low-energy particles that reach part of the corners of MOS CCDs 2 and 7, which De Luca & Molendi (2004) mask and our corner selection keeps (OPEN_ITEMS). The closure arms v1_qpbhi and v1_qpblo fix the template of each image at (1 + d) and (1 - d) times its corner level, d of its camera and band (closure_grid.QPB_LEVEL_ERROR), and the sky start removes the scaled template. The level errors of different bands are far from fully correlated in the FWC data (estimates between -0.8 and +0.5, noisy), so a common factor is not the worst case; v1_qpbsoft and v1_qpbhard move the bands at or below 2 keV and those above in opposite directions. v1_qpbfree (the level free) measures what freeing the level costs in precision, the question whether production should fix it at all; it does not bound the error of a level fixed at a wrong value. The wrong-template arm v1_esasalt changes only the FWC set (its template has the same simulated corner levels). MOS CCDs in the anomalous state (donor table soft_ccd, 0.5-1 keV excess) are left out of the fit, as X-COP and Snowden et al. (2008) discard them; pn images of Large or Small Window exposures are left out, since they have no usable unexposed corners and ESAS pnspectra refuses those modes. The one difference from X-COP is the fit itself. X-COP fits single-band (0.7-1.2 keV) surface-brightness profiles and temperatures from separate annular spectra; we forward-model four bands jointly. Motivation: with a free vignetted / unvignetted background, the closure fits put part of the flat particle background under the cluster. The background-truth oracle (closure_grid arm v1_frzbgtrue) shows that this accounts for most of the mass deficit of the background cells (DECISIONS, “The background-cell deficit”).

Value: qpb_model esas-fwc-corners (production): QPB = FWC image per CCD / quadrant x corner scale (fixed), FWC reference of the observation's epoch in the normal CCD state; sky vignetted, free; soft protons separate on the flare maps, free; pn OoT template | mixture (diagnostic); level error d per camera and band from the FWC exposures (a lower bound), closure arms v1_qpbhi / lo / soft / hard

Used by: massfit.qpb_model, instrument.qpb_model_images, instrument.qpb_units

Evidence: snowden2008 p. 5 (Sect. 3.1.1 (the QPB of an observation from the FWC data and its unexposed corners, CCD by CCD)); snowden2008 p. 5 (Sect. 3.1.1 (the corner-scaled FWC model of the QPB works, in general, with quite high reliability)); kuntz2008 p. 13 (Sect. 3.4 (QPB removal: the ratio of the spectra of two regions of a chip assumed constant in time, for want of FWC data; NASA preprint pagination)); leccardi2008 p. 18 (App. B, Eq. B.1 (closed observations: the ratio of the particle-background normalisations of two detector regions is the same in every observation)); marelli2021 p. 11 (Sect. 7 (pn corners: the revolution-to-revolution difference of the contamination by focused soft protons and photons is at most the 2.3 per cent intrinsic scatter of the pn-MOS2 relation; quoted with the paper’s spelling)); deluca2004 p. 4 (Sect. 3.1 (out-of-FOV data: a region of MOS CCDs 2 and 7 masked, reached by X-ray photons and low-energy particles)); kuntz2008 p. 9 (Sect. 3.3 (anomalous states of single MOS chips; NASA preprint pagination)); marelli2021 p. 12 (Sect. 7.1 (the pn corners as a proxy of the in-field particle background: photons concentrated by the telescope reach them)); snowden2008 p. 6 (Sect. 3.2 (soft protons: a spatial distribution of their own)); kuntz2008 p. 12 (Sect. 3.4 (QPB removal: the QPB of an observation from its unexposed regions, chip by chip; NASA preprint pagination)); kuntz2008 p. 26 (Sect. 6 (the FWC data resolve the spatial variation of the QPB; NASA preprint pagination)); esas_mosback p. 1 (Sect. 3, Description); esas_mosback p. 5 (Sect. 8, Algorithm (the MOS background image from the FWC images of the CCDs)); esas_pnback p. 5 (Sect. 8, Algorithm (pn: corner count rate per quadrant, out-of-time events removed)); ghirardini2019 p. 6 (Sect. 2.4.1 (X-COP spectral extraction: the FWC data rescaled per CCD with the corners; CCDs in anomalous mode discarded)); snowden2008 p. 14 (Sect. 4 (the 0.2-1.0 keV images examined for CCDs in an anomalous state; such a CCD excluded)); esas_pnspectra p. 3 (Sect. 5, Errors (unsupportedSMODE)); esas_pnspectra p. 7 (Sect. 8, Algorithm (the pn corner rate with the out-of-time corner events subtracted)); esas_pnspectra p. 8 (Sect. 8, Algorithm (out-of-time FWC images)); esas_proton p. 1 (Sect. 3, Description); ghirardini2019 p. 3 (Sect. 2.2.2 (X-COP imaging: the particle-background images rescaled with the corners, subtracted with a fixed level)); ghirardini2019 p. 6 (Sect. 2.4.1-2.4.2 (X-COP spectra: FWC rescaled with the corners CCD by CCD; the image of the rescaled FWC data is the particle-background model; pn out-of-time model; in the spectral fits the normalisation free within 10 per cent)); ghirardini2019 p. 7 (Sect. 2.4.2 (X-COP: residual soft protons as an additional component with free normalisation)); eckert2017xcop p. 3 (Sect. 3.1 (X-COP: separate 2-D models of the non-X-ray background, the quiescent soft protons and the sky)); sas_attcalc p. 2 (Sect. 3, Description (events without valid attitude get NULL sky coordinates)); sas_evselect p. 8 (Sect. 3.4, Exposure Information (the time selection of a product is per CCD, kept in its data subspace, and gives ONTIMEnn))

Validation: – d over the real targets’ fit regions, epoch reference in the normal state: MOS1 0.080 / 0.020 / 0.030 (68 per cent 0.024-0.036) / 0.017 at 0.5-1 / 1-2 / 2-4.5 / 4.5-12 keV, MOS2 0.027 / 0.022 / 0.026 / 0.010, pn 0.029 / 0.004 / 0.022 / 0.010 (0.037 / 0.023 / 0.032 / 0.025 with the pn corner bound of Marelli et al. 2021); a lower bound

Code: src/tngxmm/massfit/fit_hydrostatic.py, src/tngxmm/massfit/qpb_template.py, src/tngxmm/instrument/fwc_state.py, src/tngxmm/instrument/donor_corner_qpb.py, src/tngxmm/donor_tables/fwc.py, src/tngxmm/validation/closure_grid.py, tools/fwc_qpb_level.py

instrument.pn_hard_band_cu_gap¶

Kind: instrument

The PPS pn images of the 4.5-12 keV band (band 5000) leave out PI 7.8-8.2 keV, the Cu K-alpha fluorescence line of the pn particle background; the MOS images and the other pn bands are contiguous. instrument.pn_hard_band = ‘pps’ selects the mock pn band-5000 images the same way (cluster, sky, AGN, particle background alike). With a donor background the pn particle level must then come from the donor’s own corners (the ESAS path: the FWC spectrum at the corner scale, binned in the PPS PI ranges, no further factor; the gap holds 27.6 per cent of the stage’s FWC spectrum in 4.5-12 keV and 27.5 per cent of the CCF EPN_FWC in-FOV events, so the two FWC data sets agree); the stage refuses the gap on the image-decomposition level, which is referred to the contiguous band. massfit.pn_hard_band = ‘pps’ fits the pn band-5000 image with the cluster count rate of the band minus that of 7.8-8.2 keV. The real-data particle-background template (massfit.qpb_model ‘esas-fwc-corners’) takes the FWC events in the same PI ranges. Without this, the pn template exceeded the data in 4.5-12 keV for 78 per cent of the observations, and the fit predicts 1.4-3.6 per cent too many pn hard-band cluster counts on real images (XSPEC, PN_Medium PATTERN<=4 ARF, kT 3-10 keV, z 0.06-0.5).

Value: pn band 5000 = PI (4500:7800] or (8200:12000] eV (PPS); instrument.pn_hard_band / massfit.pn_hard_band None (contiguous, production) | 'pps'

Used by: instrument.pn_hard_band, massfit.pn_hard_band

Evidence: mateos2009 p. 2 (Sect. 2, footnote 4 (the 2XMM pipeline source detection, SAS 7.1.0: in the 4.5-12.0 keV band the pn photons of 7.8-8.2 keV are excluded)); katayama2004 p. 3 (Sect. 4 (pn: the 7.2-9 keV band is dominated by Cu fluorescence from the electronics board, except in the central ~6’))

Validation: The pn corner level: the cause of the excess (2026-10-28) – 14 observations: PPS pn 4.5-12 keV image / own events with the PPS PI ranges 1.00 (contiguous 0.72); 638 donors: pn template / PPS image 0.90 median, 3.8 per cent above the data

Instrument document: PPS pn image headers, keyword XPROC0 (the evselect call that made the image), PPS 21.51 / SAS 21.0, e.g. P0503490201PNS003IMAGE_5000.FTZ: expression=’(RAWY>12) && GTI(…) && GTI(…) && PATTERN<=4 && (PI in (4500:7800] || PI in (8200:12000]) && (FLAG & 0x2fb0024)==0’; the pn IMAGE_2000/3000/4000 and every MOS image of the same observations select one contiguous PI range

Code: src/tngxmm/instrument/simulate_instrument.py, src/tngxmm/instrument/donor_corner_qpb.py, src/tngxmm/massfit/fit_hydrostatic.py, src/tngxmm/massfit/qpb_template.py

lxalt.temperature_floor¶

Kind: literature

Alternative temperature floors for the emitting gas, in place of T > 3e5 K: 1e5 K (the IllustrisTNG, cosmo-OWLS and FLAMINGO X-ray mocks), 1e6 K (the TNG cool-core studies), kT > 0.1, 0.2, 0.3, 0.5 or 1 keV, and T_vir-scaled floors (0.3 T_vir; max(0.5 T_vir, 5.5 MK), the V2 floor). Floors up to 1 keV change the luminosity at fixed mass by at most -0.017 dex, because gas below 1 keV emits 0.021 of the 0.5-2 keV luminosity inside R500c. The V2 floor lowers it by 0.095 dex and the X-ray-like hydrostatic mass rises by 0.022 dex, so at fixed X-ray-like mass the shift is -0.126 dex; it removes gas of 1-3 keV in the cool cores that an X-ray telescope records, and no paper found uses a T_vir-scaled floor for a luminosity. Floors above 1e6 K have no literature support for a luminosity; the 1e6 K floor has precedent only for cool-core classification.

Value: explored, not adopted: t_min_k 1e5 K to 1 keV, tvir_frac 0.3 and 0.5; production t_min_k = 3e5 K, tvir_frac = 0

Used by: sim.t_min_k, sim.tvir_frac

Evidence: pop2022 p. 3 (Sect. 2 (X-ray mock method of IllustrisTNG groups and clusters: star-forming gas, gas below 1e5 K and gas with a positive cooling rate are excluded)); lebrun2014 p. 5 (Sect. 2.2.2 (cosmo-OWLS synthetic X-ray observations: cold gas below 1e5 K is excluded as it contributes negligibly)); braspenning2024 p. 5 (Sect. 2.5 (FLAMINGO X-ray quantities: a 1e5 K floor on the emitting particles)); barnes2018 p. 3 (Sect. 3 (cool-core criteria of IllustrisTNG clusters: non-star-forming, cooling gas with T > 1e6 K))

lxalt.star_forming_gas¶

Kind: literature

Star-forming (effective equation of state) cells: the production selection excludes them, temet assigns them the 1000 K cold-phase temperature (no emission). Letting them emit at their effective temperature, the upper bound of any share of the hot phase of the two-phase model, changes the luminosity by +0.000 dex: the effective temperatures of the star-forming cells stay below about 1.2e5 K (one halo inspected), where the 0.5-2 keV emissivity is negligible. The hot phase of the model cannot be given an emission measure from the cell’s fields, and no model of it is proposed.

Value: explored, not adopted: star-forming cells at their effective temperature; production: excluded

Used by: sim.t_min_k

Evidence: tngcluster p. 7 (Sect. 2.4 (star-forming gas is assigned its cold-phase temperature and does not contribute to the X-ray signal)); truong2020 p. 22 (Appendix C (X-ray emission of the unresolved phases of star-forming gas in TNG)); springel2003 p. 4 (Sect. 2 (two-phase model: cold clouds at about 1000 K embedded in a hot ambient phase))

lxalt.heated_cells¶

Kind: literature

Cells with net heating (GFM_CoolingRate >= 0) are excluded by the production selection and kept by temet. Keeping them changes the luminosity by +0.000 dex: the net-heating cells are colder than 3e5 K (one halo inspected: 7229 heated cells, none above 3e5 K).

Value: explored, not adopted: heated cells kept; production: excluded

Used by: sim.t_min_k

Evidence: pop2022 p. 3 (Sect. 2 (cells with a positive cooling rate are excluded from the TNG X-ray mocks)); tngcluster p. 7 (Sect. 2.4 (TNG-Cluster luminosities sum every gas cell in the aperture))

lxalt.agn_heated_gas¶

Kind: literature

Gas recently heated by AGN feedback. FLAMINGO removes particles heated by an AGN in the last 15 Myr; TNG snapshots carry no heating time, so that criterion cannot be applied. The FABLE criterion, an upper cut at 4 k T200 = 4 G M200 mu m_p / (2 r200), changes the luminosity by -0.000 dex. Both papers state that their cut has a negligible or rare effect.

Value: explored, not adopted: exclude T > 4 T200; production: no upper cut

Used by: sim.t_min_k

Evidence: braspenning2024 p. 5 (Sect. 2.5 (FLAMINGO: star-forming and recently AGN-heated particles removed, with negligible effect)); henden2019 p. 4 (Sect. 2 (FABLE: AGN-heated bubbles excluded above 4 T200))

lxalt.subhalo_gas¶

Kind: validation

Gas bound to subhaloes other than the central is excluded by the TNG, cosmo-OWLS and BAHAMAS X-ray mocks, as observers excise substructures; the production mocks keep it. Removing it lowers L in the R500c cylinder by 0.001 dex on average (median 0.0001, largest 0.013; 18 TNG-Cluster halos, snapshots 84 and 99).

Value: explored, not adopted: subhalo-bound gas removed; production: kept

Used by: sim.loader

Evidence: pop2022 p. 3 (Sect. 2 (TNG X-ray mocks exclude gas bound to galaxies other than the central)); lebrun2014 p. 5 (Sect. 2.2.2 (gas bound to substructures excluded because observers excise substructures))

Validation: The soft-band luminosity excess of TNG-Cluster – subhalo-bound gas 0.001 dex (tngxmm_dev/LX/followup/subhalo_summary.csv)

lxalt.clump_removal¶

Kind: literature

Removal of dense clumps. (a) 3-D: drop the cells whose log10(n_e n_H) exceeds the median of their radial shell by more than f_cut standard deviations (1.4826 MAD; Zhuravleva et al. 2013, f_cut = 3.5): -0.016 dex; with fixed factors over the shell median, 10x: -0.048, 3x: -0.104. (b) 2-D, as an observer masks substructure: pixels of the projected 0.5-2 keV map above k times the azimuthal median of their annulus replaced by that median, k = 3: -0.057, k = 2: -0.082; the azimuthal-median map: -0.082. (c) The luminosity of the 3-D shell-median density (no clumping at all, sphere): -0.021. The 3-D cut at f_cut = 3.5 was a switch, sim.gas_clump_fcut, off in every preset; it was removed at the integration of 2026-10-30 (an ad hoc cut that the L_X comparison does not need; docs/dev/INTEGRATION_REPORT.md). Zhuravleva et al. call f_cut “rather arbitrary” and apply it to radial profiles, not to a luminosity; no paper found removes clumps from a mock luminosity inside R500c.

Value: explored, not adopted (3-D tail at f_cut 3.5; 2-D masks k = 2, 3); production keeps every clump

Used by: sim.t_min_k

Evidence: zhuravleva2013 p. 7 (Sect. 4 (bulk component and high-density tail: cells above the median plus f_cut standard deviations of log n_e)); zhuravleva2013 p. 4 (Sect. 2 (mean profiles depend on how clumps are removed)); eckert2015clump p. 2 (Sect. 2.1 (simulated shells: the densest 1 per cent of the volume masked as clumps)); eckert2015clump p. 6 (Sect. 4 (observed clumping at R500: sqrt(C) = 1.07 +- 0.05)); pop2022 p. 11 (Sect. 4.2 (TNG cluster luminosities high at fixed mass; clumps named as a possible cause))

lxalt.entropy_cut¶

Kind: validation

Removal of low-entropy gas, K = kT n_e^-2/3 below 10, 30 or 100 keV cm^2: -0.023, -0.056, -0.169 dex. The cut removes the gas of cool cores, which observed clusters also contain (strong cool cores have central entropies below 22 keV cm^2, the Hudson et al. 2010 threshold of viz.core_class), so it removes real X-ray gas; the large spread between halos comes from the cool-core halos. No paper was found that removes low-entropy gas from a mock X-ray luminosity; the cut was not adopted, so nothing rests on it.

Value: explored, not adopted; no spec field

Used by: sim.t_min_k

Validation: – K > 30 keV cm^2: -0.056 dex at 5e14 Msun

lxalt.metallicity_and_table¶

Kind: validation

Metallicity and emission table. temet’s metal unit 0.023 instead of 0.019366: -0.008 dex. Metallicity from Fe alone (X_Fe over the AG89 Fe mass fraction): -0.016. A uniform 0.3 Z_AG89: +0.004. X_H = 0.76 in n_H instead of the cell’s X_H: +0.012. temet’s own emission table (AtomDB 3.0.9, one line per element and energy bin) instead of the complete APEC 3.1.3 table: -0.021; temet’s luminosity of every cell (its table, unit and cell set): -0.014. The complete table is the physical APEC emission; temet’s misses line emission below 2 keV (sim/build_apec_table.py), so returning to it would lower the luminosity by removing photons that plasma emits.

Value: explored, not adopted; production table_metal_unit = 0.019366, apec_version 3.1.3 complete table

Used by: sim.table_metal_unit, sim.apec_version

Validation: Reference-table metal axis – temet table: -0.021 dex at 5e14 Msun; metal unit 0.023: -0.008 dex

lxalt.geometry_and_aperture¶

Kind: validation

Projection and aperture. Sphere of R500c instead of the cylinder: -0.026 dex. Cylinder depth +-1 R500c: -0.015; +-2 R500c: -0.009 (against +-3 R500c). The cylinder of the X-ray-like R500 (smaller than the true R500c): -0.012. Core excision (0.15-1 R500c): -0.248 dex on the TNG side; against the core-excised observed relations the offset falls from 0.30 to 0.23 dex (mean of Bulbul+19, Lovisari+20, Pratt+09 at b = 0).

Value: explored; production cylinder R500c, depth of the mock cube

Used by: sim.los_depth_r500, sim.gas_radius_r500

Evidence: bulbul2019 p. 5 (Sect. 3 (observables in (0.15-1) R500 and (0-1) R500))

Validation: – core-excised comparison: offset 0.30 -> 0.23 dex

lxalt.observed_relations¶

Kind: literature

The observed side of the comparison. Two of the three reference relations rest on hydrostatic-type masses (Lovisari+20 hydrostatic; Pratt+09 Y_X calibrated on hydrostatic masses), so comparing them with TNG at the true M500c and b = 0 is the most lenient reading; the relation on SZ masses calibrated on weak lensing (Bulbul+19) gives +0.41 dex at z = 0.2 and +0.40 at its pivot z = 0.45, so its redshift extrapolation is not the cause. Pratt+09’s Malmquist-corrected relation raises the offset by 0.06 dex; Lovisari+20 is corrected for the Eddington bias and needs no Malmquist correction for an SZ sample. The band conversion 0.1-2.4 to 0.5-2 keV (1.62 against 1.66) moves 0.01 dex; the Hubble constant (0.70 against 0.6774) moves 0.003 dex. No observational-side mismatch found lowers the offset.

Value: comparison at true M500c, b = 0, z = 0.2; references Bulbul+19, Lovisari+20, Pratt+09

Used by: sim.emin_lx_kev, sim.emax_lx_kev

Evidence: pratt2009 p. 2 (Sect. 2 (R500 and masses from the Y_X mass proxy, not from each cluster’s hydrostatic profile)); lovisari2020 p. 5 (Sect. 4 (the fitted relations are corrected for the Eddington bias; the Malmquist bias is negligible for an SZ-selected sample)); bulbul2019 p. 5 (Sect. 3 (apertures of the SPT relations))

xsim.common_definition¶

Kind: validation

One definition for every simulation: the rest-frame 0.5-2 keV luminosity of the gas inside the 3-D sphere of R500c (and the shell 0.15-1 R500c) about the halo centre, computed with the production emission (the APEC 3.1.3 reference table at Z / 0.019366, the cell’s own X_H, x_e and T, T > 3e5 K, non-star-forming), against the true M500c of the halo catalogue; the gas fraction is the gas above 1e5 K inside R500c over M500c. The sphere is the one aperture all four sources provide (FLAMINGO’s SOAP catalogue has no R500c cylinder); the projected cylinder is brighter by +0.011 dex (median over the TNG-format halos), which raises every offset from the projected observed relations by that amount. The net-cooling cut is applied only to TNG (SIMBA and EAGLE carry a cooling-rate field that their conversion readme declares not available); with it, SIMBA and EAGLE change by 0.000 and 0.000 dex.

Value: 0.5-2 keV rest frame, sphere R500c and shell 0.15-1 R500c, true M500c, z = 0 and 0.2 pooled

Used by: sim.emin_lx_kev, sim.emax_lx_kev, sim.t_min_k

Validation: – cylinder - sphere +0.011 dex; cooling-rate cut on SIMBA / EAGLE 0.000 / 0.000 dex

xsim.flamingo¶

Kind: literature

FLAMINGO L1000N0900 HYDRO_FIDUCIAL (public data release): every central halo with M500c > 1e14 Msun at z = 0 and 0.2 from the SOAP SO/500_crit catalogues (TotalMass, HotGasMass, the rest-frame luminosities of the third band, ROSAT 0.5-2 keV, total, core-excised and without recently AGN-heated gas). FLAMINGO’s luminosities come from Cloudy-based tables with density and per-element dependence, T > 1e5 K, non-star-forming particles. Recomputed with our emission on the particles of 37 halos at z = 0, ours is +0.044 dex above SOAP. At z = 0.2 the SOAP observer-frame value is (1+z)^3 = 1.73 times the sum of the snapshot’s own particle luminosities (median +0.239 dex against 0.238, 36 halos; the gas masses agree to 1e-4), our rest-frame emission on the same particles is +0.038 dex above the SOAP rest-frame value divided by (1+z)^3, and with that factor removed the FLAMINGO relation evolves from z = 0 to 0.2 by about E(z)^2 (+0.08 dex). The comparison uses our emission on FLAMINGO (SOAP times the measured per-snapshot ratio). FLAMINGO is calibrated to observed cluster gas fractions (X-ray masses with a hydrostatic-bias prior, and weak lensing) and the stellar mass function.

Value: SOAP SO/500_crit, band index 2 (ROSAT 0.5-2 keV), rest frame; ratio ours/SOAP per snapshot

Used by: sim.emin_lx_kev, sim.emax_lx_kev

Evidence: flamingo_release2026 p. 1 (Abstract (public release of the FLAMINGO data)); flamingo_release2026 p. 8 (Sect. 3 (diffuse X-ray products: sums over gas particles with 1e5 <= T/K <= 10^9.5 in the eROSITA and ROSAT bands)); braspenning2024 p. 4 (Sect. 2.5 (FLAMINGO X-ray emissivity tables from Cloudy, the ROSAT 0.5-2 keV band)); schaye2023 p. 1 (Abstract (FLAMINGO feedback calibrated to the stellar mass function and the cluster gas fractions)); kugel2023 p. 6 (Sect. 3 (calibration data)); kugel2023 p. 9 (Sect. 3.2.1 (X-ray gas fractions with a hydrostatic-bias term, prior N(0.74, 0.10)))

Validation: – ours/SOAP +0.044 dex at z = 0; at z = 0.2 SOAP/particle sum +0.239 dex = (1+z)^3, ours/(SOAP/(1+z)^3) +0.038 dex

xsim.simba_eagle¶

Kind: literature

SIMBA (100 Mpc/h, L100n1024FP) and EAGLE (Ref-L0100N1504, L68n1504FP) through their conversion to the TNG format on Vera (the conversion re-ran Subfind; its readme marks the SIMBA conversion experimental and asks users to contact its author before use; the data are public). An internal comparison: the author of the conversion has not been contacted. Luminosities are recomputed with our emission on the FoF gas (V = m / rho for the particles); no wind or multiphase flag beyond the star-formation rate is used.

Value: SIMBA snapshots 151 and 140 (z = 0, 0.19), EAGLE 28 and 26 (z = 0, 0.18), M500c > 5e13 Msun

Used by: sim.emin_lx_kev

Evidence: dave2019 p. 1 (Abstract (the 100 Mpc/h, 1024^3 gas-element SIMBA run)); schaye2015 p. 7 (Table 2 (EAGLE box sizes and resolutions)); eagle2017 p. 1 (Title page (EAGLE particle-data release))

xsim.observed_fgas¶

Kind: literature

The observed gas fraction drawn in the f_gas panel: f_gas(r500) h70^1.5 = 0.128 +- 0.009 from weak-lensing masses (Mantz+16, clusters only), 0.134 in the h = 0.6774 cosmology, flat in mass (slope of M_gas-M 1.004 +- 0.014).

Value: f_gas,500 = 0.134 +- 0.009 (h = 0.6774)

Used by: sim.emin_lx_kev

Evidence: mantz2016 p. 9 (Table 3 (f_gas(r500) h70^gamma_g, clusters only))

multisim.descriptor¶

Kind: convention

Every simulation enters through a descriptor (src/tngxmm/ingest/descriptors/<name>.yml) that states its cosmology, snapshots, field mapping, star-forming, wind and net-heating rules and metallicity source, and through the canonical datasets the ingest stage writes from it: the halo table and the cube index files (TNG family; the cells are converted to the canonical gas in memory) or materialised canonical cubes (FLAMINGO, and SIMBA and EAGLE from their original snapshots). A preset that names a descriptor (sim.source) carries its sha256 in the spec hash; the TNG presets name none.

Value: descriptors flamingo, flamingo_l1m8, simba and eagle for the presets v1_flamingo, v1_flamingo_l1m8, v1_simba and v1_eagle; none for the TNG presets

Used by: sim.source, ingest.build_canonical_cubes

multisim.generation_cosmology¶

Kind: literature

The generation of a simulation’s mocks (luminosity and angular-diameter distance at z_obs, the critical density of its R500c and of the hydrostatic truth) uses that simulation’s own cosmology from its descriptor; every fit, mock and real, uses one analysis cosmology (cosmology.planck15). FLAMINGO’s massive neutrinos are counted as matter in a flat LCDM (Omega_m = 0.306), adequate below z = 1.6.

Value: TNG: H0 67.74, Om 0.3089; FLAMINGO: 68.1, 0.306; SIMBA: 68, 0.3; EAGLE: 67.77, 0.307; fits: 67.74, 0.3089

Used by: sim.source, cosmology.h0_km_s_mpc, cosmology.om0

Evidence: flamingo p. 15 (Schaye et al. 2023, Table 1 (fiducial cosmology: h, Omega_m = 0.306, Omega_b, sigma8)); simba p. 9 (Davé et al. 2019, Sect. 2.6 (Omega_m = 0.3, Omega_Lambda = 0.7, Omega_b = 0.048, H0 = 68, sigma8 = 0.82, ns = 0.97)); eagle p. 6 (Schaye et al. 2015, Table 1 (Planck 2013 parameters adopted by EAGLE))

multisim.star_forming¶

Kind: literature

Star-forming gas is excluded from the emission and from the non-star-forming phase of the truth by each model’s own flag, a positive instantaneous star formation rate: TNG and EAGLE (gas on the effective equation of state), SIMBA (its H2-based star formation rate), FLAMINGO (the StarFormationRates field; a negative value there stores the last star-forming scale factor and counts as not star forming). The FLAMINGO X-ray analysis removes the same particles.

Value: star forming: SFR > 0 in every simulation (canonical flag bit 0)

Used by: sim.source, sim.t_min_k

Evidence: braspenning2024 p. 5 (Braspenning et al. 2024, Sect. 2.5 (selection of the gas of the X-ray quantities)); simba p. 4 (Davé et al. 2019, Sect. 2.1); eagle p. 11 (Schaye et al. 2015, Sect. 4.3 (star-forming gas held on the equation of state P_eos))

multisim.simba_winds¶

Kind: literature

SIMBA launches star-formation-driven winds as decoupled particles (hydrodynamics and cooling switched off for a short time). They are excluded like star-forming gas: the original GIZMO snapshots carry DelayTime, positive while a particle is decoupled, and the ingest sets the wind flag where DelayTime > 0. The TNG-format conversion on Vera (read only for the halo catalogue) wrote the same particles as PartType4, as TNG writes its own wind phase, so they did not emit there either.

Value: wind flag: DelayTime > 0 for SIMBA (original snapshots); no wind particles in the other models

Used by: sim.source

Evidence: simba p. 4 (Davé et al. 2019, Sect. 2.3); simba p. 7 (Davé et al. 2019, Sect. 2.4 (decoupling time 1e-4 t_H))

Validation: Multi-simulation pipeline – fixture halos 7 and 2 (snapshot 140): 1929 and 6638 gas particles with DelayTime > 0, exactly the gas the conversion lacks and the stars it has in excess; every matched gas particle bit-identical; L_X(0.5-2 keV, R500c) identical (internal/ledger_tests/multisim/orig_vs_tngformat.py)

multisim.net_heating¶

Kind: validation

The net-heating cut of the TNG emission (GFM_CoolingRate >= 0 excluded) is applied only where the cooling rate is the model’s own output (TNG). SIMBA and EAGLE carry a converted GFM_CoolingRate that their readmes call not available, and SWIFT writes none for FLAMINGO; there no cell is cut as heated. FLAMINGO’s own X-ray analysis removes gas heated by AGN in the last 15 Myr; we keep it. The truth estimators that read GFM_CoolingRate as a diagnostic (the X-ray-image cells of barnes21_xcut and barnes21_tsl, the spectroscopic-like temperature inside R500c and the mock-phase cells, sim.compute_hse_mass) read the converted field in the P2 route (DECISIONS “P2: SIMBA and EAGLE”), where the SIMBA and EAGLE truth was computed from the TNG-format conversions with campaign truth-sphere. P2b replaced that route: the presets v1_simba and v1_eagle compute the truth from the cubes of the original snapshots, which carry no cooling rate, so these estimators count no cell as heated, and the conversions supply only the halo catalogue. The headline truth reads no cooling rate.

Value: TNG: GFM_CoolingRate < 0 required; SIMBA, EAGLE, FLAMINGO: no heating cut

Used by: sim.source

Evidence: braspenning2024 p. 5 (Braspenning et al. 2024, Sect. 2.5)

Validation: Multi-simulation pipeline – FLAMINGO L_X(0.5-2 keV, R500c) without the recently AGN-heated gas changes by -0.0001 dex (z = 0) and -0.0007 dex (z = 0.2); SIMBA and EAGLE with the converted cooling-rate cut: 0.000 dex (branch lx-gas-explore, docs/dev/LX_CROSS_SIM.md)

multisim.temperature_ionisation¶

Kind: literature

The temperature and the electron abundance are the model’s own where the snapshots store them: TNG and SIMBA from the stored internal energy and electron abundance (T = (gamma - 1) u mu m_p / k_B with the cell’s X_H); EAGLE the stored temperature (from its cooling tables) and internal energy, with the electron abundance of full ionisation of the particle’s smoothed composition, because EAGLE stores none (entry multisim.eagle_electron_abundance); FLAMINGO the stored temperature and electron density, which come from the Ploeckinger and Schaye (2020) tables at the particle’s density, temperature and smoothed abundances.

Value: TNG, SIMBA: u and x_e; EAGLE: Temperature, InternalEnergy, x_e of full ionisation; FLAMINGO: Temperatures, ElectronNumberDensities (u derived for the truth)

Used by: sim.source

Evidence: flamingo p. 6 (Schaye et al. 2023, Sect. 2.2 (radiative cooling and heating element by element from Ploeckinger & Schaye 2020)); ploeckinger2020 p. 14 (Ploeckinger & Schaye 2020, Table 5 (the tabulated electron fractions)); simba p. 4 (Davé et al. 2019, Sect. 2.1 (cooling and ionisation from Grackle))

multisim.eagle_electron_abundance¶

Kind: validation

EAGLE’s snapshots store the temperature but no electron abundance. The canonical x_e of an EAGLE particle is that of a fully ionised gas of its smoothed composition, sum_i (X_i / A_i) Z_i over X_H / A_H, the untracked metals at Z / A = 0.5. The emission uses x_e only for gas above 3e5 K that is not star forming, where H and He are fully ionised and the metals carry a small share of the electrons, so their partial ionisation hardly matters. Colder gas gets the same rule but emits nothing.

Value: x_e = full ionisation of the smoothed composition (tngxmm.ingest.eagle_remote.electron_abundance_full)

Used by: sim.source

Validation: Multi-simulation pipeline – EAGLE halo 4, snapshot 26, emitting gas inside R500c (8.96e6 particles): the metals carry 0.14 per cent of the electrons (median; 0.34 per cent emission-weighted, 0.9 per cent at the 99th percentile); median x_e 1.1708 against 1.1692 for H and He alone; L_X(0.5-2 keV, R500c) +0.0027 dex against the TNG-format conversion’s x_e (internal/ledger_tests/multisim/orig_vs_tngformat.py)

Code: src/tngxmm/ingest/eagle_remote.py

multisim.abundance_modes¶

Kind: validation

Two emission abundance modes, hashed: ‘full’ (He and every metal the simulation tracks per cell; the untracked metals at solar ratios scaled by the remaining metal mass) and ‘common’ (total Z with AG89 ratios for every element Li-Zn, He at the solar value of the fit’s table), each per hydrogen atom. Production default (author, 2026-10-28): ‘full’ in every preset; ‘common’ is the option of v1_common (it alone serves the TNG mini snapshots, which carry no element fractions and are refused by ‘full’). A verification subset is run in both modes.

Value: sim.emission_abundances = full; sim.emission_untracked = other-solar | v1_common: common

Used by: sim.emission_abundances, sim.emission_untracked

Validation: Multi-simulation pipeline – 20 halos per simulation: L_X(0.5-2 keV) full/common within 0.013 dex for clusters; fitted T full/common +0.3 (TNG-Cluster) to +10 per cent (SIMBA); common/legacy -0.016 to -0.024 dex, the helium of the reference table

sim.snapshot_fields¶

Kind: literature

Under v1_common every TNG snapshot, full or mini, is read with the fields of a mini snapshot: the total metallicity, but no element fractions and no net cooling rate. The hydrogen mass fraction is then the primordial X_H = 0.76 of the TNG initial conditions, in the temperature and in the emission measure, and no cell is cut as net heating, so one gas cell gives the same emission at both kinds of snapshot. With each cell’s own hydrogen at the full snapshots (0.755 in the median), the views of mini snapshots would be 0.0085 dex brighter than those of full snapshots for the same gas (DECISIONS “P4”), an offset that would follow |z_obs - z_snap|, the quantity v1_common exists to vary. The price is a uniform 2 per cent in L_X against the gas’s own hydrogen. The rule is the emission’s only: the truth step reads every field, so the diagnostic barnes21_mock (the truth on the mock’s cells) keeps the net-heating cut at the full snapshots of v1_common. Every other preset reads every field (the option is unset).

Value: sim.snapshot_fields = unset (v1 and every preset but v1_common); v1_common: mini (X_H 0.76, no net-heating cut, at all 60 snapshots)

Used by: sim.snapshot_fields

Evidence: pillepich2018 p. 4 (Sect. 2.2.4 (the hydrogen and helium mass fractions are initialised to 0.76 and 0.24)); nelson2019_tngdr p. 7 (Sect. 3.1 (full and mini snapshots))

multisim.helium¶

Kind: literature

Helium. ‘common’ mode: He/H at the solar value of the abundance table of the generator and the fit, Lodders (2003), the table the fitter sets with XSPEC abund lodd: n(He)/n(H) = 0.0793 (A(He) = 10.899), stored by XSPEC (and SOXS) as 7.92e-2. ‘full’ mode: each simulation’s own tracked helium per cell (TNG, FLAMINGO, EAGLE and SIMBA all track He; see multisim.tracked_elements); a simulation that tracks metals but not helium would get the Lodders solar He/H (code rule of tngxmm.sim.abundances.components); a snapshot without element fractions (TNG mini snapshots) is refused by ‘full’. For comparison, Anders & Grevesse (1989) give A(He) = 10.99 (He/H 9.77e-2): the AG89 helium is 0.09 dex above Lodders, the -0.016 to -0.024 dex of L_X between ‘common’ and the legacy table.

Value: He/H 7.92e-2 (lodd) in 'common'; the simulation's He in 'full'

Used by: sim.emission_abundances, sim.apec_abund_table, massfit.abund_table

Evidence: lodders2003 p. 10 (Lodders 2003, Table 4 (helium abundance and mass fractions), last row: present-day solar n(He)/n(H) = 0.0793, A(He) = 10.899 (this work)); lodders2003 p. 10 (Lodders 2003, Table 4, row (1): Anders & Grevesse 1989, A(He) = 10.99 (the comparison)); heasoft_xspec p. 210 (XSPEC 12.15.0 manual, Sect. 5.8.1 abund: the table lodd is Lodders (2003)); heasoft_xspec p. 207 (XSPEC 12.15.0 manual, Sect. 5.8.1 abund: the table angr is AG89 (photospheric, Table 2))

Validation: Multi-simulation pipeline – XSPEC 12.15.0 spectral/manager/abundances.dat (HEASoft 6.35.1 image): lodd He 7.92e-02 (Lodders, K. ApJ 591, 1220 (2003)), angr He 9.77e-02; SOXS 4.8.5 abund_tables the same

multisim.apec_elements¶

Kind: literature

APEC (AtomDB) gives the emission of every element from H to Zn separately, and an element’s abundance scales its spectrum linearly relative to the tabulated solar value. The emission of a cell is therefore the H spectrum plus the spectra of He..Zn, each weighted by the cell’s abundance of that element per hydrogen atom in solar units of the table (lodd), as XSPEC’s vvapec does with all 30 elements; the AtomDB emissivities are tabulated for the AG89 abundances and rescaled to the table in use. The elements a simulation does not track enter at solar ratios, scaled by its remaining metal mass (‘other-solar’).

Value: per-element APEC spectra He..Zn (SOXS var_elem), weights in lodd solar units

Used by: sim.emission_abundances, sim.emission_untracked, sim.apec_version

Evidence: atomdb p. 3 (Foster et al. 2012, Sect. 2 (AtomDB v2.0.2 covers the elements H to Zn)); heasoft_xspec p. 239 (XSPEC 12.15.0 manual, Sect. 6.2.4 apec, vapec, vvapec: the vvapec abundances H..Zn wrt Solar (defined by the abund command)); heasoft_xspec p. 512 (XSPEC manual, version history: vvapec allows all 30 elemental abundances to vary (AtomDB 2.0)); pyatomdb_docs p. 43 (PyAtomDB documentation (release 0.0.2.5), spectrum module, make_spectrum: elements and abund, each element’s abundance relative to AG89)

multisim.tracked_elements¶

Kind: literature

The elements each simulation tracks per gas cell, and the fields they are read from. TNG (all runs): H, He, C, N, O, Ne, Mg, Si, Fe and a tenth ‘other metals’ column (GFM_Metals; the mini snapshots carry a subset of fields and no element fractions). FLAMINGO: the same nine, SPH-smoothed (SmoothedElementMassFractions); S and Ca are not tracked (its cooling ties them to Si). EAGLE: the same nine (SmoothedElementAbundance of the original snapshots; S and Ca tied to Si). SIMBA tracks eleven (Metallicity of the original snapshots, with S and Ca), all kept in the canonical cells. In ‘full’ mode every tracked element is its own component; S and Ca, where not tracked, enter at solar ratios scaled by the remaining metal mass, not tied to Si as FLAMINGO’s and EAGLE’s own cooling assumes.

Value: tracked in the canonical cells: H, He, C, N, O, Ne, Mg, Si, Fe (every simulation); S and Ca as well for SIMBA

Used by: sim.emission_abundances, sim.source

Evidence: pillepich2018 p. 3 (Pillepich et al. 2018, Sect. 2 (TNG follows nine elements)); pillepich2018 p. 4 (Pillepich et al. 2018, Sect. 2 (the tenth ‘other metals’ species)); nelson2019_tngdr p. 9 (Nelson et al. 2019, Sect. 3 (GFM_Metals: the element abundances of the nine tracked species)); nelson2019_tngdr p. 7 (Nelson et al. 2019, Sect. 3 (full and mini snapshots: the mini snapshots have a subset of the fields)); flamingo p. 7 (Schaye et al. 2023, Sect. 2.3.1 (the tracked elements; Ca and S tied to Si)); eagle p. 11 (Schaye et al. 2015, Sect. 4.4 (nine elements tracked individually, Ca and S at fixed ratios to Si)); eagle_particles p. 15 (The EAGLE team 2017, Table (gas particle fields): ElementAbundance and SmoothedElementAbundance of C, He, H, Fe, Mg, Ne, N, O, Si); simba p. 4 (Davé et al. 2019, Sect. 2.3 (eleven elements, H, He, C, N, O, Ne, Mg, Si, S, Ca, Fe))

Validation: Multi-simulation pipeline – FLAMINGO snapshot SubgridScheme/NamedColumns/SmoothedElementMassFractions = Hydrogen, Helium, Carbon, Nitrogen, Oxygen, Neon, Magnesium, Silicon, Iron (L1000N0900 snapshot 73)

multisim.mode_verification¶

Kind: literature

The verification of the abundance modes compares each view’s fitted-like temperature and metallicity in the two modes: a single-temperature APEC fitted to the rest-frame 0.5-7 keV spectrum of the cluster photons inside the projected R500, with one abundance for C, N, O, Ne, Mg, Al, Si, S, Ar, Ca, Fe and Ni, helium and the trace elements at the solar values of the table (lodd), the convention of XSPEC’s apec, so the fit sees a mode’s helium and metal pattern as an X-ray fit would. The medians of the paired differences carry 95 per cent bootstrap intervals that resample halo-snapshots (the views of one halo-snapshot together), the method of the realism evaluation’s donor bootstrap (method.c2st_donor_bootstrap).

Value: fitted-like T, Z: 1T APEC lodd, abundance of C..Ni, He solar; bootstrap over halo-snapshots, 2,000 draws

Used by: sim.emission_abundances, sim.apec_abund_table

Evidence: heasoft_xspec p. 239 (XSPEC 12.15.0 manual, Sect. 6.2.4 apec: one metal abundance for these elements, He at the abund table’s value)

multisim.cell_resample¶

Kind: literature

The diagnostic arm sim.cell_resample_msun degrades a moving-mesh run to SPH particles of a given mass: cell i is kept with probability m_i / M and its mass divided by that probability (no tuned constant: the expectation of every extensive quantity is unchanged), and a cell without a smoothing length gets the one of an SPH particle of the new mass with 58 weighted neighbours inside the Wendland C2 support H = 1.936492 h, the kernel and neighbour number of FLAMINGO. Unset in every preset.

Value: sim.cell_resample_msun unset in every preset; 58 weighted neighbours, H = 1.936492 h

Used by: sim.cell_resample_msun

Evidence: flamingo p. 5 (Schaye et al. 2023, Sect. 2.1 and footnote 3 (Wendland C2 kernel, eta = 1.23483, 58 weighted neighbours)); dehnen2012 p. 3 (Dehnen & Aly 2012, Table 1 (Wendland C2 in three dimensions: H/h = 1.936492))

multisim.smoothed_metallicity¶

Kind: literature

FLAMINGO and EAGLE give the element fractions as SPH-smoothed values (FLAMINGO writes no particle element fractions; EAGLE writes both and its cooling uses the smoothed ones); the canonical gas takes the smoothed element fractions and total metallicity and keeps the particle total beside it (Z_particle).

Value: Z and X_el smoothed (FLAMINGO, EAGLE); Z_particle stored for FLAMINGO and EAGLE

Used by: sim.source

Evidence: eagle p. 12 (Schaye et al. 2015, Sect. 4.3 (smoothed abundances in the cooling rates))

Validation: Multi-simulation pipeline – particle against smoothed Z: FLAMINGO L_X -0.001 dex, T_fit -0.1 per cent; EAGLE Recal-L025N0752 groups L_X +0.002 dex, T_fit +2 to +4 per cent

multisim.centre_mass¶

Kind: literature

The centre is the potential minimum and M500c, R500c are the catalogue’s spherical-overdensity values: Subfind GroupPos and Group_M/R_Crit500 for the TNG family and for SIMBA and EAGLE (Subfind re-run on their TNG-format conversions, the same particles as the original snapshots read for the cubes), the VELOCIraptor most bound particle (VR/CentreOfPotential) and SOAP SO/500_crit for FLAMINGO.

Value: TNG family: GroupPos, Group_M_Crit500; FLAMINGO: VR/CentreOfPotential, SO/500_crit/TotalMass and SORadius

Used by: sim.source

Evidence: soap p. 2 (McGibbon et al. 2025, Sect. 2 (spherical-overdensity halo properties)); velociraptor p. 20 (Elahi et al. 2019, Sect. 6 (halo centre definitions))

multisim.truth_on_cubes¶

Kind: validation

The intrinsic hydrostatic truth of every simulation is the spherical truth of truth.sphere (every particle within r_cut of the centre, the same estimator and keywords): read from the snapshot for the TNG family, computed from the materialised canonical cube for the presets that read canonical cubes: FLAMINGO and, since P2b, SIMBA and EAGLE from their original snapshots; the cube must hold the whole sphere. In the P2 route, which P2b replaced, the SIMBA and EAGLE truth was read from their TNG-format conversions (DECISIONS “P2: SIMBA and EAGLE”); the conversions now supply only the halo catalogue.

Value: campaign truth-sphere for the TNG family; tngxmm.ingest.truth.cube_truth_hse in the generation of v1_flamingo, v1_flamingo_l1m8, v1_simba and v1_eagle

Used by: sim.truth_selection_hse, sim.source

Validation: Multi-simulation pipeline – TNG-Cluster fixture halo: canonical-cube truth against the snapshot truth, largest relative difference over the 108 datasets 3.3e-7, headline 2e-8

multisim.resolution_floor¶

Kind: validation

The number of emitting gas particles inside R500c is reported per simulation; FLAMINGO’s images are used only after its resolution and image-realism test (phase P3).

Value: emitting particles inside R500c at 1e14 Msun: TNG-Cluster 9.8e5, TNG300 1.2e6, FLAMINGO 967

Used by: sim.source

Validation: Multi-simulation pipeline – FLAMINGO 967 emitting gas particles inside R500c at 1.0e14 Msun, 2.2e4 at 1.5e15 Msun (z = 0.2)

multisim.photon_spread¶

Kind: literature

Each photon of a cell or particle is placed at the particle position plus an offset: uniformly in the sphere of the volume-equivalent radius (3 m / 4 pi rho)^(1/3) (every TNG product), or, as an option for SPH runs, from the 3-D Wendland C2 kernel of support 1.936492 times the snapshot smoothing length, the kernel FLAMINGO uses for its own maps. The option is a hashed switch, unset in every preset; its effect on the FLAMINGO images is measured in P3.

Value: sim.photon_spread unset (uniform in the volume-equivalent sphere) in every preset

Used by: sim.photon_spread

Evidence: dehnen2012 p. 3 (Dehnen & Aly 2012, Table 1 (Wendland C2 in three dimensions: H/h = 1.936492)); flamingo p. 42 (Schaye et al. 2023, Appendix A2 (the kernel of the FLAMINGO maps, R = h gamma))

method.closure_acceptance_rule¶

Kind: validation

The closure grid B1 is judged on pooled medians of ln(M500_fit / M500_true) over the usable fits (status ok, no flag of fit_qc.QC_EXCLUDING) of each background, with a bootstrap 95 per cent interval. Clean cells, the gate: the interval inside 0.95-1.05 and every redshift median in 0.90-1.10. Real cells, the gate: at most 5 per cent of the ok fits outside 0.67-1.5. Real cells, the hydrostatic-bias claim only: the median in 0.92-1.08 and every redshift median in 0.85-1.15. More than 10 per cent failed fits FAIL; a missing fit or more than 10 per cent pivot starts are INCOMPLETE; a redshift with fewer than half of its fits usable leaves the medians. The windows are DESIGN v2’s, set before any v2 fit and restored before any B1 fit; no published closure threshold exists for a mock-image pipeline, so they are ours, placed against the precision of the XMM-Newton hydrostatic masses they are compared with. The per-cell rule 1.00 +- 0.03 is a report. The confirmation run takes the same windows and R3p’s half-width 0.07. Disclosed: the domain rule, the QC exclusion of closure fits and the pooled form were proposed after the per-cell rule and the z = 0.06 cells of earlier configurations were known to fail; every median is also reported with all ok fits and every redshift.

Value: CLOSURE_RULE: clean 0.95-1.05 (interval), z 0.90-1.10; real tail 5 % outside 0.67-1.5; hse claim 0.92-1.08 (median), z 0.85-1.15; fail 10 %, pivot 10 %; R3p 0.07 with 192 fresh views and up to two blocks

Used by: validation.analyse_closure_grid, certify.apply_certification_rules

Evidence: ettori2019 p. 1 (Abstract (X-COP: the systematic uncertainties of the hydrostatic masses from several methods, about 5 per cent at R500 and R200)); nelson2014 p. 9 (Sect. 4, Fig. 6 discussion (on simulated clusters, the reconstruction that adds gas velocities and acceleration to the hydrostatic term recovers the true mass to within 5 per cent at all radii)); pratt2019 p. 13 (Sect. 3.2.2 (the systematic uncertainties of a hydrostatic mass in percentages; the hydrostatic bias least important, below 10 per cent, in relaxed objects)); chexmate2021 p. 1 (Abstract (CHEX-MATE exposures designed for individual hydrostatic masses accurate to 15-20 per cent))

Validation: – 54 fits per background; at a per-fit scatter of 0.05 an unbiased clean grid passes with probability near 1, one 4 per cent off with 0.26; at 0.1 the real median has a standard error of 0.017 (a true 0.92 passes the claim window half the time)

Code: src/tngxmm/validation/closure_grid.py, src/tngxmm/certify/rules.py

method.study_arm_rule¶

Kind: validation

Every paired study arm of the closure grid is judged by one rule: the median of ln M500(arm) - ln M500(base) over the real-background pairs whose two fits are usable, with a bootstrap 95 per cent interval, is NEGLIGIBLE inside +-0.03, SYSTEMATIC when it reaches beyond and excludes 0 (MATERIAL when the median exceeds 0.03), INCONCLUSIVE when it reaches beyond and includes 0. The arm’s role (error, pair, range, bound, decision, check) says how a verdict enters the budget; terms combine in quadrature. Re-seed arms of the gate arm measure the power: if one is not NEGLIGIBLE, seeds are added and the threshold stays. 0.03 is DESIGN v2’s MATERIAL line and equals the median statistical error of the X-COP masses at R500; it is ours.

Value: ARM_RULE delta 0.03, real cells, >= 27 pairs; ARM_BASE, ARM_ROLE; power check v1sas_r2, v1sas_r3

Used by: validation.analyse_closure_grid

Evidence: ettori2019 p. 1 (Abstract (X-COP: hydrostatic masses with a 3 per cent median statistical error at R500)); pratt2019 p. 13 (Sect. 3.2.2 (spherical symmetry and gas clumping each a few per cent of a hydrostatic mass))

Validation: – at 54 pairs the half-width is about 0.33 s; an arm with no effect is NEGLIGIBLE with probability 1.0 / 0.88 / 0.42 / 0 for a paired scatter s = 0.03 / 0.05 / 0.07 / 0.09

Code: src/tngxmm/validation/closure_grid.py

method.sampler_rule¶

Kind: validation

The production sampler is chosen by the Q18 job under a rule written before it: against the long chains with the differential-evolution move and the scaled start (v1sas_delong, itself not converged), a candidate is eligible when its paired difference is NEGLIGIBLE under the arm rule and its Monte-Carlo scatter (1.4826 MAD of a fit minus its re-seed, over sqrt 2) is at most 0.015 in ln M500; converged when every fit has R-hat < 1.01 and bulk ESS > 400. A converged eligible candidate is chosen first, then the eligible one with the smallest scatter (the cheaper of two within 0.005, by the cost table of SAMPLER_RULE), then the reference if its own scatter is within the limit. The QC column chain_lp_rising cuts nothing: a chain run for too few autocorrelation times is answered by the sampler, not by dropping the fit; its rate is compared between mocks and real fits and every science median is given with and without it.

Value: SAMPLER_RULE: reference v1sas_delong; candidates v1sas, v1sas_de, v1sas_scaled, v1sas_long with \*_r2 repeats; max Monte-Carlo scatter 0.015; R-hat 1.01, ESS 400; chain_lp_rising in no cut

Used by: validation.analyse_closure_grid, massfit.fit_hydrostatic

Evidence: vehtari2021 p. 4 (Recommendations (the numerical targets for R-hat and ESS are first checks; domain expertise decides whether the Monte-Carlo errors are small enough)); vehtari2021 p. 6 (The effective sample size and Monte Carlo standard error (whether an MCSE is small enough requires domain expertise)); emcee p. 11 (p. 11 (run the sampler for a few autocorrelation times; too few steps is a common mistake))

Validation: – pilot (4 cells, 2 seeds): seed rms of ln M500 0.121 (stretch, ball), 0.044 (DE), 0.023 (scaled), 0.033 (both); 0.015 is half the X-COP statistical error and the SENS tolerance

Code: src/tngxmm/validation/closure_grid.py, src/tngxmm/massfit/fit_qc.py

fit.soft_proton_band_tie¶

Kind: literature

A hashed switch, unset in every preset (massfit.sp_band_tie, fit-fix sp_bands = ‘tied’, closure arm v1_sptie), gives the residual soft protons of each camera one free level and the bands the ratios of the Leccardi & Molendi (2008) broken power law in counts, the X-COP spectral model of this component and the spectrum the mocks draw. Production takes it only if v1_sptie is SYSTEMATIC and brings the real-cell closure nearer the truth than v1, and the paired real refit tied minus free is NEGLIGIBLE or smaller than that gain: the tie is exact for the mocks, an assumption for real data, and for the pn an extrapolation of the MOS spectrum of Leccardi & Molendi (2008). The sky is not tied: the X-COP imaging measures it per image in the regions beyond 2 R500, as the production fit’s free level per image does.

Value: massfit.sp_band_tie unset (a free soft-proton level per image); 'tied' decided by v1_sptie under docs/dev/PREREGISTRATION.md, section 4

Used by: massfit.sp_band_tie, massfit.fit_hydrostatic

Evidence: ghirardini2019 p. 7 (Sect. 2.4.2 (residual soft protons: a broken power law of fixed shape, slopes 0.4 and 0.8, break 5 keV, normalisation free)); ghirardini2019 p. 4 (Sect. 2.2.4 (the sky level of each X-COP image from the regions beyond 2 R500))

Code: src/tngxmm/massfit/esas_background.py, src/tngxmm/massfit/fit_hydrostatic.py

fit.qpb_level_rule¶

Kind: validation

Whether the QPB level is fixed by the corners or free in production is decided by the closure arm v1_qpbfree under the arm rule, written before B1: NEGLIGIBLE, free on both sides; SYSTEMATIC or INCONCLUSIVE, fixed as XMM-ESAS and the X-COP imaging fix it, with the level arms sized from the real refits made with the level free, in both directions: per image 1 + (q84 - m) and 1 - (m - q16), q16 and q84 of the fitted over corner level on the real targets, m the median of the same ratio on the v1_qpbfree cells. Until those fits exist the arms keep the FWC lower bound.

Value: QPB_LEVEL_RULE: free if v1_qpbfree NEGLIGIBLE, else fixed with qpb_level_error_from_fits (16-84 per cent, mock median)

Used by: massfit.qpb_model, validation.analyse_closure_grid

Evidence: ghirardini2019 p. 6 (Sect. 2.4.2 (X-COP spectral fits: the FWC normalisation free within 10 per cent to account for scaling systematics))

Validation: – FWC level error d (lower bound) 0.010-0.080 per camera and band (QPB_LEVEL_ERROR); the real free-level refits are 6-8 node-hours

Code: src/tngxmm/validation/closure_grid.py

fit.science_sample_seed_flags¶

Kind: validation

A fit whose first guess is limited by the field (mass_seed_source field-edge) or that started from the relation’s pivot (no first guess, or a failed seed step) carries the QC flag field_edge or no_seed, both in QC_EXCLUDING: such fits leave the headline statistics of mocks and real data by one rule, read from each fit’s own record, and stay in the tables and in training. The closure grid applies the same flags. The mock and real field-edge fractions are reported side by side (certification rule Rqc_seed_chain).

Value: QC_EXCLUDING += field_edge, no_seed

Used by: massfit.fit_hydrostatic, validation.analyse_closure_grid, certify.apply_certification_rules

Validation: – 21 real field-edge targets (17 PSZ2, 4 ACT; 19 at z <= 0.077) and 1 without a source list (0720700101)

Code: src/tngxmm/massfit/fit_qc.py

method.first_guess_arms¶

Kind: literature

The closure arms v1sas_seedhi and v1sas_seedlo (STATUS B4, sensitivity test S6) scale the first-guess mass of a cell by exp(+-0.27 / 1.89), one standard deviation of the scatter of the Bulbul et al. (2019) relation the first guess iterates on, at fixed luminosity; the centre stays, the crop, pixel and start follow. A pair under the arm rule.

Value: seed_factor exp(+-0.1429)

Used by: validation.analyse_closure_grid, validation.fit_closure_views

Evidence: bulbul2019 p. 22 (Sect. 7 (the core-included soft-band L_X-M500 relation: mass slope 1.89, intrinsic scatter 0.27))

Code: src/tngxmm/validation/closure_grid.py

donors.fit_mass_pairing_rule¶

Kind: validation

The pairing on the fitted hydrostatic mass starts from pass 1 (one view of every candidate halo-snapshot under one reference observation, chosen by the smallest summed distance of its percentile ranks from the pool medians in exposure per camera, HI4PI column, N_H,tot / N_HI and particle background, among the full-frame observations) and stops when fewer than 5 per cent of the paired halo-snapshots change a physics donor between two iterations of pass 2, or after three; a streamed simulation passes its edge test when at most 5 per cent of its pairs lie in either outer tenth of its mass window. No published iterative matching gives these numbers; they are the design note’s.

Value: CONVERGENCE_MAX_CHANGED_FRAC 0.05, CONVERGENCE_MAX_ITERATIONS 3, EDGE_TENTH 0.1, EDGE_MAX_SHARE 0.05, pass1_reference

Used by: params:donors.matched.dz_max

Validation: Design note: matching on the hydrostatic fit mass, in two passes – pass 1: 3,258 views and fits, about 44 node-hours; pass 2: 128 node-hours with six views (44 with one) for the first iteration

Code: src/tngxmm/donors/pair_projections.py
